#### D. **Dynamic Noise Tile Backgrounds**

Runtime-generated procedural noise textures for UI chrome:

**`/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/ui/chromeTiles.ts` (lines 35-44):**
```typescript
export function installUiTiles(): void {
  if (installed || typeof document === "undefined") return;
  const dirt = noiseTileUrl(BLOCK_COLORS[BlockId.Dirt] ?? [0.46, 0.33, 0.2], 3, 0.55);
  const stone = noiseTileUrl(BLOCK_COLORS[BlockId.Stone] ?? [0.54, 0.56, 0.58], 7, 0.9);
  if (!dirt || !stone) return;
  document.documentElement.style.setProperty("--mc-tile-dirt", `url("${dirt}")`);
  document.documentElement.style.setProperty("--mc-tile-stone", `url("${stone}")`);
  installed = true;
}
```

These tiles are called from `MinecraftGame.tsx` (line 59) and used in pause menu backgrounds.

components/

    ├── Hotbar.test.tsx, etc.         # Component tests

app/ (Next.js styles)

├── ui.css                            # Panels, dialogs, buttons, overlays

lib/ui/ (Sprite generation)

### 2. MAJOR UI COMPONENTS - DETAILED BREAKDOWN

#### **StatusBars** (`/components/game/StatusBars.tsx`, lines 51-63)
- **Renders:** Two rows of icon-based bars above the hotbar
  - Left side: Armor (when equipped) + Health (hearts)
  - Right side: Hunger (drumsticks, reversed direction)
- **Styling:** Grid layout with gap, flex rows, `image-rendering: pixelated`
- **Data source:** `hearts`, `maxHearts`, `hunger`, `maxHunger`, `armorPoints` props
- **Icons:** Procedurally generated 16x16 pixels (full/half/container states)

#### **Hotbar** (`/components/game/Hotbar.tsx`, lines 17-44)
- **Renders:** 9 dark translucent slots with white selection outline
- **Layout:** Flexbox row with padding/border, translucent background
- **Active state:** 3px white border (inset -5px) + outline on selected slot
- **Animation:** Selected item name fades in/out above the bar (2.2s ease-out)
- **Interaction:** Click to select, displays item label tooltip
- **Size:** 46px × 46px slots (responsive: 38px on mobile)

#### **InventoryPanel** (`/components/game/InventoryPanel.tsx`, lines 37-163)
- **Renders:** Centered modal dialog with:
  - Armor column (4 slots: helmet, chestplate, legs, boots)
  - 27-slot main storage grid (3 rows × 9 columns)
  - Hotbar row (9 slots, matching the bottom HUD)
  - Recipe book sidebar (scrollable list of craftable recipes)
- **Grid setup:** 40px × 40px inv-slots (32px × 32px on mobile), 9 cols
- **Selection:** Click-to-swap mechanic with visual pending state (blue-tinted)
- **Equipped armor:** Green background highlight; ghost images when empty
- **Recipe book:** Shows ingredient icons + arrow → result icon
- **Max height:** 92vh with overflow-y auto for tall screens

#### **PauseMenu** (`/components/game/PauseMenu.tsx`, lines 36-127)
- **Renders:** Dimmed overlay (55% black) with centered gray panel
- **Content:**
  - "Game Menu" title (22px, bold, white text with shadow)
  - Buttons: Back to Game, Save, Load, Reset World (confirm warning)
  - Audio sliders (Master, Music) with range input
  - Mute toggle button
  - Appearance section: 3×1 grid of skin swatches
  - Controls legend (readonly list of keybinds)
  - Dirt-textured background panel (optional texture)
- **Button style:** Classic beveled gray (6f6f6f) with light/dark borders, hover changes to blue-gray
- **Skin swatches:** Small portrait images, selected has gold border

#### **DeathScreen** (`/components/game/DeathScreen.tsx`, lines 7-20)
- **Renders:** Red-tinted overlay (124, 10, 10, 50% alpha) with centered content
  - "You Died!" title (44px, bold, white with shadow)
  - "Respawning in N…" subtitle
  - Respawn button
- **Layout:** Grid with 14px gap, centered items

#### **SleepOverlay** (`/components/game/SleepOverlay.tsx`, lines 10-12)
- **Renders:** Pure black fade overlay (opacity 0 or 1)
- **Animation:** CSS transition 1.3s ease-in-out
- **Pointer events:** Disabled when transparent
- **Always mounted:** Driven by the `sleeping` boolean prop

#### **DebugOverlay** (`/components/game/DebugOverlay.tsx`, lines 16-54)
- **Renders:** F3-style readout, top-left corner
  - "Monecraft (F3 to close)"
  - FPS counter (updated at 500ms intervals via rAF loop)
  - Player position (XYZ: float with 1 decimal place)
  - Daylight percentage
  - Mob counts (passive/hostile)
- **Styling:** Dark semi-transparent background, small monospace text, light gray
- **Pointer events:** None (read-only overlay)

#### **ItemIcon** (`/components/game/ItemIcon.tsx`, lines 15-36)
- **Renders:** Item sprite with optional overlays
  - Item icon (16x16 pixels, scaled to size prop)
  - Item count (top-right, Minecraft-style)
  - Durability bar (bottom, green→red hue, 0–120° hue range)
- **Empty slots:** Renders nothing (slot background shows through)
- **Durability:** Width = `durabilityRatio * 100%`, hue based on condition

#### **PixelImg** (`/components/game/PixelImg.tsx`, lines 31-45)
- **Renders:** `<img>` with `image-rendering: pixelated`
- **Data URLs:** All sprites are generated pixel buffers → canvas → data-URL
- **Hydration:** Uses `useSyncExternalStore` to detect browser side + render real sprite (SSR = transparent pixel)
- **Non-draggable:** `draggable={false}`

| **Sleep fade duration** | 1.3s ease-in-out | ui.css:404 |
| **Pause overlay opacity** | 55% black (rgba(0,0,0,0.55)) | ui.css:10 |
| **Death overlay opacity** | 50% red (rgba(124,10,10,0.5)) | ui.css:364 |

---

### 5. Z-INDEX LAYERING STRATEGY

**Canvas base:** `0` (Three.js fills the `.game-canvas-wrap`)

| z-index | Component/Element | Purpose |
|---------|------------------|---------|
| **1** | `.pause-controls` | Inside pause menu, cosmetic depth |
| **3** | `.click-hint` | Center-screen "Click to play" prompt |
| **4** | `.hud-bottom` + `.minimap` | Bottom HUD + top-right minimap |
| **5** | `.debug-overlay` + `.hud-toast` | F3 readout and item pickup toasts |
| **6** | `.inventory-panel` | Main inventory dialog |
| **8** | `.death-overlay` | Death screen |
| **9** | `.pause-overlay` + `.sleep-overlay` | Fullscreen overlays (darkest/topmost) |

**Stacking order (top to bottom):**
1. Sleep overlay (fade-to-black)
2. Pause overlay (game menu)
3. Death overlay (red tint)
4. Inventory panel (modal)
5. Debug + toast (HUD notifications)
6. Hotbar + status bars (permanent HUD)
7. Click hint (center screen)
8. Three.js canvas (3D world)

---

### 6. INTEGRATION WITH THREE.JS RENDERER

**Architecture:** React sits in front of a fullscreen Three.js canvas.

**Mount structure** (MinecraftGame.tsx:77-90):
```typescript
<div className="game-root">
  {/* Three.js canvas injected here by ref */}
  <div ref={attachMount} className="game-canvas-wrap" />
  
  {/* React overlays rendered in front */}
  <div className="hud-bottom">...</div>
  <div className="minimap" ref={attachMinimap} />
  {inventoryOpen ? <InventoryPanel ... /> : null}
  {paused ? <PauseMenu ... /> : null}
  {/* ... etc */}
</div>
```

**Canvas lifecycle** (useMinecraftGame.ts):
- Canvas is created in `GameRenderer` constructor
- Attached to `.game-canvas-wrap` via callback ref
- GameEngine pumps Three.js rendering on rAF loop
- Minimap canvas rendered separately (Canvas API, not Three.js)

**Pointer lock:** Click on canvas locks pointer; Escape or pause unlocks.

**Event handling:** Input controller listens to canvas for mouse/keyboard events.

---

### 7. RESPONSIVE DESIGN

**Mobile breakpoint:** `@media (max-width: 720px)` (hud.css:411, ui.css:467)

**Mobile adjustments:**
- Hotbar slots: 46px → 38px
- Inventory slots: 40px → 32px
- Inventory columns: flex-direction → column (armor above grid)
- Recipe book: border-left → border-top
- Recipe list max-height: 360px → 180px
- Minimap: forced 96px × 96px

---

### 8. ANIMATION & TRANSITIONS

| Animation | Duration | Easing | Element |
|-----------|----------|--------|---------|
| Hotbar item name fade-in | 2.2s | ease-out | `.hotbar-item-name` |
| Click hint pulse | 2.4s infinite | ease-in-out | `.click-hint` |
| Sleep fade | 1.3s | ease-in-out | `.sleep-overlay` |

### 9. ACCESSIBILITY

- **ARIA labels** on all buttons (slot numbers, item names)
- **ARIA roles** on status bars (`role="meter"`)
- **Semantic `<button>` elements** throughout
- **Alt text** on images (PixelImg provides alt prop)
- **Focus-visible styles** on buttons (matches hover)
- **aria-pressed** on toggle buttons (skin swatches, mute)

---

### 10. SUMMARY OF VISUAL APPROACH

**Style:** Minecraft-accurate retro UI with:
- Monospace font (Lucida Console)
- Beveled button borders (light top-left, dark bottom-right) for 3D effect
- Pixelated image rendering throughout
- Procedurally generated icons (hearts, armor, items, skins)
- Procedurally generated noise textures (dirt, stone)
- Grid-based layout (hotbar 9 cols, inventory 9 cols)
- Dark translucent panels with outlines
- Minimal color palette (grays, reds for death, greens for health)
- Text shadows for legibility on varied backgrounds

**Technology:** Pure vanilla CSS + procedural sprite generation (Canvas API + data URLs)

---

This UI layer is elegant, fully procedural (no image files), and stays true to Minecraft's classic aesthetic while being fully responsive and accessible!

### 2. BLOCK TEXTURE GENERATION (WORLD ATLAS)

**File:** `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/world/atlas.ts`

**Key Constants:**
- `ATLAS_TILE_SIZE = 16` (16x16 pixels per block face)
- `ATLAS_FACE_VARIANTS = 3` (top, side, bottom)
- Dynamic atlas sizing based on block count

**Representative Example Function:**
```typescript
export function createBlockAtlasTexture(): THREE.CanvasTexture {
  if (atlasTextureCache) return atlasTextureCache;

  const width = ATLAS_COLUMNS * ATLAS_TILE_SIZE;
  const height = ATLAS_ROWS * ATLAS_TILE_SIZE;
  const canvas = document.createElement("canvas");
  canvas.width = width;
  canvas.height = height;
  const ctx = canvas.getContext("2d");
  if (!ctx) throw new Error("Failed to create atlas context");
  ctx.imageSmoothingEnabled = false;

  const drawTile = (block: number, face: "top" | "side" | "bottom") => {
    const tile = tileIndexFor(block, face);
    const col = tile % ATLAS_COLUMNS;
    const row = Math.floor(tile / ATLAS_COLUMNS);
    const ox = col * ATLAS_TILE_SIZE;
    const oy = row * ATLAS_TILE_SIZE;

    const baseBlockColor = BLOCK_COLORS[block] ?? [1, 0, 1];
    let base = baseBlockColor;
    if (face === "top") base = tone(base, 1.08);
    if (face === "bottom") base = tone(base, 0.96);
    if (block === BlockId.Grass && face === "bottom") base = BLOCK_COLORS[BlockId.Dirt];

    for (let y = 0; y < ATLAS_TILE_SIZE; y += 1) {
      for (let x = 0; x < ATLAS_TILE_SIZE; x += 1) {
        const h = Math.sin((x + block * 13 + (face === "top" ? 7 : face === "side" ? 17 : 29)) * 12.1 + (y + block * 19) * 7.7) * 43758.5453;
        const n = h - Math.floor(h);
        let c = tone(base, 0.92 + n * 0.22);

        // Special handling for specific blocks (Grass, Stone, Ore, Water, Sand, etc.)
        if (block === BlockId.Grass && face === "side" && y < 4) c = tone(BLOCK_COLORS[BlockId.Grass], 0.95 + n * 0.15);
        if ((block === BlockId.Stone || block === BlockId.Cobblestone || block === BlockId.Bedrock) && n > 0.8) c = tone(base, 1.18);
        if ((block === BlockId.Wood || block === BlockId.Planks) && (x + y) % 4 === 0) c = tone(base, 0.82);
        // ... ore accent colors, water special handling, etc.

        ctx.fillStyle = rgb(c);
        ctx.fillRect(ox + x, oy + y, 1, 1);
      }
    }
  };

  for (let block = BlockId.Grass; block < ATLAS_BLOCK_COUNT; block += 1) {
    drawTile(block, "top");
    drawTile(block, "side");
    drawTile(block, "bottom");
  }

  const texture = new THREE.CanvasTexture(canvas);
  texture.magFilter = THREE.NearestFilter;
  texture.minFilter = THREE.NearestMipmapNearestFilter;
  texture.wrapS = THREE.ClampToEdgeWrapping;
  texture.wrapT = THREE.ClampToEdgeWrapping;
  texture.flipY = false;
  texture.needsUpdate = true;
  atlasTextureCache = texture;
  return texture;
}
```

**Techniques:**
- **Per-pixel noise hash:** `Math.sin(x * 12.1 + y * 7.7) * 43758.5453` for deterministic variation
- **Face shading:** Top faces brighten (+1.08), bottom darken (-0.96)
- **Block-specific patterns:** Grass has dirt bottom & grass lip; ores get accent sparkles; stone gets bright spots for depth
- **Color toning:** `tone(base, mul, add)` applies multiplicative & additive adjustments with clamping

**Block Color Palette (float RGB):**
Lines 71-99 define BLOCK_COLORS record with normalized RGB values for all block types (Grass, Dirt, Stone, Wood, etc.).

### 3. ITEM/ICON SPRITE GENERATION (INVENTORY)

**File:** `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/ui/spritePixels.ts`

**Resolution:** 16x16 pixels (defined as `SPRITE_SIZE = 16`)

**Representative Functions:**

#### A. `paintGrid()` - Core shape-to-pixels converter (lines 18-35):
```typescript
export function paintGrid(grid: string[], palette: PixelPalette): Uint8ClampedArray {
  const out = new Uint8ClampedArray(SPRITE_SIZE * SPRITE_SIZE * 4);
  for (let y = 0; y < SPRITE_SIZE; y += 1) {
    const row = grid[y] ?? "";
    for (let x = 0; x < SPRITE_SIZE; x += 1) {
      const ch = row[x] ?? ".";
      if (ch === "." || ch === " ") continue;
      const rgb = palette[ch];
      if (!rgb) continue;
      const i = (y * SPRITE_SIZE + x) * 4;
      out[i] = rgb[0];
      out[i + 1] = rgb[1];
      out[i + 2] = rgb[2];
      out[i + 3] = 255;
    }
  }
  return out;
}
```

#### B. `paintIsoBlock()` - Shaded isometric cube (lines 544-589):
```typescript
export function paintIsoBlock(blockId: BlockId): Uint8ClampedArray {
  const out = new Uint8ClampedArray(SPRITE_SIZE * SPRITE_SIZE * 4);
  const accent = ORE_ACCENTS[blockId];
  const topColor = blockRgb(blockId);
  const sideColor = blockId === BlockId.Grass ? blockRgb(BlockId.Dirt) : topColor;

  for (let x = 0; x < SPRITE_SIZE; x += 1) {
    const px = x + 0.5;
    const onLeft = px <= 8;
    const dx = onLeft ? (px - 0.5) / 7.5 : (15.5 - px) / 7.5;
    const topY = 4 - dx * 3.5;
    const midY = 4 + dx * 3.5;
    const botY = midY + 8;

    for (let y = 0; y < SPRITE_SIZE; y += 1) {
      const py = y + 0.5;
      if (py < topY || py >= botY) continue;
      const onTop = py < midY;

      let rgb: Rgb;
      let shade: number;
      if (onTop) {
        rgb = topColor;
        shade = 1.18;
      } else if (blockId === BlockId.Grass && py < midY + 1.6) {
        rgb = topColor;
        shade = onLeft ? 0.86 : 0.62;
      } else {
        rgb = sideColor;
        shade = onLeft ? 0.82 : 0.56;
      }

      const noise = 0.92 + pixelHash(x, y, blockId) * 0.16;
      if (accent && !onTop && pixelHash(x * 3 + 1, y * 3 + 2, blockId) > 0.82) {
        rgb = accent;
      }
      const i = (y * SPRITE_SIZE + x) * 4;
      out[i] = clampByte(rgb[0] * shade * noise);
      out[i + 1] = clampByte(rgb[1] * shade * noise);
      out[i + 2] = clampByte(rgb[2] * shade * noise);
      out[i + 3] = 255;
    }
  }
  return out;
}
```

**Shape Grids (Examples):**
- **PICKAXE_GRID** (lines 66-83): 16-row string where `m`=main, `M`=dark, `l`=light, `h`/`H`=handle
- **SWORD_GRID** (lines 85-102): Vertical blade with handle
- **KNIFE_GRID** (lines 108-125): Single-edged blade, narrower than sword
- **HOE_GRID** (lines 127-144): Wide flat blade
- **Food/Armor/Craft grids:** Similar structure for each item type

**Material Palettes (lines 47-55):**
```typescript

  wood: { m: [158, 110, 57], M: [104, 72, 37], l: [199, 154, 98] },
  stone: { m: [130, 134, 138], M: [84, 88, 92], l: [171, 175, 179] },
  sliver: { m: [200, 205, 214], M: [138, 144, 155], l: [238, 241, 247] },
  ruby: { m: [214, 72, 84], M: [140, 38, 50], l: [243, 134, 144] },
  sapphire: { m: [62, 128, 222], M: [34, 80, 156], l: [130, 180, 245] },
  gold: { m: [240, 190, 60], M: [180, 128, 32], l: [252, 228, 130] },

};

**Main Render Function (lines 597-612):**
```typescript
export function renderSpritePixels(itemId: string): Uint8ClampedArray {
  const def = ITEM_DEF_BY_ID[itemId];
  const custom = ITEM_SPRITE_GRIDS[itemId];
  if (custom) return paintGrid(custom.grid, custom.palette);
  if (def?.kind === "block" && def.blockId !== undefined) return paintIsoBlock(def.blockId);
  if (def?.kind === "tool" && itemId.endsWith("_hoe")) return paintGrid(HOE_GRID, toolPalette(materialFor(itemId)));
  if (def?.kind === "tool") return paintGrid(PICKAXE_GRID, toolPalette(materialFor(itemId)));
  if (def?.kind === "weapon") {
    if (itemId === "knife") return paintGrid(KNIFE_GRID, toolPalette(STEEL));
    return paintGrid(SWORD_GRID, toolPalette(materialFor(itemId)));
  }
  if (def?.kind === "armor" && def.armorSlot) {
    return paintGrid(ARMOR_GRIDS[def.armorSlot] ?? UNKNOWN_GRID, { m: STEEL.m, M: STEEL.M, l: STEEL.l });
  }
  return paintGrid(UNKNOWN_GRID, { p: [240, 40, 240], P: [40, 8, 40] });
}
```

**Half-Icon Blending (lines 92-105):**
```typescript
function halfIcon(full: Uint8ClampedArray, container: Uint8ClampedArray): Uint8ClampedArray {
  const out = new Uint8ClampedArray(container);
  for (let y = 0; y < SPRITE_SIZE; y += 1) {
    for (let x = 0; x < SPRITE_SIZE / 2; x += 1) {
      const i = (y * SPRITE_SIZE + x) * 4;
      if (full[i + 3] === 0) continue;
      out[i] = full[i];
      out[i + 1] = full[i + 1];
      out[i + 2] = full[i + 2];
      out[i + 3] = full[i + 3];
    }
  }
  return out;
}
```
This blends the left half of the full icon over the container background.

**Noise-Generated UI Chrome (chromeTiles.ts):**

**File:** `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/ui/chromeTiles.ts` (lines 9-44)

```typescript
function noiseTileUrl(base: [number, number, number], salt: number, dimming: number): string {
  if (typeof document === "undefined") return "";
  const canvas = document.createElement("canvas");
  canvas.width = SPRITE_SIZE;
  canvas.height = SPRITE_SIZE;
  const ctx = canvas.getContext("2d");
  if (!ctx) return "";

  const image = ctx.createImageData(SPRITE_SIZE, SPRITE_SIZE);
  for (let y = 0; y < SPRITE_SIZE; y += 1) {
    for (let x = 0; x < SPRITE_SIZE; x += 1) {
      const v = Math.sin(x * 12.9898 + y * 78.233 + salt * 37.719) * 43758.5453;
      const noise = 0.75 + (v - Math.floor(v)) * 0.5;
      const i = (y * SPRITE_SIZE + x) * 4;
      image.data[i] = Math.round(base[0] * 255 * noise * dimming);
      image.data[i + 1] = Math.round(base[1] * 255 * noise * dimming);
      image.data[i + 2] = Math.round(base[2] * 255 * noise * dimming);
      image.data[i + 3] = 255;
    }
  }
  ctx.putImageData(image, 0, 0);
  return canvas.toDataURL();
}

export function installUiTiles(): void {
  if (installed || typeof document === "undefined") return;
  const dirt = noiseTileUrl(BLOCK_COLORS[BlockId.Dirt] ?? [0.46, 0.33, 0.2], 3, 0.55);
  const stone = noiseTileUrl(BLOCK_COLORS[BlockId.Stone] ?? [0.54, 0.56, 0.58], 7, 0.9);
  if (!dirt || !stone) return;
  document.documentElement.style.setProperty("--mc-tile-dirt", `url("${dirt}")`);
  document.documentElement.style.setProperty("--mc-tile-stone", `url("${stone}")`);
  installed = true;
}
```

**Usage in CSS (hud.css line 187):**
```css
.pause-controls {
  margin-top: 12px;
  padding: 10px 12px;
  background: var(--mc-tile-dirt), rgba(20, 14, 8, 0.9);
  outline: 2px solid rgba(0, 0, 0, 0.85);
  display: grid;
  gap: 3px;
}
```

These noise tiles are used as tileable background patterns for UI panels (pause menu controls section).

### 8. AUTHENTICATION & REUSE FOR MINECRAFT UI TEXTURES

To generate Minecraft-authentic UI textures (stone panels, beveled buttons, slot backgrounds), you can:

**1. Stone GUI Panel (classic Minecraft look):**
Use `noiseTileUrl()` pattern from chromeTiles.ts:
- Base color: Stone float RGB `[0.54, 0.56, 0.58]`
- Salt parameter: varies the noise pattern
- Dimming: 0.9 for brightness preservation
- Result: Tiles seamlessly as a background texture

**2. Beveled Button/Slot (3D depth):**
Use palette-based grid + `paintGrid()`:
```typescript
const BUTTON_GRID = [
  "................",
  ".bbbbbbbbbbbbb..",
  ".bllllllllllbb..",
  ".blDDDDDDDDlbb..",
  ".blDDDDDDDDlbb..",
  ".blDDDDDDDDlbb..",
  ".blDDDDDDDDlbb..",
  ".blDDDDDDDDlbb..",
  ".bllllllllllbb..",
  ".bbbbbbbbbbbbb..",
  "................",
  "................",
  "................",
  "................",
  "................",
  "................"
];

const BUTTON_PALETTE = {
  b: [200, 200, 200],  // border/shadow
  l: [255, 255, 255],  // light bevel
  D: [150, 150, 150]   // dark recessed area
};

paintGrid(BUTTON_GRID, BUTTON_PALETTE); // → 16×16 RGBA
```

**3. Slot Well Background:**
- Top-left light bevel: `[220, 220, 220]`
- Bottom-right dark bevel: `[80, 80, 80]`
- Interior recessed: `[140, 140, 140]`
- Apply same grid+palette approach or use CSS border tricks (current codebase does CSS)

**4. Durability/Status Bar Colors:**
See ItemIcon.tsx (line 20): `hsl(${durabilityRatio * 120}, 90%, 45%)` — greens to reds on HSL scale

**5. Ore Accent Sparkles:**
Reference ORE_ACCENTS (spritePixels.ts line 526-532):
```typescript
const ORE_ACCENTS: Partial<Record<BlockId, Rgb>> = {
  [BlockId.SliverOre]: [222, 226, 233],
  [BlockId.RubyOre]: [220, 68, 84],
  [BlockId.GoldOre]: [244, 196, 72],
  [BlockId.SapphireOre]: [70, 140, 230],
  [BlockId.DiamondOre]: [140, 235, 244]
};
```
These are applied during paintIsoBlock via `pixelHash()` thresholds.

---

### SUMMARY

This codebase achieves **zero binary assets** through:
1. **Block textures:** Procedural noise + per-face shading in canvas at runtime
2. **Item sprites:** Hand-authored string grids + material palettes → `paintGrid()`
3. **HUD icons:** Same grid system with container/full/half variants
4. **UI chrome:** Seeded noise tiles (dirt, stone) installed as CSS variables
5. **3D integration:** Extrudes pixel sprites to voxel geometry for first-person view

All pixel generation is **pure**, **deterministic**, and **testable** (runs under bun test without DOM), with canvas/data-URL conversion deferred to the DOM layer.
```

1	import { BLOCK_COLORS, BlockId } from "@/lib/world";
2	import { SPRITE_SIZE } from "./spritePixels";
3	
4	/**
5	 * Tiled noise backgrounds for UI chrome (the pause-menu dirt backdrop and the
6	 * panel stone texture), generated once and installed as CSS custom properties.
7	 */
8	
9	function noiseTileUrl(base: [number, number, number], salt: number, dimming: number): string {
10	  if (typeof document === "undefined") return "";
11	  const canvas = document.createElement("canvas");
12	  canvas.width = SPRITE_SIZE;
13	  canvas.height = SPRITE_SIZE;
14	  const ctx = canvas.getContext("2d");
15	  if (!ctx) return "";
16	
17	  const image = ctx.createImageData(SPRITE_SIZE, SPRITE_SIZE);
18	  for (let y = 0; y < SPRITE_SIZE; y += 1) {
19	    for (let x = 0; x < SPRITE_SIZE; x += 1) {
20	      const v = Math.sin(x * 12.9898 + y * 78.233 + salt * 37.719) * 43758.5453;
21	      const noise = 0.75 + (v - Math.floor(v)) * 0.5;
22	      const i = (y * SPRITE_SIZE + x) * 4;
23	      image.data[i] = Math.round(base[0] * 255 * noise * dimming);
24	      image.data[i + 1] = Math.round(base[1] * 255 * noise * dimming);
25	      image.data[i + 2] = Math.round(base[2] * 255 * noise * dimming);
26	      image.data[i + 3] = 255;
27	    }
28	  }
29	  ctx.putImageData(image, 0, 0);
30	  return canvas.toDataURL();
31	}
32	
33	let installed = false;
34	
35	/** Sets --mc-tile-dirt / --mc-tile-stone on the document root (idempotent). */
36	export function installUiTiles(): void {
37	  if (installed || typeof document === "undefined") return;
38	  const dirt = noiseTileUrl(BLOCK_COLORS[BlockId.Dirt] ?? [0.46, 0.33, 0.2], 3, 0.55);
39	  const stone = noiseTileUrl(BLOCK_COLORS[BlockId.Stone] ?? [0.54, 0.56, 0.58], 7, 0.9);
40	  if (!dirt || !stone) return;
41	  document.documentElement.style.setProperty("--mc-tile-dirt", `url("${dirt}")`);
42	  document.documentElement.style.setProperty("--mc-tile-stone", `url("${stone}")`);
43	  installed = true;
44	}
45

1	import { useState } from "react";
2	import ItemIcon from "@/components/game/ItemIcon";
3	import PixelImg from "@/components/game/PixelImg";
4	import { ARMOR_SLOT_LABELS, ARMOR_SLOTS, createSlot } from "@/lib/game/items";
5	import { itemIconUrl } from "@/lib/ui/sprites";
6	import type { EquippedArmor, InventorySlot, Recipe } from "@/lib/game/types";
7	
8	type InventoryPanelProps = {
9	  inventory: InventorySlot[];
10	  equippedArmor: EquippedArmor;
11	  selectedHotbarSlot: number;
12	  hotbarSlots: number;
13	  recipes: Recipe[];
14	  craftingStation: "furnace" | null;
15	  canCraft: (recipe: Recipe) => boolean;
16	  onSwapSlots: (fromIndex: number, toIndex: number) => void;
17	  onToggleEquipArmor: (index: number) => void;
18	  onCraft: (recipe: Recipe) => void;
19	};
20	
21	const STATION_LABELS: Record<NonNullable<Recipe["station"]>, string> = {
22	  furnace: "Furnace"
23	};
24	
25	function slotTitle(slot: InventorySlot): string | undefined {
26	  if (!slot.id || slot.count <= 0) return undefined;
27	  if (slot.maxDurability) return `${slot.label} (${slot.durability ?? slot.maxDurability}/${slot.maxDurability})`;
28	  return slot.label;
29	}
30	
31	/**
32	 * The survival inventory: armor column, 27-slot storage grid, hotbar row, and
33	 * a recipe book where every entry shows ingredient icons and the result.
34	 * Items move by clicking one slot and then another (click-to-swap); clicking
35	 * an armor item toggles equipping it instead.
36	 */
37	export default function InventoryPanel({
38	  inventory,
39	  equippedArmor,
40	  selectedHotbarSlot,
41	  hotbarSlots,
42	  recipes,
43	  craftingStation,
44	  canCraft,
45	  onSwapSlots,
46	  onToggleEquipArmor,
47	  onCraft
48	}: InventoryPanelProps) {
49	  const [pendingIndex, setPendingIndex] = useState<number | null>(null);
50	
51	  const onSlotClick = (index: number) => {
52	    const slot = inventory[index];
53	    if (slot.kind === "armor" && slot.count > 0) {
54	      onToggleEquipArmor(index);
55	      setPendingIndex(null);
56	      return;
57	    }
58	
59	    if (pendingIndex === null) {
60	      setPendingIndex(index);
61	      return;
62	    }
63	    if (pendingIndex === index) {
64	      setPendingIndex(null);
65	      return;
66	    }
67	    onSwapSlots(pendingIndex, index);
68	    setPendingIndex(null);
69	  };
70	
71	  const isEquipped = (slot: InventorySlot) => slot.kind === "armor" && !!slot.id && equippedArmor[slot.armorSlot ?? "helmet"] === slot.id;
72	
73	  const renderSlot = (slot: InventorySlot, idx: number, extraClass = "") => (
74	    <button
75	      key={`inv-slot-${idx}`}
76	      className={["inv-slot", extraClass, pendingIndex === idx ? "pending" : "", isEquipped(slot) ? "equipped" : ""].filter(Boolean).join(" ")}
77	      onClick={() => onSlotClick(idx)}
78	      title={slotTitle(slot)}
79	      aria-label={slot.id && slot.count > 0 ? `Slot ${idx + 1}: ${slot.label}` : `Slot ${idx + 1}: empty`}
80	    >
81	      <ItemIcon slot={slot} size={32} />
82	    </button>
83	  );
84	
85	  const hotbar = inventory.slice(0, hotbarSlots);
86	  const storage = inventory.slice(hotbarSlots);
87	
88	  return (
89	    <div className="inventory-panel">
90	      <div className="inventory-columns">
91	        <div className="inventory-main">
92	          <div className="inventory-heading">Inventory</div>
93	          <div className="inventory-upper">
94	            <div className="armor-column">
95	              {ARMOR_SLOTS.map((armorSlot) => {
96	                const equippedId = equippedArmor[armorSlot];
97	                const equippedIndex = equippedId ? inventory.findIndex((slot) => slot.id === equippedId && slot.count > 0) : -1;
98	                const equippedItem = equippedIndex >= 0 ? inventory[equippedIndex] : undefined;
99	                return (
100	                  <button
101	                    key={`armor-${armorSlot}`}
102	                    className={equippedItem ? "inv-slot armor-slot filled" : "inv-slot armor-slot"}
103	                    onClick={() => equippedIndex >= 0 && onToggleEquipArmor(equippedIndex)}
104	                    title={equippedItem ? slotTitle(equippedItem) : `${ARMOR_SLOT_LABELS[armorSlot]} (empty)`}
105	                    aria-label={equippedItem ? `${ARMOR_SLOT_LABELS[armorSlot]}: ${equippedItem.label}` : `${ARMOR_SLOT_LABELS[armorSlot]}: empty`}
106	                  >
107	                    {equippedItem ? (
108	                      <ItemIcon slot={equippedItem} size={32} />
109	                    ) : (
110	                      <span className="armor-ghost">
111	                        <PixelImg src={itemIconUrl(armorSlot)} alt="" size={32} aria-hidden />
112	                      </span>
113	                    )}
114	                  </button>
115	                );
116	              })}
117	            </div>
118	            <div className="inventory-hint">
119	              Click one slot, then another to move items.
120	              <br />
121	              Click armor to equip or unequip it.
122	            </div>
123	          </div>
124	          <div className="inv-grid storage" data-testid="storage-grid">
125	            {storage.map((slot, offset) => renderSlot(slot, offset + hotbarSlots))}
126	          </div>
127	          <div className="inv-grid hotbar-row" data-testid="hotbar-grid">
128	            {hotbar.map((slot, idx) => renderSlot(slot, idx, idx === selectedHotbarSlot ? "active" : ""))}
129	          </div>
130	        </div>
131	
132	        <div className="recipe-book">
133	          <div className="inventory-heading">Crafting</div>
134	          <div className="recipe-list">
135	            {recipes.map((recipe) => {
136	              const stationLocked = !!recipe.station && recipe.station !== craftingStation;
137	              return (
138	                <button
139	                  key={recipe.id}
140	                  className="recipe-entry"
141	                  onClick={() => onCraft(recipe)}
142	                  disabled={stationLocked || !canCraft(recipe)}
143	                  aria-label={recipe.label}
144	                  title={stationLocked ? `Requires ${STATION_LABELS[recipe.station!]}` : recipe.label}
145	                >
146	                  <span className="recipe-ingredients">
147	                    {recipe.cost.map((cost) => (
148	                      <ItemIcon key={`${recipe.id}-${cost.slotId}`} slot={createSlot(cost.slotId, cost.count)} size={24} />
149	                    ))}
150	                  </span>
151	                  <span className="recipe-arrow" aria-hidden>
152	                    →
153	                  </span>
154	                  <ItemIcon slot={createSlot(recipe.result.slotId, recipe.result.count)} size={24} />
155	                </button>
156	              );
157	            })}
158	          </div>
159	        </div>
160	      </div>
161	    </div>
162	  );
163	}
164

1	import ItemIcon from "@/components/game/ItemIcon";
2	import type { InventorySlot } from "@/lib/game/types";
3	
4	type HotbarProps = {
5	  inventory: InventorySlot[];
6	  selectedSlot: number;
7	  hotbarSlots: number;
8	  onSelectSlot: (index: number) => void;
9	};
10	
11	/**
12	 * The Minecraft hotbar: 9 dark translucent slots with a white outline on the
13	 * selection and the selected item's name fading in above the bar. The name is
14	 * keyed on the selection so every change remounts it and replays the CSS
15	 * fade-in/fade-out animation — no timers or state.
16	 */
17	export default function Hotbar({ inventory, selectedSlot, hotbarSlots, onSelectSlot }: HotbarProps) {
18	  const visible = inventory.slice(0, hotbarSlots);
19	  const selected = visible[selectedSlot];
20	  const selectedLabel = selected?.id && selected.count > 0 ? selected.label : "";
21	
22	  return (
23	    <div className="hotbar-area">
24	      {selectedLabel && (
25	        <div key={`${selectedSlot}:${selectedLabel}`} className="hotbar-item-name">
26	          {selectedLabel}
27	        </div>
28	      )}
29	      <div className="hotbar" data-testid="hotbar">
30	        {visible.map((slot, idx) => (
31	          <button
32	            key={`hotbar-${idx}`}
33	            className={idx === selectedSlot ? "hotbar-slot active" : "hotbar-slot"}
34	            onClick={() => onSelectSlot(idx)}
35	            aria-label={slot.id && slot.count > 0 ? `Slot ${idx + 1}: ${slot.label}` : `Slot ${idx + 1}: empty`}
36	            title={slot.id && slot.count > 0 ? slot.label : undefined}
37	          >
38	            <ItemIcon slot={slot} size={32} />
39	          </button>
40	        ))}
41	      </div>
42	    </div>
43	  );
44	}
45

> AGENT

I now have a thorough understanding of the current UI: pure vanilla CSS (`app/base.css`, `hud.css`, `ui.css`) with a `--mc-*` token palette, CSS bevel-border tricks, procedural 16×16 sprites (`spritePixels.ts`, `hudPixels.ts`), and procedural noise tiles wired in via `chromeTiles.ts` → CSS custom properties. The biggest authenticity gaps are the font, flat (untextured) panels/buttons/slots, and the use of native browser tooltips instead of Minecraft's iconic item tooltips.

Before I design the plan, two decisions are genuinely yours to make — especially around the font, since it collides with the project's documented zero-binary-asset rule.

# Implementation Plan: Authentic Minecraft UI Polish

## File-mapping correction (verified)
The Read tool displayed the two CSS files with their labels swapped. The real contents are:
- **`app/hud.css`** = pause overlay/menu, `.mc-button`, `.mc-slider`, skin swatches, `.pause-controls`, debug overlay, click hint, `.hud-toast`, `.renderer-error`. (This is what the prompt calls "ui.css".)
- **`app/ui.css`** = bottom HUD, hotbar, item icons, crosshair, caps, inventory panel/grid/slots, recipe book, death, sleep, mobile breakpoint, minimap. (This is what the prompt calls "hud.css".)

I use the prompt's *descriptive* names below but point at the verified real file for each rule. Where it matters I write the literal selector so there's no ambiguity. Both `.mc-button` and `.skin-swatch` (real `app/hud.css`) currently use `font-family: inherit`, so a single body-level font var propagates to them for free.

The work is ordered into 6 slices, each independently lint/typecheck/test-green.

---

## Slice 1 — Pixel web font (`next/font/google`)

**Font choice: "Pixelify Sans".** Rationale: it is a true pixel font but with generous x-height and open counters, so it stays legible at the HUD's 11-15px sizes (stack counts, controls list, recipe labels). "Silkscreen" is heavier/blockier and gets muddy below ~12px; "VT323" is a terminal font that reads more "DOS" than "Minecraft." Pixelify Sans ships weights 400-700 — we need 700 because nearly every HUD label uses `font-weight: 700`, and 400 for body/sub-lines. `next/font/google` self-hosts the woff2 at build time (no runtime Google request, no committed binary), which is why this is the approved exception.

**New file: `app/fonts.ts`**
```ts
import { Pixelify_Sans } from "next/font/google";

// Self-hosted at build by next/font; exposes a CSS var consumed by base.css.
// 400 for body/sub-lines, 700 for the bold HUD labels used throughout.
export const pixelFont = Pixelify_Sans({
  subsets: ["latin"],
  weight: ["400", "700"],
  display: "swap",
  variable: "--mc-font",
  // Fall back to the existing monospace stack until the woff2 loads / if it fails.
  fallback: ["Lucida Console", "Monaco", "Courier New", "monospace"]
});
```

**Edit `app/layout.tsx`** — attach the font's `variable` class to `<html>` so the CSS var is in scope for everything (overlays portal into `body`, all are descendants):
```tsx
import { pixelFont } from "./fonts";
// ...
<html lang="en" className={pixelFont.variable}>
  <body>{children}</body>
</html>
```

**Edit `app/base.css`** — consume the var, keep monospace fallback, and add a dedicated monospace token for the debug overlay:
- In `:root` add `--mc-font-mono: "Lucida Console", Monaco, "Courier New", monospace;`
- Change the `html, body` rule (line 30) to:
  `font-family: var(--mc-font), var(--mc-font-mono);`
- The `--mc-font` value already carries the monospace fallback list, so even before hydration / if the font 404s, text renders in monospace.

**Edit `app/hud.css` (real file with `.debug-line`)** — the F3 overlay should stay monospace for column alignment (it uses `white-space: pre`). Add to `.debug-line`:
`font-family: var(--mc-font-mono);`
(`.debug-overlay` itself sets no font; setting it on `.debug-line` is enough since that's where text lives.)

**Risks/notes:**
- Pixel-font blurriness at non-integer sizes: pixel fonts only look crisp when the rendered glyph grid lands on device pixels. Keep all HUD `font-size` values integers (they already are: 10-22px). Do **not** introduce `rem`/`vw`-derived fractional sizes. No change needed to existing sizes.
- Hydration: `next/font` injects the `variable` className server-side, so `<html className>` is identical on server and client — no mismatch. The font CSS is inlined in `<head>` by Next, so there's no FOUT beyond the `swap` fallback.
- The font slightly changes glyph metrics; the fixed-width slots/buttons absorb this, but eyeball the `.pause-controls-row` two-column layout (it uses `justify-content: space-between`, so it reflows fine).

**Verify after slice 1:** `bun run lint`, `bun run typecheck`, `bun run build` (build pulls the font). Tests unaffected (happy-dom ignores `@font-face`).

---

## Slice 2 — Procedural GUI chrome textures

Extend the existing noise-tile approach. The current `noiseTileUrl` multiplies a base color by noise then by a flat `dimming` factor. For GUI chrome we want a *subtle additive* per-pixel grain around a fixed gray (not a multiply that darkens), plus optional sunken shading. Add new generators alongside `installUiTiles`, keeping everything 16×16, idempotent, and `typeof document` guarded.

**Edit `lib/ui/chromeTiles.ts`** — add a second generator (don't break `noiseTileUrl`):
```ts
// Additive per-pixel grain around a fixed 8-bit gray — for GUI panel/slot/button
// faces. delta is the +/- noise amplitude in 0..255; bias shifts the whole tile
// (negative = sunken slot well, positive = raised button face).
function grayGrainTileUrl(base: number, salt: number, delta: number, bias = 0): string {
  if (typeof document === "undefined") return "";
  const canvas = document.createElement("canvas");
  canvas.width = SPRITE_SIZE;
  canvas.height = SPRITE_SIZE;
  const ctx = canvas.getContext("2d");
  if (!ctx) return "";
  const image = ctx.createImageData(SPRITE_SIZE, SPRITE_SIZE);
  for (let y = 0; y < SPRITE_SIZE; y += 1) {
    for (let x = 0; x < SPRITE_SIZE; x += 1) {
      const v = Math.sin(x * 12.9898 + y * 78.233 + salt * 37.719) * 43758.5453;
      const n = (v - Math.floor(v)) * 2 - 1; // -1..1
      const g = Math.max(0, Math.min(255, Math.round(base + bias + n * delta)));
      const i = (y * SPRITE_SIZE + x) * 4;
      image.data[i] = g;
      image.data[i + 1] = g;
      image.data[i + 2] = g;
      image.data[i + 3] = 255;
    }
  }
  ctx.putImageData(image, 0, 0);
  return canvas.toDataURL();
}
```

Extend `installUiTiles()` to set three new vars (keep the existing `installed` guard and dirt/stone vars):
```ts
const panel  = grayGrainTileUrl(0xc6, 11, 4);          // ~#c6c6c6 +/-4 grain
const well   = grayGrainTileUrl(0x8b, 13, 5, -6);      // darker sunken slot well
const button = grayGrainTileUrl(0x6f, 17, 4, 2);       // button face, faint lift
if (panel)  document.documentElement.style.setProperty("--mc-tile-panel", `url("${panel}")`);
if (well)   document.documentElement.style.setProperty("--mc-tile-well", `url("${well}")`);
if (button) document.documentElement.style.setProperty("--mc-tile-button", `url("${button}")`);
```

**Edit `app/base.css` `:root`** — declare the new vars with `none` defaults (parallels `--mc-tile-dirt/stone`), so SSR/pre-install renders fall back to the existing flat colors:
```css
--mc-tile-panel: none;
--mc-tile-well: none;
--mc-tile-button: none;
```

**Switch flat colors to layered `image + color` backgrounds** (the tile draws on top, the flat color is the fallback when the var is `none` — same pattern as `.pause-controls` which already does `background: var(--mc-tile-dirt), rgba(...)`). Bevel borders are unaffected because they're `border-color`, drawn over the background box. Edits:

- `.inventory-panel` (real `app/ui.css`, ~line 211 in displayed content) and `.renderer-error` (real `app/hud.css`): `background: var(--mc-tile-panel), var(--mc-panel);`
- `.inv-slot` and `.recipe-entry` (real `app/ui.css`): `background: var(--mc-tile-well), var(--mc-slot-well);` Their `:hover` rules set a flat lighter color — keep those, but make them layer too: `background: var(--mc-tile-well), #a3a3a3;` so the grain persists on hover.
- `.mc-button` and `.skin-swatch` (real `app/hud.css`): `background: var(--mc-tile-button), var(--mc-button);` Hover/active keep their bevel inversion; the hover color is the blue `--mc-button-hover`, which should override the grain — set hover to flat `background: var(--mc-button-hover);` (drop the tile on hover so the blue reads clearly). `.mc-slider` track likewise can take the button tile.
- `.hotbar-slot` (real `app/ui.css`) stays translucent dark `rgba(...)` — do **not** add the opaque well tile there (it must show the world through it). Leave as-is.

Because `image-rendering: pixelated` only applies to `<img>`, CSS `background-image` data-URLs scale with smoothing by default. The tiles are sized 16×16 and tiled via the browser's default `repeat`, so each tile renders at native 16×16 (no upscaling) and stays crisp — no extra property needed. (If we ever scale them, add `image-rendering: pixelated` to the element, which is valid for backgrounds in modern browsers.)

**Risks:** `installUiTiles` runs in a `useEffect` after first paint, so there's a one-frame flash of flat color → grain. Acceptable and already the behavior for dirt/stone. SSR safe via the `typeof document` guard and `none` defaults.

**Verify after slice 2:** lint/typecheck/build. `chromeTiles` has no unit test today; under happy-dom `installUiTiles` early-returns (no canvas), so nothing breaks. Manual: open inventory, confirm panel/slots show subtle grain with bevels intact.

## Slice 3 — Authentic item tooltip (replaces `title=`)

**New file: `components/game/ItemTooltip.tsx`** — a self-contained, dependency-free, cursor-following tooltip rendered in a portal to `document.body`. Cursor-follow (not anchored) matches Minecraft and sidesteps slot-overflow/clipping inside the scrollable inventory panel.

Design of the component:
- Props: `{ title: string; lines?: string[]; titleColor?: string }` plus a render-controlling hook. Simplest robust approach: export a small `useItemTooltip()` hook returning `{ tooltip, bind }` where `bind(content)` yields `onMouseEnter/onMouseMove/onMouseLeave` handlers for a slot, and `tooltip` is the JSX to render once at the panel root. This keeps per-slot wiring to one spread and one shared portal.

```tsx
"use client";
import { useCallback, useState } from "react";
import { createPortal } from "react-dom";

export type TooltipContent = { title: string; lines?: string[] } | null;

export function useItemTooltip() {
  const [content, setContent] = useState<TooltipContent>(null);
  const [pos, setPos] = useState({ x: 0, y: 0 });

  const show = useCallback((c: TooltipContent, e: React.MouseEvent) => {
    setContent(c);
    setPos({ x: e.clientX, y: e.clientY });
  }, []);
  const move = useCallback((e: React.MouseEvent) => setPos({ x: e.clientX, y: e.clientY }), []);
  const hide = useCallback(() => setContent(null), []);

  const bind = (c: TooltipContent) =>
    c
      ? {
          onMouseEnter: (e: React.MouseEvent) => show(c, e),
          onMouseMove: move,
          onMouseLeave: hide
        }
      : {};

  const tooltip =
    content && typeof document !== "undefined"
      ? createPortal(
          <div
            className="item-tooltip"
            style={{ left: pos.x + 14, top: pos.y + 14 }}
            role="presentation"
            aria-hidden
          >
            <span className="item-tooltip-title">{content.title}</span>
            {content.lines?.map((line, i) => (
              <span key={i} className="item-tooltip-line">{line}</span>
            ))}
          </div>,
          document.body
        )
      : null;

  return { tooltip, bind };
}
```

**CSS — add to `app/ui.css`** (lives with the other inventory/HUD rules). Minecraft's tooltip is a near-black fill with a 2px violet gradient border (the border is actually two nested borders: a dark outer + a purple gradient inner). Reproduce with a `border` + a gradient via `border-image`, or simpler, an outer dark `border` plus a `::before`/`::after`. Cleanest dependency-free version uses `border-image`:
```css
.item-tooltip {
  position: fixed;
  z-index: 20;                 /* above inventory(6)/pause(9); below nothing interactive */
  pointer-events: none;        /* never steals hover / never blocks canvas */
  max-width: 280px;
  padding: 5px 8px;
  display: grid;
  gap: 2px;
  background: var(--mc-tooltip-bg);
  border: 2px solid transparent;
  border-image: linear-gradient(180deg, var(--mc-tooltip-border-a), var(--mc-tooltip-border-b)) 1;
  outline: 1px solid #100010;  /* the thin black keyline MC draws outside the violet */
}
.item-tooltip-title { color: var(--mc-text-light); font-size: 14px; font-weight: 700; text-shadow: var(--mc-text-shadow); white-space: nowrap; }
.item-tooltip-line  { color: #a8a8a8; font-size: 12px; text-shadow: 1px 1px 0 rgba(0,0,0,.7); }
```

**Wire into `Hotbar.tsx`:**
- Call `const { tooltip, bind } = useItemTooltip();` in the component.
- Replace `title={...}` on each slot button with `{...bind(selected/slot has item ? { title: slot.label } : null)}`.
- Render `{tooltip}` once inside the returned `.hotbar-area`.
- **Keep `aria-label` exactly as-is** (the tooltip is `aria-hidden`; the accessible name comes from `aria-label`).

**Wire into `InventoryPanel.tsx`:**
- Replace the three `title=` sites. Build content from the existing `slotTitle()` logic, but split into title + sub-line:
  - For a durable item: `{ title: slot.label, lines: ["Durability " + (slot.durability ?? slot.maxDurability) + " / " + slot.maxDurability] }` (gray sub-line is more authentic than the old `Label (x/y)`).
  - For stacks > 1, optionally add a `lines: ["x" + count]` (kept minimal — Minecraft shows count in the slot, so this is optional; recommend leaving it off).
  - Armor empty slot: `{ title: ARMOR_SLOT_LABELS[armorSlot] + " (empty)" }` — but since it has no item, you may also pass `null` to suppress the tooltip; recommend passing a faint title to preserve the affordance.
  - Recipe entries: `{ title: recipe.label, lines: stationLocked ? ["Requires " + STATION_LABELS[recipe.station!]] : undefined }`.
- Render `{tooltip}` once at the `.inventory-panel` root.
- Keep all `aria-label`s. Remove the now-unused `slotTitle` only if nothing else references it (extract its label/durability split into the content builder).

**Pointer-lock interaction (critical):** Tooltips only appear while the inventory/hotbar is hovered. The inventory is open only when pointer lock is released (per `docs/architecture.md`, `KeyI` exits lock). The portal node has `pointer-events: none`, so it can never intercept clicks destined for the canvas or trigger an unwanted pointer-lock request. The hotbar is visible during play, but mouse-move events while pointer is locked report movement deltas, not client coords; in locked mode the cursor is hidden so the hotbar isn't hover-reachable anyway — the tooltip simply won't show. No interference.

**Accessibility:** `aria-label` retained on every button; tooltip marked `aria-hidden` so screen readers read the label, not duplicated text.

**Risks:** `createPortal` to `document.body` is SSR-safe here because the guard `typeof document !== "undefined"` returns `null` on the server and these components are already under `"use client"` and only mount client-side. State updates on `onMouseMove` are cheap (two numbers); they only fire while hovering a slot, not during gameplay.

---

## Slice 4 — World dimming backdrop + vignette

**Backdrop (dim the frozen world behind menus).** Currently `.pause-overlay` already has `background: rgba(0,0,0,.55)` (it acts as its own backdrop). The inventory has **no** backdrop. Add a shared dimming element so both read consistently.

Approach: render a single `.menu-backdrop` div in `MinecraftGame.tsx` whenever `inventoryOpen || paused`, placed in JSX *before* the inventory/pause blocks:
```tsx
{(inventoryOpen || paused) ? <div className="menu-backdrop" /> : null}
```
CSS (add to `app/hud.css` near the overlays):
```css
.menu-backdrop {
  position: absolute;
  inset: 0;
  z-index: 5;                 /* above canvas/hotbar(4), below inventory(6) & pause(9) */
  background: rgba(0, 0, 0, 0.55);
  animation: mc-fade-in 140ms ease-out;
}
```
Then **remove** the `background: rgba(0,0,0,.55)` from `.pause-overlay` (keep it transparent — the backdrop now provides the dim) so the two don't double-darken. The pause overlay's z-index 9 keeps it above the backdrop. Inventory (z 6) sits above the backdrop (z 5) — correct.

**Vignette (always-on, subtle).** Add one full-screen, non-interactive layer inside `.game-root`, after the canvas wrap:
```tsx
<div className="vignette" aria-hidden />
```
CSS (add to `app/base.css`, since it's a global game-shell concern):
```css
.vignette {
  position: absolute;
  inset: 0;
  z-index: 2;                 /* above canvas(1-ish/none), below all HUD (>=4) */
  pointer-events: none;
  background: radial-gradient(ellipse at center,
    rgba(0,0,0,0) 55%,
    rgba(0,0,0,var(--mc-vignette)) 100%);
}
```
The canvas wrap has no explicit z-index (auto), so give `.vignette` `z-index: 2` and leave the canvas as the lowest. HUD elements are z 4+, so the vignette sits over the 3D world but under the HUD. `--mc-vignette` token (see Slice 6) keeps strength tunable; ~`0.28` is subtle.

**Risk:** the vignette must not darken HUD text (it doesn't — HUD is above it) and must never block the canvas click that acquires pointer lock (`pointer-events: none` guarantees this).

---

## Slice 5 — Subtle motion

Add keyframes + transitions; gate everything behind `prefers-reduced-motion`.

**Shared fade-in keyframe (add to `app/base.css`):**
```css
@keyframes mc-fade-in { from { opacity: 0; } to { opacity: 1; } }
@keyframes mc-pop-in  { from { opacity: 0; transform: translate(-50%, -50%) scale(0.96); }
                        to   { opacity: 1; transform: translate(-50%, -50%) scale(1); } }
```

**Menu/inventory entrance:**
- `.inventory-panel` (real `app/ui.css`) — already uses `transform: translate(-50%,-50%)`; add `animation: mc-pop-in 150ms ease-out;`. (Keyframe preserves the centering transform so it doesn't jump.)
- `.pause-overlay` (real `app/hud.css`) — `animation: mc-fade-in 140ms ease-out;` and `.pause-menu` a slightly delayed `mc-fade-in`.
- `.menu-backdrop` — `mc-fade-in` (from Slice 4).

**Slot hover** (real `app/ui.css`): add `transition: background-color 90ms ease, transform 90ms ease;` to `.inv-slot` and `.hotbar-slot`, and a tiny lift on hover `transform: translateY(-1px);` (skip lift on `.hotbar-slot` to avoid nudging during play — apply lift only to `.inv-slot:hover`).

**Button press depth** (`.mc-button`, real `app/hud.css`): it already inverts the bevel on `:active`. Add `transition: background-color 80ms ease;` and on `:active` a `transform: translateY(1px);` for a tactile press. Keep the bevel inversion.

**Hotbar item-name pop** (`.hotbar-item-name`, real `app/ui.css`): the existing `hotbar-name-fade` only fades opacity. Refine to add a small rise so it "pops" up like Minecraft:
```css
@keyframes hotbar-name-fade {
  0%   { opacity: 0; transform: translateY(4px); }
  8%   { opacity: 1; transform: translateY(0); }
  70%  { opacity: 1; transform: translateY(0); }
  100% { opacity: 0; transform: translateY(0); }
}
```
(Add `transform: translateY(0)` baseline to `.hotbar-item-name` so the animation has a defined start.)

**Reduced motion (append to whichever CSS file, e.g. `app/base.css`):**
```css
@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce) {
  .inventory-panel, .pause-overlay, .pause-menu, .menu-backdrop,
  .hotbar-item-name, .click-hint { animation: none !important; }
  .inv-slot, .hotbar-slot, .mc-button { transition: none !important; transform: none !important; }
}
```
This also tames the existing `click-hint-pulse`. The `.sleep-overlay` opacity transition is a gameplay fade (sleeping), so leave it.

**Risk:** animations on conditionally-rendered overlays replay every open (desired). `transform` on `.inv-slot:hover` could shift the durability/count overlays by 1px — acceptable, and they're positioned relative to `.item-icon`, not the slot, so they move together.

---

## Slice 6 — Token / palette refinements (`app/base.css`)

Add new `:root` tokens (no churn to existing ones):
```css
--mc-tooltip-bg: #100010ee;           /* near-black with a violet undertone, ~93% opaque */
--mc-tooltip-border-a: #28007f;       /* MC tooltip violet (top) */
--mc-tooltip-border-b: #5b00b5;       /* violet (bottom) — the 2px gradient */
--mc-tooltip-sub: #a8a8a8;            /* light-gray sub-lines */
--mc-vignette: 0.28;                  /* vignette strength (unitless, fed into rgba) */
```
Point the Slice-3 CSS at `--mc-tooltip-sub` for `.item-tooltip-line` color instead of the hard-coded `#a8a8a8`. Keep the gray chrome tokens (`--mc-panel`, etc.) unchanged — they're already authentic and now back the tile fallbacks.

---

## Verification (run in this order; keep each slice green)

Per-slice and final:
1. `bun run lint`
2. `bun run typecheck`
3. `bun run format:check` (printWidth 160, trailingComma none — match the new files to this; e.g. multiline function args, no trailing commas)
4. `bun test`
5. `bun run build` (mandatory after Slice 1 — exercises the `next/font` fetch/self-host)

**Tests that WILL break and the fixes:**
- `components/game/InventoryPanel.test.tsx`:
  - Line 98: `expect(helmetSlot.getAttribute("title")).toBe("Helmet (260/260)")` — the `title` attribute is removed. **Fix:** drop this `getAttribute("title")` assertion. The accessible name is still asserted via `getByRole("button", { name: "Helmet: Helmet" })` (the `aria-label`), so coverage of the slot's identity remains. If we want to assert the new tooltip, that requires hovering (`user.hover`) and querying the portal text — heavier; recommend simply asserting the durability still surfaces via the icon's durability bar or just removing the title check.
  - Line 131: `expect(button.getAttribute("title")).toBe("Requires Furnace")` — same removal. **Fix:** the lock is already asserted by `expect(button.disabled).toBe(true)` immediately above; drop the title assertion (or convert to a hover-then-find-portal-text check if we want explicit tooltip coverage). Recommended minimal: remove the two `getAttribute("title")` lines.
- `components/game/Hotbar.test.tsx`: only asserts roles, alt text, counts, and click — **no `title` assertion**, so it passes unchanged. The added `{...bind(...)}` spread and `{tooltip}` render don't change existing queries (tooltip renders `null` with no hover).
- `DeathScreen.test.tsx`, `PauseMenu.test.tsx`, `StatusBars.test.tsx`: unaffected.
- Optionally **add** a focused `ItemTooltip.test.tsx`: render the hook host, `user.hover` a slot, assert the portal shows the title + sub-line, and that the node is `aria-hidden`. Keep it light since happy-dom + portals to `document.body` work in this setup (Testing Library already uses `document.body`).

**Manual gameplay pass** (required — visual + pointer-lock change, per AGENTS.md):
- Open inventory (`I`): backdrop dims world; panel pops in; slots/panel show grain with bevels; hover items → violet-bordered tooltip follows cursor; durable items show gray durability sub-line; locked furnace recipe shows "Requires Furnace".
- Open pause (`Esc`): backdrop + fade-in, buttons press with depth, no double-dim.
- Confirm vignette is visible at screen edges during play and never blocks the "Click to play" pointer-lock acquisition.
- Confirm clicking the canvas still acquires pointer lock and the tooltip never appears during locked play.
- Toggle OS "reduce motion" and confirm animations stop.

**Docs to update (same change, per AGENTS.md):**
- `CHANGELOG.md` — new entry: pixel web font, procedural GUI chrome tiles, item tooltips replacing native `title=`, menu backdrop/vignette, UI motion.
- `docs/architecture.md` — in the "React shell" bullet about `lib/ui/` procedural pixel art, note the new chrome tiles (`--mc-tile-panel/well/button`) and the `ItemTooltip`/`useItemTooltip` component; note the approved font exception to "zero binary assets" (build-time self-hosted via `next/font`).
- `AGENTS.md` — the "Zero binary assets" paragraph should note the one approved exception (a build-time self-hosted pixel web font) so future agents don't "fix" it.
- `README.md` / `docs/manual.md` — optional: mention tooltips if player-facing docs describe the HUD.

## Suggested branch / commit slicing
Branch `feat/mc-ui-polish` off `main`. One commit per slice above (font → chrome tiles → tooltip → backdrop+vignette → motion → tokens), with the test edits folded into the tooltip commit (Slice 3) and doc/CHANGELOG updates in the final commit. Each commit keeps lint/typecheck/test green.

## Top risks (flagged)
1. **Font self-host build step** — `bun run build` must succeed offline-capable; `next/font/google` caches the font at build. If the build environment blocks the one-time Google fetch, the fallback monospace stack still renders (graceful), but flag it.
2. **Pixel-font crispness** — keep all font sizes integer; do not refactor to relative units.
3. **Tooltip + pointer-lock** — guaranteed safe only because the portal is `pointer-events: none` and inventory implies unlocked; do not remove that rule.
4. **Double-dim regression** — must remove `.pause-overlay` background when adding `.menu-backdrop`, or the pause screen darkens twice.
5. **happy-dom canvas** — `installUiTiles` and `spriteDataUrl` already no-op without a 2D context; the new `grayGrainTileUrl` follows the same guard, so tests stay green.

{
  "file_path": "/Users/hutusi/.claude/plans/please-try-to-further-idempotent-sparrow.md",
  "content": "# Plan: Authentic Minecraft UI Polish\n\n## Context\n\nThe game's UI is already Minecraft-flavored (gray bevel panels, pixelated sprites,\nprocedural noise tiles) but several things keep it from reading as *authentically*\nMinecraft: the font is a generic monospace stack (Lucida Console), panels/slots/buttons\nare **flat colors** rather than textured, item hovers use plain **browser `title=`\ntooltips** instead of Minecraft's iconic violet-bordered tooltip, open menus don't dim\nthe world behind them, and there's no screen vignette or interaction motion.\n\nThe user asked to \"further refine and polish the UI… sleeker and more detailed… match\nthe authentic texture of real Minecraft,\" and made two decisions:\n\n1. **Font** → add a real **pixel web font** via `next/font/google`. This bundles a\n   build-time, self-hosted font file — a **deliberate, documented exception** to the\n   project's zero-binary-asset rule (everything else stays procedural/CSS).\n2. **Scope** → full pass: generated GUI textures + authentic tooltips + world-dimming\n   backdrop + vignette + **subtle motion** (transitions/animations).\n\nOutcome: the HUD, inventory, and menus gain texture grain, real depth, a Minecraft\ntooltip, and tasteful motion, while staying procedural everywhere except the one font.\n\n## Verified file mapping (important)\n\nEdits are mapped from **direct file Reads**, not descriptive names. The two HUD CSS files\nare easy to confuse:\n\n- **`app/hud.css`** → `.hud-bottom`, `.status-*`, `.hotbar`, `.hotbar-slot`, `.item-*`,\n  `.crosshair`, `.caps-indicator`, `.inventory-panel`, `.inv-grid`, `.inv-slot`,\n  `.recipe-*`, `.death-*`, `.sleep-*`, the `max-width:720px` block, `.minimap`.\n- **`app/ui.css`** → `.pause-*`, `.mc-button`, `.skin-swatch`, `.mc-slider`,\n  `.debug-overlay`/`.debug-line`, `.click-hint`, `.hud-toast`, `.renderer-error`.\n\nNew tooltip CSS and `.menu-backdrop` go in `app/hud.css` (they belong with the inventory/HUD\nrules); the vignette + shared keyframes + tokens go in `app/base.css`.\n\n## Decisions\n\n- **Font: \"Pixelify Sans\"** (`next/font/google`, weights 400 + 700, `display: swap`).\n  It's a true pixel font but stays legible at the 10–15px HUD sizes; Silkscreen muddies\n  below ~12px and VT323 reads more \"DOS\" than Minecraft. Keep the existing monospace\n  stack as the fallback list, and keep the F3 debug overlay monospace for column alignment.\n\n---\n\n## Slice 1 — Pixel web font\n\n**New file `app/fonts.ts`:**\n```ts\nimport { Pixelify_Sans } from \"next/font/google\";\nexport const pixelFont = Pixelify_Sans({\n  subsets: [\"latin\"],\n  weight: [\"400\", \"700\"],\n  display: \"swap\",\n  variable: \"--mc-font\",\n  fallback: [\"Lucida Console\", \"Monaco\", \"Courier New\", \"monospace\"]\n});\n```\n\n**`app/layout.tsx`** — apply the variable on `<html>`:\n```tsx\nimport { pixelFont } from \"./fonts\";\n<html lang=\"en\" className={pixelFont.variable}> …\n```\n\n**`app/base.css`:**\n- Add token `--mc-font-mono: \"Lucida Console\", Monaco, \"Courier New\", monospace;`\n- Change `html, body` `font-family` (line 30) to `var(--mc-font), var(--mc-font-mono);`\n\n**`app/ui.css`** — keep the F3 readout monospace: add `font-family: var(--mc-font-mono);`\nto `.debug-line`.\n\nKeep all font-size values **integer** (they already are) so the pixel font stays crisp.\n\n---\n\n## Slice 2 — Procedural GUI chrome textures\n\n**`lib/ui/chromeTiles.ts`** — add a second generator next to `noiseTileUrl` (same 16×16,\n`typeof document` guard, data-URL → CSS var, idempotent `installed` flag). This one does\n*additive* grain around a fixed 8-bit gray (panel/slot/button faces shouldn't be darkened\nby a multiply):\n```ts\nfunction grayGrainTileUrl(base: number, salt: number, delta: number, bias = 0): string {\n  if (typeof document === \"undefined\") return \"\";\n  const canvas = document.createElement(\"canvas\");\n  canvas.width = SPRITE_SIZE; canvas.height = SPRITE_SIZE;\n  const ctx = canvas.getContext(\"2d\");\n  if (!ctx) return \"\";\n  const image = ctx.createImageData(SPRITE_SIZE, SPRITE_SIZE);\n  for (let y = 0; y < SPRITE_SIZE; y += 1) {\n    for (let x = 0; x < SPRITE_SIZE; x += 1) {\n      const v = Math.sin(x * 12.9898 + y * 78.233 + salt * 37.719) * 43758.5453;\n      const n = (v - Math.floor(v)) * 2 - 1;\n      const g = Math.max(0, Math.min(255, Math.round(base + bias + n * delta)));\n      const i = (y * SPRITE_SIZE + x) * 4;\n      image.data[i] = g; image.data[i + 1] = g; image.data[i + 2] = g; image.data[i + 3] = 255;\n    }\n  }\n  ctx.putImageData(image, 0, 0);\n  return canvas.toDataURL();\n}\n```\nExtend `installUiTiles()` to also set:\n```ts\nconst panel  = grayGrainTileUrl(0xc6, 11, 4); bevel `border-color`\nis unaffected):\n- `app/hud.css`: `.inventory-panel` → `background: var(--mc-tile-panel), var(--mc-panel);`\n- `app/hud.css`: `.inv-slot` and `.recipe-entry` → `background: var(--mc-tile-well), var(--mc-slot-well);`\n  and their `:hover` → `var(--mc-tile-well), #a3a3a3;` (grain persists on hover).\n- `app/ui.css`: `.mc-button`, `.skin-swatch` → `background: var(--mc-tile-button), var(--mc-button);`\n  Leave `:hover`/`:focus-visible` as the **flat blue** `--mc-button-hover` (drop the tile so\n  the highlight reads cleanly); Cursor-following (matches Minecraft,\navoids clipping inside the scrollable inventory), portaled to `document.body`,\n`pointer-events: none`.\n```tsx\n\"use client\";\nimport { useCallback, useState } from \"react\";\nimport { createPortal } from \"react-dom\";\nexport type TooltipContent = { title: string; lines?: string[] } | null;\nexport function useItemTooltip() {\n  const [content, setContent] = useState<TooltipContent>(null);\n  const [pos, setPos] = useState({ x: 0, y: 0 });\n  const bind = (c: TooltipContent) =>\n    c ? {\n      onMouseEnter: (e: React.MouseEvent) => { setContent(c); setPos({ x: e.clientX, y: e.clientY }); },\n      onMouseMove: (e: React.MouseEvent) => setPos({ x: e.clientX, y: e.clientY }),\n      onMouseLeave: () => setContent(null)\n    } : {};\n  const tooltip = content && typeof document !== \"undefined\"\n    ? { title: slot.label } : null)}`; render `{tooltip}`\n  once inside `.hotbar-area`.\n- `components/game/InventoryPanel.tsx`: call the hook; render `{tooltip}` once at the\n  `.inventory-panel` root. Replace the three `title=` sites with `bind(...)`:\n  - durable item → `{ title: slot.label, lines: [\"Durability \" + (slot.durability ?? slot.maxDurability) + \" / \" + slot.maxDurability] }`\n  - plain item → `{ title: slot.label }`\n  - empty armor slot → `{ title: ARMOR_SLOT_LABELS[armorSlot] + \" (empty)\" }`\n  - recipe entry → `{ title: recipe.label, lines: stationLocked ? [\"Requires \" + STATION_LABELS[recipe.station!]] : undefined }`\n  - Refactor `slotTitle()` into a content builder (or inline); remove it if unused.\n\n**Pointer-lock safety:** inventory is only open when pointer lock is released; the portal\nis `pointer-events: none`, so it can never intercept the canvas click that re-acquires\nlock, and during locked play the cursor is hidden so slots aren't hover-reachable (tooltip\nsimply won't show). Do not remove the `pointer-events: none` rule.\n\n---\n\n## Slice 4 — World-dimming backdrop + vignette\n\n**Backdrop** (`components/MinecraftGame.tsx`): render one shared dimmer when either menu is\nopen, *before* the inventory/pause blocks:\n```tsx\n{(inventoryOpen || paused) ? <div className=\"menu-backdrop\" /> : null}\n```\n**`app/hud.css`:**\n```css\n.menu-backdrop { position: absolute; inset: 0; z-index: 5; background: rgba(0,0,0,.55); animation: mc-fade-in 140ms ease-out; }\n```\nThen **remove** `background: rgba(0,0,0,.55)` from `.pause-overlay` (`app/ui.css`) so the\npause screen doesn't double-darken (its z-index 9 keeps it above the backdrop; inventory\nz-index 6 sits above the backdrop's z-index 5 — correct).\n\n**Vignette** (`components/MinecraftGame.tsx`): one non-interactive layer after the canvas\nwrap: `<div className=\"vignette\" aria-hidden />`.\n**`app/base.css`:**\n```css\n.vignette {\n  position: absolute; inset: 0; z-index: 2; pointer-events: none;\n  background: radial-gradient(ellipse at center, rgba(0,0,0,0) 55%, rgba(0,0,0,var(--mc-vignette)) 100%);\n}\n```\nSits above the canvas (auto z-index) and below all HUD (z-index ≥ 4); `pointer-events: none`\nkeeps the \"Click to play\" lock acquisition working.\n\n---\n\n## Slice 5 — Subtle motion (respect `prefers-reduced-motion`)\n\n**`app/base.css`** — shared keyframes:\n```css\n@keyframes mc-fade-in { from { opacity: 0; } to { opacity: 1; } }\n@keyframes mc-pop-in  { from { opacity: 0; transform: translate(-50%,-50%) scale(.96); } to { opacity: 1; transform: translate(-50%,-50%) scale(1); } }\n```\n- `.inventory-panel` (`app/hud.css`) — `animation: mc-pop-in 150ms ease-out;` (keyframe\n  preserves its centering transform).\n- `.pause-overlay` (`app/ui.css`) — `animation: mc-fade-in 140ms ease-out;`.\n- `.inv-slot` (`app/hud.css`) — `transition: background-color 90ms ease, transform 90ms ease;`\n  and `:hover { transform: translateY(-1px); }` (skip lift on `.hotbar-slot` so it doesn't\n  nudge during play; a `background-color` transition there is fine).\n- `.mc-button` (`app/ui.css`) — `transition: background-color 80ms ease;` and\n  `:active { transform: translateY(1px); }` (keep the bevel inversion).\n- `.hotbar-item-name` keyframe `hotbar-name-fade` (`app/hud.css`) — add a small rise so the\n  name \"pops\": `0% {opacity:0; transform:translateY(4px)} 8%/70% {opacity:1; transform:translateY(0)} 100% {opacity:0}`; set baseline `transform: translateY(0)` on the element.\n\n**`app/base.css`** — reduced-motion guard:\n```css\n@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce) {\n  .inventory-panel, .pause-overlay, .pause-menu, .menu-backdrop, .hotbar-item-name, .click-hint { animation: none !important; }\n  .inv-slot, .hotbar-slot, .mc-button { transition: none !important; transform: none !important; }\n}\n```\n(Leave the `.sleep-overlay` opacity transition — it's a gameplay fade.)\n\n---\n\n## Slice 6 — Tokens / palette (`app/base.css` `:root`)\n\nAdd (no churn to existing gray tokens, which now back the tile fallbacks):\n```css\n--mc-tooltip-bg: #100010ee;\n--mc-tooltip-border-a: #28007f;\n--mc-tooltip-border-b: #5b00b5;\n--mc-tooltip-sub: #a8a8a8;\n--mc-vignette: 0.28;\n```\n\n---\n\n## Files\n\n- **New:** `app/fonts.ts`, `components/game/ItemTooltip.tsx`\n- **Edit:** `app/layout.tsx`, `app/base.css`, `app/hud.css`, `app/ui.css`,\n  `lib/ui/chromeTiles.ts`, `components/MinecraftGame.tsx`, `components/game/Hotbar.tsx`,\n  `components/game/InventoryPanel.tsx`, `components/game/InventoryPanel.test.tsx`\n- **Optional new:** `components/game/ItemTooltip.test.tsx`\n\n## Test updates\n\n`components/game/InventoryPanel.test.tsx` — the `title` attribute is gone after the tooltip\nswitch:\n- **Line 98** `expect(helmetSlot.getAttribute(\"title\")).toBe(\"Helmet (260/260)\")` — remove\n  (identity already covered by the `aria-label` query `\"Helmet: Helmet\"` on line 96).\n- **Line 131** `expect(button.getAttribute(\"title\")).toBe(\"Requires Furnace\")` — remove\n  (the lock is already asserted by `expect(button.disabled).toBe(true)` on line 130).\n\n`Hotbar.test.tsx` and all other component tests pass unchanged. > AGENT

The plan is complete and written. Here's a summary of what I designed:

## What I found
Your UI is already solidly Minecraft-flavored — pure vanilla CSS (`base.css`/`hud.css`/`ui.css`) with a `--mc-*` token palette, 4-part bevel borders, procedural 16×16 sprites, and noise tiles installed as CSS vars via `chromeTiles.ts`. The authenticity gaps are: the generic monospace font, **flat** (untextured) panels/slots/buttons, native browser `title=` tooltips instead of Minecraft's violet-bordered tooltip, no world-dimming behind menus, and no vignette or motion.

## The plan (6 lint-green slices)
1. **Pixel font** — "Pixelify Sans" via `next/font/google` (self-hosted at build; the one approved zero-binary exception), monospace kept as fallback and for the F3 overlay.
2. **Procedural GUI textures** — extend `chromeTiles.ts` with a gray-grain tile generator; layer `--mc-tile-panel/well/button` over the existing flat colors (bevels preserved) so panels/slots/buttons gain real texture.
3. **Authentic tooltip** — a new `useItemTooltip()` hook + portal (near-black fill, 2px violet gradient border) replacing all `title=` attributes, `pointer-events:none` so it never breaks pointer-lock.
4. **Depth** — a shared `.menu-backdrop` dimmer behind inventory/pause + a subtle full-screen vignette.
5. **Motion** — pop/fade-in for menus, slot hover lift, button press depth, hotbar name pop — all gated behind `prefers-reduced-motion`.
6. **Tokens** — tooltip + vignette colors in `:root`.

I caught and corrected a file-mapping error from the design agent (the `hud.css`/`ui.css` contents), and verified the two `InventoryPanel.test.tsx` assertions (lines 98, 131) that need updating when `title=` goes away.

Plan saved to `/Users/hutusi/.claude/plans/please-try-to-further-idempotent-sparrow.md`.
