{\n      onMouseEnter: (e: React.MouseEvent) => { setContent(c); setPos({ x: e.clientX, y: e.clientY }); },\n      onMouseMove: (e: React.MouseEvent) => setPos({ x: e.clientX, y: e.clientY }),\n      onMouseLeave: () => setContent(null)\n    } : {};\n  const tooltip = content && typeof document !== \"undefined\"\n    ? createPortal(\n        <div className=\"item-tooltip\" style={{ left: pos.x + 14, top: pos.y + 14 }} aria-hidden>\n          <span className=\"item-tooltip-title\">{content.title}</span>\n          {content.lines?.map((l, i) => <span key={i} className=\"item-tooltip-line\">{l}</span>)}\n        </div>, document.body)\n    : null;\n  return { tooltip, bind };\n}\n```\n\n**`app/hud.css`** — add the tooltip styling (near-black fill + 2px violet gradient border +\nthin black keyline, like Minecraft):\n```css\n.item-tooltip {\n  position: fixed; z-index: 20; pointer-events: none; max-width: 280px;\n  padding: 5px 8px; display: grid; gap: 2px; background: var(--mc-tooltip-bg);\n  border: 2px solid transparent;\n  border-image: linear-gradient(180deg, var(--mc-tooltip-border-a), var(--mc-tooltip-border-b)) 1;\n  outline: 1px solid #100010;\n}\n.item-tooltip-title { color: var(--mc-text-light); font-size: 14px; font-weight: 700; text-shadow: var(--mc-text-shadow); white-space: nowrap; }\n.item-tooltip-line  { color: var(--mc-tooltip-sub); font-size: 12px; text-shadow: 1px 1px 0 rgba(0,0,0,.7); }\n```\n\n**Wire in (keep every `aria-label` exactly as-is — tooltip is `aria-hidden`):**\n- `components/game/Hotbar.tsx`: call the hook; replace each slot's `title=` with\n  `{...bind(slot.id && slot.count > 0 ? { title: slot.label } : null)}`; render `{tooltip}`\n  once inside `.hotbar-area`.\n- `components/game/InventoryPanel.tsx`: call the hook; render `{tooltip}` once at the\n  `.inventory-panel` root. Replace the three `title=` sites with `bind(...)`:\n  - durable item → `{ title: slot.label, lines: [\"Durability \" + (slot.durability ?? slot.maxDurability) + \" / \" + slot.maxDurability] }`\n  - plain item → `{ title: slot.label }`\n  - empty armor slot → `{ title: ARMOR_SLOT_LABELS[armorSlot] + \" (empty)\" }`\n  - recipe entry → `{ title: recipe.label, lines: stationLocked ? [\"Requires \" + STATION_LABELS[recipe.station!]] : undefined }`\n  - Refactor `slotTitle()` into a content builder (or inline); remove it if unused.\n\n**Pointer-lock safety:** inventory is only open when pointer lock is released; the portal\nis `pointer-events: none`, so it can never intercept the canvas click that re-acquires\nlock, and during locked play the cursor is hidden so slots aren't hover-reachable (tooltip\nsimply won't show). Do not remove the `pointer-events: none` rule.\n\n---\n\n## Slice 4 — World-dimming backdrop + vignette\n\n**Backdrop** (`components/MinecraftGame.tsx`): render one shared dimmer when either menu is\nopen, *before* the inventory/pause blocks:\n```tsx\n{(inventoryOpen || paused) ? <div className=\"menu-backdrop\" /> : null}\n```\n**`app/hud.css`:**\n```css\n.menu-backdrop { position: absolute; inset: 0; z-index: 5; background: rgba(0,0,0,.55); animation: mc-fade-in 140ms ease-out; }\n```\nThen **remove** `background: rgba(0,0,0,.55)` from `.pause-overlay` (`app/ui.css`) so the\npause screen doesn't double-darken (its z-index 9 keeps it above the backdrop; inventory\nz-index 6 sits above the backdrop's z-index 5 — correct).\n\n**Vignette** (`components/MinecraftGame.tsx`): one non-interactive layer after the canvas\nwrap: `<div className=\"vignette\" aria-hidden />`.\n**`app/base.css`:**\n```css\n.vignette {\n  position: absolute; inset: 0; z-index: 2; pointer-events: none;\n  background: radial-gradient(ellipse at center, rgba(0,0,0,0) 55%, rgba(0,0,0,var(--mc-vignette)) 100%);\n}\n```\nSits above the canvas (auto z-index) and below all HUD (z-index ≥ 4); `pointer-events: none`\nkeeps the \"Click to play\" lock acquisition working.\n\n---\n\n## Slice 5 — Subtle motion (respect `prefers-reduced-motion`)\n\n**`app/base.css`** — shared keyframes:\n```css\n@keyframes mc-fade-in { from { opacity: 0; } to { opacity: 1; } }\n@keyframes mc-pop-in  { from { opacity: 0; transform: translate(-50%,-50%) scale(.96); } to { opacity: 1; transform: translate(-50%,-50%) scale(1); } }\n```\n- `.inventory-panel` (`app/hud.css`) — `animation: mc-pop-in 150ms ease-out;` (keyframe\n  preserves its centering transform).\n- `.pause-overlay` (`app/ui.css`) — `animation: mc-fade-in 140ms ease-out;`.\n- `.inv-slot` (`app/hud.css`) — `transition: background-color 90ms ease, transform 90ms ease;`\n  and `:hover { transform: translateY(-1px); }` (skip lift on `.hotbar-slot` so it doesn't\n  nudge during play; a `background-color` transition there is fine).\n- `.mc-button` (`app/ui.css`) — `transition: background-color 80ms ease;` and\n  `:active { transform: translateY(1px); }` (keep the bevel inversion).\n- `.hotbar-item-name` keyframe `hotbar-name-fade` (`app/hud.css`) — add a small rise so the\n  name \"pops\": `0% {opacity:0; transform:translateY(4px)} 8%/70% {opacity:1; transform:translateY(0)} 100% {opacity:0}`; set baseline `transform: translateY(0)` on the element.\n\n**`app/base.css`** — reduced-motion guard:\n```css\n@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce) {\n  .inventory-panel, .pause-overlay, .pause-menu, .menu-backdrop, .hotbar-item-name, .click-hint { animation: none !important; }\n  .inv-slot, .hotbar-slot, .mc-button { transition: none !important; transform: none !important; }\n}\n```\n(Leave the `.sleep-overlay` opacity transition — it's a gameplay fade.)\n\n---\n\n## Slice 6 — Tokens / palette (`app/base.css` `:root`)\n\nAdd (no churn to existing gray tokens, which now back the tile fallbacks):\n```css\n--mc-tooltip-bg: #100010ee;\n--mc-tooltip-border-a: #28007f;\n--mc-tooltip-border-b: #5b00b5;\n--mc-tooltip-sub: #a8a8a8;\n--mc-vignette: 0.28;\n```\n\n---\n\n## Files\n\n- **New:** `app/fonts.ts`, `components/game/ItemTooltip.tsx`\n- **Edit:** `app/layout.tsx`, `app/base.css`, `app/hud.css`, `app/ui.css`,\n  `lib/ui/chromeTiles.ts`, `components/MinecraftGame.tsx`, `components/game/Hotbar.tsx`,\n  `components/game/InventoryPanel.tsx`, `components/game/InventoryPanel.test.tsx`\n- **Optional new:** `components/game/ItemTooltip.test.tsx`\n\n## Test updates\n\n`components/game/InventoryPanel.test.tsx` — the `title` attribute is gone after the tooltip\nswitch:\n- **Line 98** `expect(helmetSlot.getAttribute(\"title\")).toBe(\"Helmet (260/260)\")` — remove\n  (identity already covered by the `aria-label` query `\"Helmet: Helmet\"` on line 96).\n- **Line 131** `expect(button.getAttribute(\"title\")).toBe(\"Requires Furnace\")` — remove\n  (the lock is already asserted by `expect(button.disabled).toBe(true)` on line 130).\n\n`Hotbar.test.tsx` and all other component tests pass unchanged. Optionally add\n`ItemTooltip.test.tsx`: hover a slot, assert the portal shows title + sub-line and is\n`aria-hidden`.\n\n## Verification\n\nRun (and keep green per slice): `bun run lint`, `bun run typecheck`, `bun run format:check`\n(printWidth 160, no trailing commas — match new files), `bun test`, and **`bun run build`**\n(mandatory after Slice 1 — exercises the `next/font` self-host).\n\nManual gameplay pass (renderer/input + visual change):\n- Open inventory (`I`): world dims behind, panel pops in, panel/slots show grain with bevels\n  intact, hovering items shows the violet-bordered tooltip following the cursor, durable\n  items show a gray \"Durability x / y\" line, a furnace recipe shows \"Requires Furnace\".\n- Open pause (`Esc`): backdrop + fade-in, buttons press with depth, **no double-dim**.\n- Vignette visible at screen edges; \"Click to play\" still acquires pointer lock; the tooltip\n  never appears during locked play.\n- Toggle OS \"reduce motion\" → animations stop.\n\n## Docs (same change, per AGENTS.md)\n\n- `CHANGELOG.md` — entry: pixel web font, procedural GUI chrome tiles, item tooltips\n  replacing native `title=`, menu backdrop + vignette, UI motion.\n- `docs/architecture.md` — note the new chrome tiles (`--mc-tile-panel/well/button`) and\n  `ItemTooltip`/`useItemTooltip` in the `lib/ui/` + React-shell sections; record the approved\n  font exception to \"zero binary assets\".\n- `AGENTS.md` — add the one approved exception (build-time self-hosted pixel web font via\n  `next/font`) to the \"Zero binary assets\" note so future agents don't revert it.\n\n## Branch / commits\n\nBranch `feat/mc-ui-polish` off `main`; one commit per slice (font → chrome tiles →\ntooltip+test edits → backdrop+vignette → motion → tokens), docs/CHANGELOG in the final\ncommit. Conventional Commit messages with a body; no `Co-Authored-By`. Don't push or open a\nPR unless asked.\n\n## Risks\n\n1. **Font self-host at build** — `next/font/google` fetches+caches at build; if the build\n   env blocks that one fetch, the monospace fallback still renders (graceful) — flag if it\n   fails.\n2. **Pixel-font crispness** — keep all font sizes integer; don't switch to fractional units.\n3. **Tooltip + pointer-lock** — safe only because the portal is `pointer-events: none` and\n   inventory implies unlocked; keep that rule.\n4. **Double-dim** — must remove `.pause-overlay`'s background when adding `.menu-backdrop`.\n5. **happy-dom canvas** — `grayGrainTileUrl` early-returns without a 2D context (same guard\n   as the existing tiles), so tests stay green.\n",

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/ui/chromeTiles.ts",
  "old_string": "let installed = false;\n\n/** Sets --mc-tile-dirt / --mc-tile-stone on the document root (idempotent). */\nexport function installUiTiles(): void {\n  if (installed || typeof document === \"undefined\") return;\n  const dirt = noiseTileUrl(BLOCK_COLORS[BlockId.Dirt] ?? [0.46, 0.33, 0.2], 3, 0.55);\n  const stone = noiseTileUrl(BLOCK_COLORS[BlockId.Stone] ?? [0.54, 0.56, 0.58], 7, 0.9);\n  if (!dirt || !stone) return;\n  document.documentElement.style.setProperty(\"--mc-tile-dirt\", `url(\"${dirt}\")`);\n  document.documentElement.style.setProperty(\"--mc-tile-stone\", `url(\"${stone}\")`);\n  installed = true;\n}",
  "new_string": "/**\n * A grayscale grain tile for GUI chrome (panel face, slot well, button face).\n * Unlike noiseTileUrl this adds a small +/- variation around a fixed 8-bit gray\n * rather than multiplying a color, so the panel stays its intended shade with\n * just a faint texture. `bias` shifts the whole tile (negative = darker/sunken\n * slot well, positive = a faint raised button face); `delta` is the grain\n * amplitude in 0..255.\n */\nfunction grayGrainTileUrl(base: number, salt: number, delta: number, bias = 0): string {\n  if (typeof document === \"undefined\") return \"\";\n  const canvas = document.createElement(\"canvas\");\n  canvas.width = SPRITE_SIZE;\n  canvas.height = SPRITE_SIZE;\n  const ctx = canvas.getContext(\"2d\");\n  if (!ctx) return \"\";\n\n  const image = ctx.createImageData(SPRITE_SIZE, SPRITE_SIZE);\n  for (let y = 0; y < SPRITE_SIZE; y += 1) {\n    for (let x = 0; x < SPRITE_SIZE; x += 1) {\n      const v = Math.sin(x * 12.9898 + y * 78.233 + salt * 37.719) * 43758.5453;\n      const n = (v - Math.floor(v)) * 2 - 1;\n      const g = Math.max(0, Math.min(255, Math.round(base + bias + n * delta)));\n      const i = (y * SPRITE_SIZE + x) * 4;\n      image.data[i] = g;\n      image.data[i + 1] = g;\n      image.data[i + 2] = g;\n      image.data[i + 3] = 255;\n    }\n  }\n  ctx.putImageData(image, 0, 0);\n  return canvas.toDataURL();\n}\n\nlet installed = false;\n\n/**\n * Sets the generated UI tile CSS custom properties on the document root\n * (idempotent): --mc-tile-dirt / --mc-tile-stone for the menu backdrops, and\n * --mc-tile-panel / --mc-tile-well / --mc-tile-button for the chrome grain that\n * layers over the flat gray panel, slot, and button colors.\n */\nexport function installUiTiles(): void {\n  if (installed || typeof document === \"undefined\") return;\n  const dirt = noiseTileUrl(BLOCK_COLORS[BlockId.Dirt] ?? [0.46, 0.33, 0.2], 3, 0.55);\n  const stone = noiseTileUrl(BLOCK_COLORS[BlockId.Stone] ?? [0.54, 0.56, 0.58], 7, 0.9);\n  if (!dirt || !stone) return;\n  const root = document.documentElement.style;\n  root.setProperty(\"--mc-tile-dirt\", `url(\"${dirt}\")`);\n  root.setProperty(\"--mc-tile-stone\", `url(\"${stone}\")`);\n  // 0xc6 ~ --mc-panel, 0x8b ~ --mc-slot-well (sunk a touch), 0x6f ~ --mc-button.\n  const panel = grayGrainTileUrl(0xc6, 11, 4);\n  const well = grayGrainTileUrl(0x8b, 13, 5, -6);\n  const button = grayGrainTileUrl(0x6f, 17, 4, 2);\n  if (panel) root.setProperty(\"--mc-tile-panel\", `url(\"${panel}\")`);\n  if (well) root.setProperty(\"--mc-tile-well\", `url(\"${well}\")`);\n  if (button) root.setProperty(\"--mc-tile-button\", `url(\"${button}\")`);\n  installed = true;\n}"
}
```

Slice 3 — creating the tooltip component:

> TOOL

tool_use Write
id: toolu_01TzvYxjiKRybp6aS1M15nCd
```json
{
  "file_path": "/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/components/game/ItemTooltip.tsx",
  "content": "\"use client\";\n\nimport { type MouseEvent, useCallback, useState } from \"react\";\nimport { createPortal } from \"react-dom\";\n\nexport type TooltipContent = { title: string; lines?: string[] } | null;\n\n/**\n * Minecraft-style item tooltip: a near-black box with a violet gradient border\n * that follows the cursor (matching the game and sidestepping clipping inside\n * the scrollable inventory). It renders in a portal on document.body and is\n * pointer-events:none, so it can never intercept the canvas click that\n * re-acquires pointer lock. During locked play the cursor is hidden and slots\n * aren't hover-reachable, so the tooltip simply never shows.\n *\n * Usage: `const { tooltip, bind } = useItemTooltip();` then spread\n * `{...bind(content)}` onto each hoverable element and render `{tooltip}` once.\n * Pass `null` content for empty/non-hover targets to disable the tooltip there.\n * Keep each element's aria-label — the tooltip is aria-hidden.\n */\nexport function useItemTooltip() {\n  const [content, setContent] = useState<TooltipContent>(null);\n  const [pos, setPos] = useState({ x: 0, y: 0 });\n\n  const track = useCallback((e: MouseEvent) => setPos({ x: e.clientX, y: e.clientY }), []);\n\n  const bind = useCallback(\n    (c: TooltipContent) =>\n      c\n        ? {\n            onMouseEnter: (e: MouseEvent) => {\n              setContent(c);\n              setPos({ x: e.clientX, y: e.clientY });\n            },\n            onMouseMove: track,\n            onMouseLeave: () => setContent(null)\n          }\n        : {},\n    [track]\n  );\n\n  let tooltip: React.ReactNode = null;\n  if (content && typeof document !== \"undefined\") {\n    // Flip to the cursor's left near the right edge so wide tooltips stay on screen.\n    const flip = typeof window !== \"undefined\" && pos.x > window.innerWidth - 300;\n    const style = flip ? { right: window.innerWidth - pos.x + 14, top: pos.y + 14 } : { left: pos.x + 14, top: pos.y + 14 };\n    tooltip = createPortal(\n      <div className=\"item-tooltip\" style={style} aria-hidden>\n        <span className=\"item-tooltip-title\">{content.title}</span>\n        {content.lines?.map((line, i) => (\n          <span key={i} className=\"item-tooltip-line\">\n            {line}\n          </span>\n        ))}\n      </div>,\n      document.body\n    );\n  }\n\n  return { tooltip, bind };\n}\n"
}
```

```json
  "old_string": "export default function Hotbar({ inventory, selectedSlot, hotbarSlots, onSelectSlot }: HotbarProps) {\n  const visible = inventory.slice(0, hotbarSlots);\n  const selected = visible[selectedSlot];\n  const selectedLabel = selected?.id && selected.count > 0 ? selected.label : \"\";\n\n  return (\n    <div className=\"hotbar-area\">\n      {selectedLabel && (\n        <div key={`${selectedSlot}:${selectedLabel}`} className=\"hotbar-item-name\">\n          {selectedLabel}\n        </div>\n      )}\n      <div className=\"hotbar\" data-testid=\"hotbar\">\n        {visible.map((slot, idx) => (\n          <button\n            key={`hotbar-${idx}`}\n            className={idx === selectedSlot ? \"hotbar-slot active\" : \"hotbar-slot\"}\n            onClick={() => onSelectSlot(idx)}\n            aria-label={slot.id && slot.count > 0 ? `Slot ${idx + 1}: ${slot.label}` : `Slot ${idx + 1}: empty`}\n            title={slot.id && slot.count > 0 ? slot.label : undefined}\n          >\n            <ItemIcon slot={slot} size={32} />\n          </button>\n        ))}\n      </div>\n    </div>\n  );\n}",
  "new_string": "export default function Hotbar({ inventory, selectedSlot, hotbarSlots, onSelectSlot }: HotbarProps) {\n  const visible = inventory.slice(0, hotbarSlots);\n  const selected = visible[selectedSlot];\n  const selectedLabel = selected?.id && selected.count > 0 ? selected.label : \"\";\n  const { tooltip, bind } = useItemTooltip();\n\n  return (\n    <div className=\"hotbar-area\">\n      {selectedLabel && (\n        <div key={`${selectedSlot}:${selectedLabel}`} className=\"hotbar-item-name\">\n          {selectedLabel}\n        </div>\n      )}\n      <div className=\"hotbar\" data-testid=\"hotbar\">\n        {visible.map((slot, idx) => (\n          <button\n            key={`hotbar-${idx}`}\n            className={idx === selectedSlot ? \"hotbar-slot active\" : \"hotbar-slot\"}\n            onClick={() => onSelectSlot(idx)}\n            aria-label={slot.id && slot.count > 0 ? `Slot ${idx + 1}: ${slot.label}` : `Slot ${idx + 1}: empty`}\n            {...bind(slot.id && slot.count > 0 ? { title: slot.label } : null)}\n          >\n            <ItemIcon slot={slot} size={32} />\n          </button>\n        ))}\n      </div>\n      {tooltip}\n    </div>\n  );\n}"
}
```

```json
{
  "file_path": "/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/components/game/ItemTooltip.test.tsx",
  "content": "import { describe, expect, test } from \"bun:test\";\nimport { render, screen } from \"@testing-library/react\";\nimport userEvent from \"@testing-library/user-event\";\nimport { type TooltipContent, useItemTooltip } from \"@/components/game/ItemTooltip\";\n\nfunction Host({ content }: { content: TooltipContent }) {\n  const { tooltip, bind } = useItemTooltip();\n  return (\n    <div>\n      <button {...bind(content)}>target</button>\n      {tooltip}\n    </div>\n  );\n}\n\ndescribe(\"useItemTooltip\", () => {\n  test(\"shows the title and sub-lines on hover and hides on leave\", async () => {\n    const user = userEvent.setup();\n    render(<Host content={{ title: \"Wood Pickaxe\", lines: [\"Durability 59 / 59\"] }} />);\n\n    expect(screen.queryByText(\"Wood Pickaxe\")).toBeNull();\n\n    await user.hover(screen.getByRole(\"button\", { name: \"target\" }));\n    expect(screen.getByText(\"Wood Pickaxe\")).toBeTruthy();\n    expect(screen.getByText(\"Durability 59 / 59\")).toBeTruthy();\n    // The tooltip is decorative; the accessible name comes from the element itself.\n    expect(screen.getByText(\"Wood Pickaxe\").closest(\".item-tooltip\")?.getAttribute(\"aria-hidden\")).toBe(\"true\");\n\n    await user.unhover(screen.getByRole(\"button\", { name: \"target\" }));\n    expect(screen.queryByText(\"Wood Pickaxe\")).toBeNull();\n  });\n\n  test(\"renders no tooltip for null content (empty slot)\", async () => {\n    const user = userEvent.setup();\n    render(<Host content={null} />);\n    await user.hover(screen.getByRole(\"button\", { name: \"target\" }));\n    expect(document.querySelector(\".item-tooltip\")).toBeNull();\n  });\n});\n"
}
```

```json
{

}
```

```
1	# Changelog
2	
3	All notable changes to this project are documented in this file.
4	
5	## [Unreleased]
6	
7	### Added
8	
9	- **Pickup & status toasts**: because mob loot drops straight into inventory storage (no ground item), kills could feel like they paid out nothing. A kill now shows a brief on-screen toast just above the hotbar (e.g. "+2 Wool, +1 Raw Mutton"). The same in-game toast also surfaces the sleep-denied messages ("You can only sleep at night" / "Monsters are nearby"), which previously only rendered inside the pause menu and so were invisible during play
10	- **Animal breeding**: right-click a sheep or horse with wheat (or a chicken with seeds) to put it "in love"; two in-love adults of the same kind standing close together spawn a baby that follows the parents, can't be farmed for drops, and grows to full size after ~90 seconds. This makes mob drops renewable, closing the loop back to combat loot. The passive population is capped (24) and feeding costs crops, so it stays bounded
11	  - New `MobState.fedTimer`/`ageTimer`, a `breeding` system ticked after mob AI, and a shared `findAimedMobIndex` so feeding and attacking use the same "what's in my crosshair" rule. Feeding joins the right-click precedence ahead of block interaction. New fed/bred sounds; babies render at 55% scale. Breeding state is session-only (mobs are never saved). No save-format or worldgen impact
12	- **Furnace & cooking**: craft a furnace (8 cobble) and right-click it to open the crafting panel in furnace mode, which unlocks smelting recipes — raw chicken or mutton + 1 planks (the fuel) cook into the cooked version, restoring 8 hunger versus 3 raw. Smelting recipes show as locked ("Requires Furnace") until a furnace is open
13	  - Reuses the Phase 2 interact system and the existing crafting panel — no separate furnace UI. Recipes gained an optional `station` field; the gate is enforced engine-side in the `craft` command (UI gating alone is spoofable). New `Furnace` block with a glowing-mouth atlas tile, `cooked_chicken`/`cooked_mutton` items, and a smelt sound. No save-format or worldgen impact
14	- **Farming & food**: craft a wood hoe (2 planks + 1 wood), right-click grass or dirt to till it into farmland, then right-click farmland with seeds to plant wheat. Crops grow through four stages over ~2.5 minutes and, when mature, harvest into wheat plus 1–2 seeds; an immature crop just returns its seed. Craft 3 wheat into bread (restores 6 hunger)
15	  - Seeds come from breaking grass (20% chance per block) — `addBlockDrop` now rolls a per-block `rollBlockDrops` table (`lib/game/items.ts`) instead of a single fixed drop
16	  - New **random-tick system** (`lib/game/engine/systems/randomTicks.ts`): each interval samples columns near the player and runs per-block handlers — the extensible basis for crop growth (and future saplings / grass spread). Crops are solid full-cube blocks (so they can be targeted and harvested) with each growth stage its own `BlockId`, which means they persist through the existing block-diff save with **no save-format change**
17	  - New `Farmland` + `WheatStage0..3` blocks, `wood_hoe`/`seeds`/`wheat`/`bread` items with generated sprites, and till/plant sounds. The right-click "use held item" step joins the interact precedence (after block interaction, before placement). No worldgen changes
18	- **Beds & sleeping**: craft a bed (3 wool + 3 planks), place it, and right-click it at night to skip to morning — the screen fades to black, the day clock jumps to a fresh dawn, and the bed becomes your respawn point (respawn falls back to a random land point if the bed is gone). Sleeping is refused during the day or with a hostile within 12 blocks, with an on-screen reason
19	  - New **right-click interact system** (`lib/game/engine/systems/interact.ts`): a fixed precedence in the `placeBlock` command runs block interaction before placement, so interactive blocks (beds now; furnaces later) take the click instead of getting a block placed on them. New `Bed` block + `bed` item, synthesized sleep/wake sounds, and a `SleepOverlay` fade component
20	  - **Save format v3** (additive — `SAVE_KEY` unchanged, v1/v2 saves still load): time of day (`dayClock`), `hearts`, `hunger`, and the bed `spawnPoint` now persist. Previously all four reset on reload, so sleeping through the night wouldn't have survived a reload. `readSave` chains the migrations v1 → v2 → v3
21	- **Mob loot & drops**: mobs now drop kind-specific items when they die instead of the old flat "cobble for hostiles, food for everything else" rule. Sheep drop wool + raw mutton, chickens feather + raw chicken, horses leather, zombies rotten flesh, skeletons bone, spiders string — counts are randomized per the engine's RNG (`lib/game/mobLoot.ts`). A new `mobDied` engine event plays a synthesized death thud
22	  - New items: `wool`, `feather`, `bone`, `leather`, `string` (crafting materials) and `rotten_flesh`, `raw_chicken`, `raw_mutton` (edible food), each with a generated 16×16 sprite (zero-asset)
23	  - **Per-food hunger**: food now restores a value carried on the item itself rather than one global constant. The generic `food` item still restores 7; raw meats restore 3, rotten flesh 2. New `food` and `material` item kinds; the legacy `food` item is reinterpreted (old saves load unchanged — saves store only item id + count)
24	  - New recipe: 4 string → 1 wool, so spider drops feed the wool supply (and, later, beds)
25	  - No save-format or worldgen impact
26	  - Selection recolors the body live (material color swap, no rebuild) and persists as a player preference under its own localStorage key (`minecraft_skin_v1`), separate from the world save — it survives world resets. No save-format or worldgen impact
27	- **Camera view toggle (V)**: cycles first-person → third-person rear → third-person front, like Minecraft's F5 (V instead, because F5 reloads the page in browsers)
28	  - New humanoid player body (head, torso, two arms, two legs) in the zero-asset box-mesh style, visible only in third person — walk gait scaled by speed, a chop animation on attacks and while mining, the look pitch on the head, and the held hotbar item rendered in the right hand via the shared item-model builder
29	  - The third-person camera boom raycasts against terrain and clamps so walls never occlude the player; the front view flips the heading and inverts the tilt while mouse control of the player stays unchanged
30	  - Gameplay is intentionally eye-relative in every mode (mining/placing reach, combat aim, audio panning are unaffected); the crosshair stays centered, matching Minecraft
31	  - The view mode is session-only (resets to first-person on reload). No save-format or worldgen impact
32	
33	### Documentation
34	
35	- **Added `docs/manual.md`**, a player-facing guide: getting started / your first day, the full control map, survival (health, hunger gates, combat, armor, death), the day-night thresholds, mining with tool-tier gating, crafting and smelting, the mob roster and breeding, farming, beds and sleeping, the procedural audio, and saving. Linked from the README and AGENTS.md
36	- **Added `docs/reference.md`**, scannable tables cross-checked against the code: all 31 recipes, the 26 block types (hardness + tool gate), the 6 mobs (stats + drop ranges), and item stats for tools, weapons, armor, and food
37	- **Added `docs/tuning.md`**, a contributor balance guide that groups the `config.ts` tunables by gameplay effect (player feel, survival pressure, danger, progression, farming/breeding, persistence/rendering), names the system that reads each, and flags the save-sensitive ones (`SAVE_KEY`, inventory layout)
38	- **Refreshed `docs/architecture.md`** to match the shipped engine: the per-frame step order now lists the random-tick (crop growth) and animal-breeding systems, the right-click precedence documents the full feed → interact → use-held → place chain, the mob/block drop tables (`rollMobDrops`/`rollBlockDrops`) are described, and the `window.__monecraft` debug handle the Playwright `e2e/` suite drives through is noted
39	
40	## [0.5.0] - 2026-06-13
```

```
6:React UI (components/)          declarative HUD/panels; reads snapshots, sends commands
18:## React shell (`lib/game/useMinecraftGame.ts`, `components/`)
22:- UI intents (`craft`, `swapSlots`, `selectSlot`, …) are dispatched as engine `Command`s. The only React state in the shell is pure UI concern: pointer lock, transient save messages, renderer failure.
24:- On mount the shell installs a `window.__monecraft` debug handle (`{ engine, renderer, input, audio }`) and deletes it on unmount. The Playwright suite in `e2e/` drives the game through this handle — asserting on engine state and `renderer.renderedTriangles()` rather than pixels (see [testing.md](testing.md)); it's also handy from the browser console.
25:- UI pixel art is procedural: `lib/ui/` generates 16×16 item/HUD sprites as pure pixel buffers (`spritePixels.ts`, `hudPixels.ts` — DOM-free, unit-tested) wrapped by a cached canvas→data-URL layer (`sprites.ts`, falls back to a transparent pixel under happy-dom) plus noise tiles installed as CSS vars (`chromeTiles.ts`). No image assets, no licensing exposure. `lib/ui/` must not import Three.js or the engine.
59:- `heldItem.ts` / `crackOverlay.ts` — first-person item model and the 8-stage mining crack box (stage = progress / hardness). Held blocks stay cubes; everything else is the item's 16×16 inventory sprite extruded into a pixel-thick voxel mesh (`extrudedSprite.ts`, vertex colors, silhouette-only side faces) — the render layer imports `lib/ui/spritePixels` for this, which is legal because spritePixels is pure pixel-buffer code with no DOM. The holder group is posed every frame by `heldItemPose.ts` (pure math): a one-shot swing on the `attackSwung` event (the shell calls `renderer.triggerSwing()` from the event drain), a looping swing while mining, a walk bob scaled by horizontal speed, an equip dip on slot switch, and a faint idle sway.
61:- **Skins**: the body is colored by a `PlayerPalette` from `lib/game/playerSkins.ts` (six presets); `GameRenderer.setPlayerSkin(palette)` recolors the named materials in place. The choice persists under its own localStorage key (`lib/game/skinSettings.ts`, `minecraft_skin_v1`) — a player preference like audio volumes, never part of the world save — and the pause-menu picker's bust portraits (`lib/ui/skinPortrait.ts`) derive from the same palettes.
66:All sound is procedural — no audio files, mirroring the sprite system. SFX are synthesized by [ZZFX](https://github.com/KilledByAPixel/ZzFX) parameter arrays; music is a generative ambient pad over raw WebAudio nodes.
68:- `audioDirector.ts` — the shell-side observer, exactly parallel to the renderer: `handleEvent(GameEvent)` plays one-shots (block break/place by material, hurt/eat/jump/land, mob attacks, death/respawn stingers) and `sync(state, dt)` drives everything continuous (footsteps, mining hit ticks, mob ambience, music mood). Music follows `state.daylight` immediately and the biome with 5 s hysteresis, and ducks while paused. The engine never imports this module.
```

```
18	## React shell (`lib/game/useMinecraftGame.ts`, `components/`)
19	
20	- `useMinecraftGame` creates the `GameEngine` in the canvas mount's callback ref, then an effect builds the `GameRenderer`, the `AudioDirector`, and `inputController` and drives the `requestAnimationFrame` loop: `engine.step(dt, input)` (in bounded catch-up substeps of ≤50 ms, so slow frames — e.g. software GL — don't run the simulation in slow motion) → drain engine events (death/respawn handling, `attackSwung` → `renderer.triggerSwing()`, + `audio.handleEvent`) → `minimap.sync(state)` → `renderer.sync(state)` → `audio.sync(state, dt)` → `renderer.render()`. The minimap must sync **before** the renderer because it reads `state.worldMeshDirty`, which `renderer.sync` clears.
21	- UI state arrives as immutable `GameSnapshot`s via `useSyncExternalStore`; the engine replaces the snapshot object only when a visible value changes, so React re-renders are minimal and identity-driven.
22	- UI intents (`craft`, `swapSlots`, `selectSlot`, …) are dispatched as engine `Command`s. The only React state in the shell is pure UI concern: pointer lock, transient save messages, renderer failure.
23	- WebGL init failure is surfaced as `rendererError` and rendered as a fallback panel instead of crashing.
24	- On mount the shell installs a `window.__monecraft` debug handle (`{ engine, renderer, input, audio }`) and deletes it on unmount. The Playwright suite in `e2e/` drives the game through this handle — asserting on engine state and `renderer.renderedTriangles()` rather than pixels (see [testing.md](testing.md)); it's also handy from the browser console.
25	- UI pixel art is procedural: `lib/ui/` generates 16×16 item/HUD sprites as pure pixel buffers (`spritePixels.ts`, `hudPixels.ts` — DOM-free, unit-tested) wrapped by a cached canvas→data-URL layer (`sprites.ts`, falls back to a transparent pixel under happy-dom) plus noise tiles installed as CSS vars (`chromeTiles.ts`). No image assets, no licensing exposure. `lib/ui/` must not import Three.js or the engine.
26	- Note for the React Compiler lint rules: consume the hook with destructuring (`const { … } = useMinecraftGame()`); property access on the result object can false-positive `react-hooks/refs`.
```

Done. All six slices are implemented, verified, and committed to `feat/mc-ui-polish` — working tree clean, nothing pushed. Here's the branch:

```
f7cf539 docs: record the UI polish pass and the font asset exception
7972f54 feat(ui): add tasteful UI motion with reduced-motion support
8e30438 feat(ui): dim the world behind menus and add a screen vignette
cd943a4 feat(ui): replace native title tooltips with Minecraft item tooltips

```

## What changed

- **Pixel font** — UI text now uses Pixelify Sans via `next/font/google` (self-hosted at build), exposed as `--mc-font`; monospace kept as fallback and on the F3 readout. This is the one deliberate exception to the zero-binary rule (recorded in AGENTS.md so it doesn't get reverted).
- **Textured GUI chrome** — a new `grayGrainTileUrl` generator gives panels, slots, recipe entries, and buttons a faint generated grain (`--mc-tile-panel/well/button`), layered over the existing flat colors so bevels and SSR fallback are untouched.
- **Item tooltips** — a `useItemTooltip` hook renders a cursor-following, violet-bordered, near-black tooltip (portaled, `pointer-events:none`, `aria-hidden`), replacing native `title=`. Durable items show "Durability x / y"; locked recipes show "Requires <station>".
- **Depth** — a shared `.menu-backdrop` dims the frozen world behind inventory/pause (no more double-dim), plus an always-on screen vignette.
- **Motion** — inventory pop-in, pause fade-in, slot/button hover & press feedback, hotbar name rise — all gated by `prefers-reduced-motion`.

## Verification

`lint`, `typecheck`, `format:check`, and all **312 tests** pass (I added `ItemTooltip.test.tsx` and converted the inventory durability assertion to a hover-tooltip check). The production **build succeeds** with network available.

⚠️ One caveat: `bun run build` intermittently failed in this sandbox with *"Failed to fetch Pixelify Sans from Google Fonts"* — `next/font` fetches at build time and the sandbox sometimes blocks egress. It built fine when I allowed network, and your CI has network, so it'll pass there.

One thing I **couldn't** do here is the manual gameplay pass that AGENTS.md asks for on visual/input changes. Before you push, it's worth a quick `bun run dev` to eyeball: open inventory (`I`) → backdrop dims, panel pops in, slots show grain, hover items → tooltip follows cursor; open pause (`Esc`) → fade-in, buttons press; and confirm clicking the canvas still grabs pointer lock and no tooltip shows during play.

Want me to tweak any of the specifics — e.g. font choice (Silkscreen/VT323 instead), vignette strength (`--mc-vignette`, currently `0.28`), or the grain intensity?

> DEVELOPER

what do you means of font choice (Silkscreen/VT323 instead)? replace google fonts?

> AGENT

Good question — to be clear: **all three are Google Fonts**, loaded the exact same way (`next/font/google`, self-hosted at build). Switching between them is a one-line change in `app/fonts.ts` (the import name + the loader call) — it does *not* change the loading mechanism, and it stays just as "zero runtime request" as now. So "Silkscreen/VT323 instead" just means *which typeface* to pick, not replacing the Google Fonts approach.

How they differ visually:

- **Pixelify Sans** (current) — a pixel font with slightly rounded, open letterforms. Good x-height, so it stays readable down to the ~10px HUD sizes. Reads as "pixel/blocky" without being hard to parse.
- **Silkscreen** — a true tiny bitmap font, very square and condensed; the most "retro pixel" of the three, but it muddies below ~12px and leans uppercase, so small labels (durability lines, controls list) get harder to read.
- **VT323** — a monospace CRT/terminal font. Looks like old DOS text — retro, but reads more "computer terminal" than "Minecraft."

One thing worth knowing: **the actual Minecraft typeface is *not* on Google Fonts.** If you want the literal Minecraft look, the closest open-source clone is **Monocraft**, which would mean self-hosting a font file via `next/font/local` instead of `next/font/google` (still self-hosted/no runtime fetch, just a committed `.woff2` rather than a build-time download). That's the most authentic option but adds an actual font file to the repo.

So your options are roughly:

| Choice | Loader | Authenticity | Trade-off |
|---|---|---|---|
| Pixelify Sans (current) | `next/font/google` | Good pixel feel | Not the literal MC font |
| Silkscreen | `next/font/google` | Very pixel/retro | Muddy at small sizes |
| VT323 | `next/font/google` | Retro terminal | Reads "DOS," not "MC" |
| Monocraft | `next/font/local` (committed `.woff2`) | Closest to real Minecraft | Adds a font file to the repo |

Want me to switch to any of these? My recommendation stays Pixelify Sans for legibility at HUD sizes, but if you want maximum authenticity and don't mind one committed font file, Monocraft is the truest match.

> DEVELOPER

I think Monocraft is better choice, what do you think?

> AGENT

I agree — Monocraft is the most authentic per-glyph match to the real Minecraft typeface, and it's under the SIL Open Font License, so it's safe to bundle. The trade-off is one committed font file (and switching from `next/font/google` → `next/font/local`), which you're fine with.

A couple of implementation choices I'll make:
- Use the **no-ligatures** variant, so programming ligatures don't silently rewrite UI text (e.g. recipe labels like `"2 Wood -> 4 Planks"`).
- Ship a **woff2** if I can produce one (smaller); otherwise commit the `.ttf`.
- Include the OFL license file alongside it.

Done — switched to **Monocraft**, the open-source font modeled on Minecraft's actual typeface. Here's where the branch stands (7 commits, nothing pushed):

```
2eb78c3 feat(ui): switch UI font from Pixelify Sans to Monocraft (self-hosted)
f7cf539 docs: record the UI polish pass and the font asset exception
7972f54 feat(ui): add tasteful UI motion with reduced-motion support
8e30438 feat(ui): dim the world behind menus and add a screen vignette
cd943a4 feat(ui): replace native title tooltips with Minecraft item tooltips
88cd6a4 feat(ui): texture GUI panels, slots, and buttons with generated grain
1a4d287 feat(ui): add Pixelify Sans pixel font for authentic Minecraft text
```

What I did for the swap:
- **Self-hosted via `next/font/local`** — committed a **36KB woff2** (converted from the official v4.2.1 release TTF with `bunx ttf2woff2`, no permanent install) at `app/fonts/Monocraft.woff2`, replacing the `next/font/google` loader.
- **Bundled the license** — `app/fonts/OFL.txt` (SIL Open Font License 1.1, which permits bundling).
- **Disabled coding ligatures** in `base.css` so UI text stays literal (e.g. `"2 Wood -> 4 Planks"` doesn't get rewritten to `→`).
- **Updated AGENTS.md / architecture.md / CHANGELOG** to name Monocraft as the one sanctioned font exception.

**Bonus fix:** because the font is now local, the production build no longer fetches from Google at build time — `bun run build` succeeds **with no network** now, eliminating the intermittent "Failed to fetch" failure I hit earlier. Full verify is green: lint, typecheck, format:check, all **312 tests**, and the offline build.

Two notes:
1. I still recommend a quick `bun run dev` gameplay pass to eyeball Monocraft at the small HUD sizes (it's monospaced, so long labels sit a touch wider than Pixelify Sans did) — purely a look check.
2. The history has commit 1 ("add Pixelify Sans") and commit 7 ("switch to Monocraft"). It's honest, but if you'd prefer a clean single font commit for the PR, I can squash those two into one ("add Monocraft font") — say the word and I'll do it safely before you push.

```json
{
  "command": "gh pr create --repo hutusi/monecraft --base main --head feat/mc-ui-polish --title \"feat(ui): authentic Minecraft UI polish — font, textures, tooltips, depth, motion\" --body \"$(cat <<'EOF'\nPolishes the UI to read more authentically like real Minecraft, across five fronts. Everything stays procedural except one deliberately-added font.\n\n## What changed\n\n- **Pixel font (Monocraft).** UI text moves off the generic monospace stack to **Monocraft**, an open-source face modeled on the Minecraft typeface. Self-hosted via `next/font/local` from a committed 36 KB woff2 in `app/fonts/` (SIL OFL 1.1, license bundled). Coding ligatures are disabled so UI text stays literal. The monospace stack remains the fallback and stays pinned on the F3 debug readout. This is the one sanctioned exception to the zero-binary-asset rule (recorded in `AGENTS.md`).\n- **Textured GUI chrome.** Panels, inventory slots, recipe entries, and buttons get a faint generated grain instead of flat fills — a new `grayGrainTileUrl` generator installs `--mc-tile-panel/well/button` as CSS vars, layered over the existing flat colors so bevels and SSR fallback are untouched.\n- **Item tooltips.** A dependency-free `useItemTooltip` hook renders a cursor-following, violet-bordered, near-black tooltip (portaled, `pointer-events:none`, `aria-hidden`), replacing native `title=` on the hotbar, inventory, and recipe entries. Durable items show \"Durability x / y\"; locked recipes show \"Requires <station>\". Accessible names (the `aria-label`s) are unchanged.\n- **Depth.** A shared `.menu-backdrop` dims the frozen world behind the inventory and pause menu (the dim moved off `.pause-overlay` so it no longer double-darkens), plus an always-on screen-edge `.vignette` over the 3D view (tunable via `--mc-vignette`).\n- **Motion.** Inventory pop-in, pause fade-in, slot/button hover & press feedback, and a small rise on the hotbar item-name pop — all disabled under `prefers-reduced-motion`.\n\nNo save-format or worldgen impact.\n\n## Verification\n\n- `bun run lint`, `bun run typecheck`, `bun run format:check`, and `bun test` (**312 pass**) all green.\n- `bun run build` green. Because the font is now local, the build no longer fetches from Google at build time (the build is fully offline-capable).\n- Added `components/game/ItemTooltip.test.tsx`; the two `title=` assertions removed from `InventoryPanel.test.tsx` are now covered by a hover-tooltip check and the existing disabled-state check.\n\n## Notes for review\n\n- Monocraft is monospaced, so long labels sit a touch wider than a proportional font would — worth a quick `bun run dev` look at the HUD sizes.\n- Manual gameplay pass recommended (open inventory/pause, hover items, confirm pointer-lock is unaffected) since this touches the React shell and visuals.\nEOF\n)\" 2>&1 | tail -5",

}
```

> AGENT

Found it. Two things:

1. **The e2e failure is a real regression I introduced.** `e2e/smoke.e2e.ts:47` locates inventory slots by the `title` attribute (`.inv-slot[title="Planks"]`) — which I removed when replacing native tooltips. The `verify` job passed but e2e broke because it depends on `title=`.
2. **CodeRabbit** flagged: (Major) hotbar tooltips dropped the durability line that inventory tooltips show — inconsistent; and (Minor) prefer explicit `aria-hidden="true"`.

Let me read the e2e test to fix it properly, and check the full CodeRabbit comment for the hotbar fix.

```
3	test("boots without errors and renders the world", async ({ gamePage: page }) => {
4	  await expect(page.locator(".game-canvas-wrap canvas")).toBeVisible();
5	  await expect(page.getByTestId("hotbar")).toBeVisible();
6	  const triangles = await page.evaluate(() => window.__monecraft!.renderer.renderedTriangles());

53	test("holding the mouse mines the block underfoot", async ({ gamePage: page }) => {
54	  await calmDaytime(page);
55	  await acquirePointerLock(page);
56	  await page.waitForTimeout(1000); // settle (slow CI renderers need the margin)
57	
58	  // Aim straight down from the center of the cell (a ray origin exactly on a

62	    player.pitch = -Math.PI / 2 + 0.02;

156	});
```

_⚠️ Potential issue_ | _🟠 Major_ | _⚡ Quick win_

**Hotbar tooltip drops durability details for damageable items.**

Hotbar now only passes `{ title }`, while inventory tooltips include durability lines. This creates inconsistent item feedback between two primary surfaces.

<details>
<summary>Suggested patch</summary>

```diff
-            {...bind(slot.id && slot.count > 0 ? { title: slot.label } : null)}
+            {...bind(
+              slot.id && slot.count > 0
+                ? slot.maxDurability
+                  ? {
+                      title: slot.label,
+                      lines: [`Durability ${slot.durability ?? slot.maxDurability} / ${slot.maxDurability}`]
+                    }
+                  : { title: slot.label }
+                : null
+            )}
```
</details>

<details>
<summary>🤖 Prompt for AI Agents</summary>

```

rest with a brief reason, keep changes minimal, and validate.

In `@components/game/Hotbar.tsx` at line 38, The Hotbar tooltip currently calls
bind(...) with only { title: slot.label } when slot.id and slot.count > 0, which
drops durability/details; update the bind call in Hotbar.tsx so it uses the same
tooltip content/formatter used by the inventory tooltip (instead of only
slot.label) — e.g., replace the title-only payload passed to bind(...) for the
active slot with the full tooltip string/lines produced by your inventory
tooltip builder (the same function that includes durability), so bind(slot.id &&
slot.count > 0 ? { title: <inventory tooltip for slot> } : null) supplies the
complete tooltip for slot (refer to the inventory tooltip builder to copy/invoke
```

Empty slots get no tooltip.\n */\nexport function itemTooltipFor(slot: InventorySlot): TooltipContent {\n  if (!slot.id || slot.count <= 0) return null;\n  if (slot.maxDurability) return { title: slot.label, lines: [`Durability ${slot.durability ?? slot.maxDurability} / ${slot.maxDurability}`] };\n  return { title: slot.label };\n}"
```json
}
```

```
import { defineConfig, devices } from "@playwright/test";

/**
 * E2E smoke tests run against the production build (next start) — the dev
 * server's React StrictMode double-mounts the game engine and would make
 * runs slower and noisier.
 *
 * Files are named *.e2e.ts (NOT *.test.ts / *.spec.ts) so `bun test` does not
 * try to execute them with its own runner.
 */
export default defineConfig({
  testDir: "e2e",
  testMatch: "**/*.e2e.ts",
  fullyParallel: false,
  forbidOnly: !!process.env.CI,
  retries: process.env.CI ? 2 : 0,
  workers: 1,
  reporter: process.env.CI ? [["github"], ["html", { open: "never" }]] : "list",
  timeout: 60000,
  use: {
    baseURL: "http://localhost:3000",
    trace: "retain-on-failure"
  },
  // channel "chromium" runs the full browser in new-headless mode: the default
  // headless shell rejects requestPointerLock (WrongDocumentError).
  projects: [{ name: "chromium", use: { ...devices["Desktop Chrome"], channel: "chromium" } }],
  webServer: {
    command: "bun run build && bun run start",
    url: "http://localhost:3000",
    reuseExistingServer: !process.env.CI,
    timeout: 180000
  }
});
```

Here's where PR #14 stands after my review.

## CI results
| Check | Status |
|---|---|
| `verify` (lint/typecheck/format/test/build) | ✅ pass |
| CodeRabbit | ✅ pass (review posted) |
| `e2e` (Playwright) | ❌ **was failing** — now fixed |

## What was wrong & what I fixed (committed locally, not pushed)

**1. e2e regression (the failure) — my bug.** `e2e/smoke.e2e.ts:47` located the planks slot by `.inv-slot[title="Planks"]` — the exact `title` attribute I removed when swapping to custom tooltips. The `verify` job doesn't run e2e, so it slipped through. Fixed by selecting via the slot's `aria-label` (`"Slot N: Planks"`); the assertion is otherwise unchanged.

**2. CodeRabbit — Major:** hotbar tooltips dropped the durability line that inventory tooltips show. Fixed by extracting a shared `itemTooltipFor(slot)` builder into `ItemTooltip.tsx` and using it on both surfaces, so a damageable item reads identically everywhere (also DRYs the two copies).

**3. CodeRabbit — Minor:** made `aria-hidden="true"` explicit on the vignette and tooltip nodes.

lint, typecheck, format:check, **312 unit tests**, and the **full 9-test Playwright e2e suite** all pass — including the previously-failing crafting test. Committed as `c94021a`.

```
## Comprehensive Gameplay Content & Systems Inventory

I've thoroughly explored the codebase. Here's a complete picture of what exists:

### 1. ENGINE SYSTEMS (Execution Order)
Located in `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/engine/systems/`:

**Per-frame tick order** (from `GameEngine.step()` lines 180–190):
1. **playerMotion.ts** — Walk/sprint/crouch/jump, gravity, collision, fall damage, void damage, hunger drain tracking
2. **playerStats.ts** — Hunger drain applied (sprint/walk/jump), health regeneration (needs food)
3. **mining.ts** — Mine block progression, block breaking, item drops
4. **dayNight.ts** — 240-second day cycle (sin-based), daylight 0.04–1.0, triggers hostile behavior thresholds
5. **randomTicks.ts** — Crop growth (wheat stages 0→3), fires on interval from player location
6. **spawnDirector.ts** — Hostile spawning at night (capped at 16), initial mob spawn on world load
7. **mobAI.ts** — Mob movement, detection, aggression, attacks, line-of-sight checks, day/night behavior
8. **breeding.ts** — Love timers, baby aging, passive breeding (capped at 24)

**Separate loops:**
- **playerLife.ts** — Damage intake with armor mitigation, respawn timer (3 sec), death flag
- **interact.ts** — Right-click: feed animals, sleep in beds (night-only, sets respawn), open furnace, till soil (hoe), plant seeds

---

### 2. BLOCKS (28 types)
Source: `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/world/blocks.ts` (BlockId enum)

**Terrain (8)**
- Grass, Dirt, Stone, Bedrock (indestructible), Sand, Snow, Water (visual only), Cactus

**Ores (5) — mining tier-gated**
- SliverOre (tier 2), RubyOre (tier 3), GoldOre (tier 3), SapphireOre (tier 4), DiamondOre (tier 4)

**Crafting/Building (7)**
- Wood (logs), Leaves (drops dirt), Planks, Cobblestone, Brick, Glass, Air (placeholder)

**Functional/Interactive (3)**
- Bed (sleep, set spawn), Furnace (smelt recipes), Farmland (tilled soil for crops)

**Crops (4) — consecutive IDs for +1 growth**
- WheatStage0, WheatStage1, WheatStage2, WheatStage3 (mature, harvestable)

**Drop behavior:** Most blocks drop themselves; Grass drops seeds at 20% chance; mature wheat (Stage3) drops wheat + 1–2 seeds; Farmland reverts to Dirt; immature wheat drops seeds.

---

### 3. ITEMS & TOOLS
Source: `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/items.ts` (ITEM_DEFS, 46 items)

**Items by kind:**

**Tools (9) — mining-tier progression, durability, minePower**
- Wood Pickaxe (tier 1, power 1.05, 70 durability) → Stone (tier 2, 1.55, 140) → Sliver (tier 3, 2.2, 240) → Ruby (tier 4, 2.8, 340) → Sapphire (tier 5, 3.3, 430) → Gold (tier 6, 3.8, 520) → Diamond (tier 7, 4.4, 700)
- Wood Hoe (durability 90, tier 0, for tilling; no mining power)
- Knife (9 attack, 50 durability) — early weapon

**Weapons (8) — attack damage, durability**
- Wood Sword (13, 80) → Stone (18, 160) → Sliver (24, 260) → Ruby (31, 360) → Sapphire (35, 450) → Gold (40, 540) → Diamond (47, 720)

**Armor (6) — equipped items, defense points, durability**
- Helmet (defense 2, 260 durability)
- Face Mask (defense 2, 220)
- Neck Protection (defense 2, 230)
- Chestplate (defense 4, 420)
- Leggings (defense 3, 340)
- Boots (defense 2, 250)
- **Armor system:** Defense reduces damage by 5% per point, capped at 75% mitigation; armor durability consumed on hit (1 per damage event)

**Food (6) — restore hunger stat**
- Food generic (7), Bread (6), Rotten Flesh (2)
- Raw Chicken (3) / Cooked Chicken (8)
- Raw Mutton (3) / Cooked Mutton (8)

**Materials/Mob drops (7) — crafting ingredients, no direct use**
- Wool, Feather, Bone, Leather, String, Wheat, Seeds

**Blocks (18) — placeable as items**
- All blocks with visuals: Grass, Dirt, Stone, Wood, Planks, Cobblestone, Sand, Brick, Glass, all Ores, Snow, Cactus, Bed, Furnace

**Initial inventory:** Starter gear includes 64× Grass/Dirt/Stone/Wood, 20× Planks/Cobble/Sand, 1× Wood Pickaxe, 1× Knife (rest slots empty).

---

### 4. RECIPES & CRAFTING (31 recipes)
Source: `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/recipes.ts`

**Crafting grid recipes (no station required, 25 recipes):**
- **Wood processing:** 2 Wood → 4 Planks
- **Bed:** 3 Wool + 3 Planks → 1 Bed (sleep & respawn)
- **Hoe:** 2 Planks + 1 Wood → Wood Hoe
- **Furnace:** 8 Cobble → 1 Furnace (unlocks smelting)
- **Tools:** 7 pickaxe recipes (Wood through Diamond), each: 2 Ore + 1 Wood
- **Weapons:** 8 sword recipes, same progression pattern
- **Armor:** 6 armor pieces, mixed ore costs (e.g., Helmet: 4 Sapphire + 1 Ruby)
- **Building:** Glass (4 Sand → 2), Brick (2 Dirt + 2 Stone → 2)
- **Food:** Bread (3 Wheat → 1), Wool (4 String → 1)

**Furnace recipes (station-gated, 2 recipes):**
- Raw Chicken + Planks → Cooked Chicken
- Raw Mutton + Planks → Cooked Mutton

**Progression implication:** Starter tools → stone tools (enables ore mining) → rare ore tools (progression gates combat/armor), ending in diamond tier. Furnace unlocked mid-game for meat cooking.

---

### 5. MOBS (6 kinds)
Source: `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/mobs.ts`, `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/mobLoot.ts`

**Passive (breedable, no aggro, farmable):**
- **Sheep** — speed 0.9, hp 10, 20% spawn; drops: 1–2 Wool + 1 Raw Mutton
- **Chicken** — speed 1.2, hp 7, 14% spawn; drops: 0–2 Feather + 1 Raw Chicken
- **Horse** — speed 1.4, hp 14, 10% spawn; drops: 1–2 Leather (no meat)

**Hostile (aggro at night, burn in daylight, respawn at night):**
- **Zombie** — speed 1.05, hp 10, detect range 11, attack damage 3, cooldown 1.35s; drops: 1–2 Rotten Flesh
- **Skeleton** — speed 1.08, hp 9, detect range 12, attack damage 3, cooldown 1.4s; drops: 1–2 Bone
- **Spider** — speed 1.2, hp 8, detect range 10, attack damage 2, cooldown 1.1s; drops: 0–2 String; **nocturnal except daylight < 0.42** (active longer at dusk/dawn)

**AI behaviors:**
- Passives flee (walk away) if player < 4.2 blocks away, turn randomly otherwise
- Hostiles pursue if player < detect range, lose sight > 4 blocks + > 1.6 blocks vertical, have line-of-sight checks
- Attack cooldown enforces melee rhythm; knockback applies (0.75 units + 0.12 up)
- Zombies/Skeletons burn when daylight > 0.72, lose 0.8 hp/sec

**Breeding:**
- Adult passives fed correct item (Wheat for sheep/horses, Seeds for chickens) enter "love" window (30 sec)
- Two fed same-kind adults within 3 blocks pair every 0.5 sec, spawn baby at midpoint
- Babies (55% scale) grow for 90 sec, capped at 24 passives total
- Babies drop no loot until fully grown

**Spawning:**
- Initial spawn on world load: 6 sheep, 5 chickens, 3 horses (wider radius); 8 zombies, 6 skeletons, 6 spiders (tight radius)
- Nightly respawn: 1–2 random hostile types every 10 sec, at night only, < 16 cap, ~26+ blocks from player

---

### 6. SURVIVAL MECHANICS
Source: `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/engine/systems/playerLife.ts`, `playerStats.ts`, `playerMotion.ts`

**Health:**
- Max 20 hearts (full bar)
- Regenerates 0.5 hearts every 3 sec while alive, hurt, fed ≥ 12 hunger (half bar)
- Damage sources:
  - Mob melee attacks: 3 damage (zombie/skeleton), 2 (spider), mitigated by armor
  - Fall: 0.5 hearts per 1 unit velocity above 13 m/s fall speed (threshold ~8 blocks)
  - Void: 1 heart every 0.4 sec below y=-4
  - Armor reduces damage by 5% per defense point, capped at 75% mitigation
  - Minimum 1 damage always gets through (armor doesn't nullify)
- Death: respawn in 3 sec at spawn point (bed or random land), restore full 20 hearts + hunger

**Hunger:**
- Max 20 (full bar)
- Drain rate:
  - Sprint: 1 per 100 blocks
  - Walk: 1 per 300 blocks
  - Jump: 1 per 50 jumps
- Food restores: generic Food (7), Bread (6), Rotten Flesh (2), Raw Chicken/Mutton (3), Cooked versions (8)
- Speed penalty: hunger < max shrinks movement speed from 100% to 62% (linear scaling)
- Sprint lock: hunger ≤ 6 blocks sprinting (Shift while moving forward); hunger 6–20 allows it
- Health regen lock: hunger < 12 prevents regeneration

**Sleep/Beds:**
- Right-click bed only at night (daylight < 0.28) with no hostiles within 12 blocks
- Sets spawn point (respawn location)
- Skips to next morning (day phase ~0.07), 1.5 sec fade-to-black transition
- Pauses all simulation (mobs, clock, mining) during fade

**Day-Night cycle:**
- 240-second full cycle
- Daylight = sin(phase × 2π) × 0.95 + 0.05, range 0.04–1.0
- Hostiles spawn below daylight 0.28 (night/dusk/dawn)
- Spiders active below daylight 0.42 (more hours than undead)
- Undead burn above daylight 0.72 (bright day)
- Sleep allowed below daylight 0.28 (nighttime only)

---

### 7. PLAYER ABILITIES

**Movement:**
- Walk: 4.8 m/s (modified by hunger)
- Sprint (Shift): 12.8 m/s, requires hunger > 6, costs 1 per 100 blocks
- Crouch (C): 2.1 m/s, no hunger cost, prevents jumping
- Jump: 8.2 m/s upward, costs 1 per 50 jumps, requires ground
- Physics: gravity 26 m/s², collision with blocks (stepwise), depenetration if stuck, world border padding

**Interaction radius:** 7 blocks (MINE_REACH)

**Mining:**
- Left-click held to break blocks; progress visible as cracks
- **Tool gating:** Tier 0 (bare hand) breaks grass/dirt/leaves/sand/snow/glass/cactus; Tier 1+ for Stone/Cobble/Brick; higher tiers unlock ores
- **Mining speed:** minePower stat × 2.1 × dt, hardness thresholds 1–14
- **Durability:** all tools lose 1 durability per block broken; broken tools return null item (disappear)
- **Drops:** block-specific; ores drop themselves; grass seeds 20%; wheat seeds & grain at harvest
- **Block placement:** right-click in air/water; uses 1 item from selected slot; refuses self-entombment

**Tool interaction:**
- **Hoe:** right-click Grass/Dirt → Farmland, costs 1 durability
- **Seeds:** right-click Farmland (cell above empty) → plant WheatStage0, consume 1 seed

**Combat:**
- Left-click aimed mob (within 4.5 blocks, within ~39° cone of look direction)
- **Damage:** Weapon attack stat or fist 6 damage; durability consumed on hit
- **Knockback:** mobs pushed 0.75 units + 0.12 up in hit direction

**Eating:**
- Right-click held food (hotbar only, inventory closed, not sleeping) → consume 1, restore hunger

**Inventory:**
- 36 slots (9 hotbar + 27 storage), drag-swap, equip armor to 6 dedicated armor slots
- Stacking: max 99 per slot (most items stackable except tools/weapons/armor)

**Camera modes:** First-person (default), third-person rear, third-person front (toggle with F5, render-only, no gameplay impact)

---

### 8. SAVE FORMAT
Source: `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/docs/save-format.md`

**Persists to localStorage (key: `minecraft_save_v5`):**
- World seed (deterministic generation)
- **Block diff** (changes array, not full world): edits against procedural baseline; player-placed crops/beds/furnaces ride this
- Inventory: 36 slots with count + durability per tool/armor
- Equipped armor: 6 slots
- Selected hotbar slot (0–8)
- Player position (xyz)
- **Optional stats:** hearts, hunger, dayClock (restores time-of-day on reload), spawnPoint (bed location or null)

**Does NOT persist:**
- Mobs (respawn on every load)
- Crafting progress
- Mining progress
- Camera mode
- Pause/debug states

**Versioning:** v3 (current, adds optional dayClock/hearts/hunger/spawnPoint); v1–v2 auto-migrated on load. **CRITICAL:** Changing worldgen or voxel index formula silently corrupts saves — must bump SAVE_KEY.

**Autosave:** Every 15 sec + beforeunload.

---

### 9. PROGRESSION TRACE

**Spawn → Survival (Hour 0–1):**
1. Spawn with starter pickaxe + knife + materials
2. Punch trees or mine Planks
3. Craft Wood Pickaxe (if not already owned)
4. Mine Stone with wood pickaxe
5. Craft Stone Pickaxe
6. **Day 1 night:** Find/breed sheep for Wool + Planks, craft Bed, sleep to set spawn
7. Eat raw meat or forage to maintain hunger

**Early Mining (Hour 1–2):**
8. Mine Sliver Ore (needs tier 2) with stone pickaxe
9. Craft Sliver Pickaxe
10. Mine Ruby/Gold Ore (tier 3)
11. Craft upgraded pickaxe
12. Build furnace, smelt meat for food

**Combat/Armor (Hour 2–4):**
13. Craft swords from ores (any tier works, higher = more damage)
14. Hunt hostiles at night for drops
15. Accumulate Sapphire/Ruby for armor recipes
16. Equip helmet → face_mask → neck protection → chestplate → leggings → boots
17. Return to mining with armor defense

**Farming (Parallel, Hour 1+):**
18. Hoe farmland near spawn
19. Plant seeds (from grass drops)
20. Wait for wheat growth (random ticks)
21. Harvest mature wheat for bread (fills 6 hunger vs. raw meat 3)

**Endgame (Hour 4+):**
22. Mine Diamond Ore (tier 7 needed — only top-tier pickaxe)
23. Craft Diamond Sword/Pickaxe
24. Farm passives for breeding (unlimited wool/feathers via population)
25. **No apparent endgame content** — endless mob grind, no bosses, no dimension transitions, no progression gates past diamond

---

### 10. HALF-BUILT / EXTENSION POINTS

**Obvious gaps / scaffolding:**
1. **Mob drops as materials:** Wool, bone, string, leather exist as items but have no crafting use yet (some craft into armor, but incomplete leather armor set)
2. **Furnace system:** Only 2 recipes (cooking meat). No fuel mechanic (unlimited use). Designed to extend with more smelting recipes
3. **Random tick handler registry:** `randomTicks.ts` has a clean handler registry — crop growth works, but saplings/grass spread/fire would slot here
4. **Tool durability:** Implemented everywhere but some tools feel underutilized (hoe only tills, no other special tools)
5. **Interactive block pattern:** `interact.ts` shows clear extensibility (Bed, Furnace handlers, easy to add Chest/Crafting Table/Brewing/etc.)
6. **Armor recipe costs** are ore-heavy (endgame only), but no secondary armor material loops (no leather/iron progression like vanilla)
7. **Line-of-sight AI:** Implemented for mob attacks but no ranged weapons for player yet
8. **Physics:** Crouch ledge-detection exists, but no double-jump, wall-climbing, or advanced parkour mechanics
9. **Biomes:** Five defined (`Plains, Desert, Ocean, Forest, Mountains`) but generation is flat/uniform without distinct biome rules
10. **Void damage:** Applied below y=-4 but no lava, drowning, or suffocation damage yet

---

### Summary Statistics
- **28 block types** (8 terrain, 5 ore, 7 building, 3 interactive, 4 crop stages, 1 air)
- **46 item types** (9 tools, 8 weapons, 6 armor, 6 food, 7 materials, 18 block items)
- **31 recipes** (25 crafting grid, 2 furnace smelting, 4 unused slots for extension)
- **6 mobs** (3 passive, 3 hostile)
- **13 game systems** (engine step order above)
- **~240-second day/night** with 4 behavioral thresholds (spawn, sleep, burn, aggro)
- **7-tier tool/weapon progression**, diamond as soft endgame cap
- **Session-only mobs** (no persistence), **persistent block edits** (diffs), **optional player stats** restore

This is a **complete, playable survival loop** with mining, farming, crafting, combat, and breeding, but **no end-state content** (bosses, rare loot, story) and **significant design room for systems like magic, trading, dungeons, or advanced builds.**
```

```
Perfect. Now I have enough information to provide a comprehensive report. Let me compile all findings.

## Comprehensive Inventory Report: Monecraft

Based on a thorough exploration of the codebase, here is the complete technical inventory of the Minecraft-inspired voxel game.

---

### 1. WORLD GENERATION

**Implementation**: `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/world/generation.ts`

**Terrain Generation**:
- Deterministic Simplex-like noise via `smoothNoise2D()` seeded by world seed
- Biome system with 5 biomes: Plains, Desert, Ocean, Forest, Mountains
- Biome parameters (baseHeight, noiseScale, treeChance) control terrain elevation and variation
- Mountain stone caps above y=65; snow above y=68
- Top block types: Grass, Sand (desert/ocean), or Snow (mountains)
- Layer composition: top block → dirt (y-3 to y-1) → occasional cobblestone → stone core
- Unbreakable bedrock border walls at x/z boundaries and y=0

**Caves**:
- YES - present and substantial
- 230 cave tunnels (random walk with pitch variation, carving spheres)
- 150 cave chambers (larger chambers scattered throughout)
- Caves carved from stone/cobblestone (not bedrock), creating natural cavities

**Ores**:
- YES - 5 ore types with tiered depth distribution:
  - SliverOre (minY: 3, range: 13 levels)
  - RubyOre (minY: 2, range: 18 levels)
  - GoldOre (minY: 2, range: 24 levels)
  - SapphireOre (minY: 2, range: 30 levels)
  - DiamondOre (minY: 2, range: 38 levels)
- Veins clustered (3–10 blocks per vein depending on ore type)
- Ore placement checks for air/water adjacency (exposed ores visible in caves/water)

**Structures**:
- YES - 120 houses per world
- Houses are 7×7 footprints (cobblestone floor, brick/wood walls, wood corners, glass windows, plank roof)
- Placed on flat terrain near spawn; skip if terrain too uneven
- No dungeons, temples, fortresses, or other structures

**Vegetation**:
- Trees: 9000 placement attempts per generation, biome-dependent spawn chance (0% ocean → 60% forest)
  - Trunk height: 3–6 blocks (taller in forest)
  - Leaf canopy: 5-block radius from trunk top (diamond shape)
- Cacti: scattered in desert via spatial hash (density depends on actual desert area)
- No saplings, no grass-to-grass spread, no flowers

**Water & Oceans**:
- YES - static water fills below sea level (y=43) up to the ocean floor
- No water flow, no current, no dynamic water physics
- Beaches auto-place sand at sea-level-adjacent columns (band ±2 blocks)

**World Bounds**:
- FINITE: 512×150×512 (X×Y×Z)
- X/Z wrapped by bedrock border walls at y=1–14
- No world wrapping or tiling; single contiguous world
- Render distance: 90-block radius around player (180 blocks total), rebuilt on 20-block grid boundaries

---

### 2. LIGHTING

**Implementation**: `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/render/GameRenderer.ts` (syncDayNight method)

**Sky Lighting & Day-Night Cycle**:
- YES - dynamic sky color lerp: day (0x8bc2ff, light blue) ↔ night (0x06111f, very dark blue)
- `daylight` value ranges 0.04–1.0, governs sky, sun/hemisphere light intensity
- Day cycle: 240 seconds (4 minutes) per full day-night
- Sun position orbits via `sunAngleAt(dayClock)` — travels from east to west

**Lighting Model**:
- DirectionalLight (sun) + HemisphereLight (ambient)
- Sun intensity: 0.2 + daylight × 1.2 (0.2–1.4 range)
- Hemisphere intensity: 0.24 + daylight × 1.05 (0.24–1.29 range)
- Fog color dynamically matches sky color; fog distance 30–200 units

**Ambient Occlusion**:
- YES - baked per-vertex during mesh generation
- `faceOcclusion()` checks 4 adjacent edges of each face, reduces brightness by up to 0.2
- Darkens corners where faces meet edges

**Block Light & Sky Light**:
- NO - no per-block light propagation
- No torches, no light sources other than sun/sky
- All surfaces lit uniformly by sun + hemisphere (day-dependent)

**Material Shading**:
- Top faces: brightest (1.0 tint)
- Side faces: 0.9 tint
- Bottom faces: 0.95 tint
- Applied before AO darkening

---

### 3. FLUIDS

**Water**:
- STATIC BLOCKS ONLY - no flow physics
- Water is non-solid (player can swim through, no collision)
- Renders faces even against neighboring water (special case in meshing)
- DoubleSide material so water is visible from inside
- No water displacement, no buoyancy boost, no drowning

**Lava**:
- NOT IMPLEMENTED - no lava blocks, no lava physics

**Swimming/Drowning**:
- NOT IMPLEMENTED - player can occupy water blocks without effect
- No oxygen meter, no damage, no slowness

**Flow Simulation**:
- NOT IMPLEMENTED - water is static at generation time

---

### 4. RENDERING FEATURES

**Chunk Meshing**:
- Single unified mesh per RENDER_RADIUS (90-block) region, rebuilt every RENDER_GRID (20-block) boundary cross
- One-shot rebuild on block edit via `state.worldMeshDirty` flag
- Face culling: internal faces skipped, water renders against all neighbors
- No greedy meshing; every exposed face → 2 triangles (quad)

**Greedy Meshing**:
- NO - direct face-per-block approach

**Geometry Details**:
- Positions (3), Normals (3), Colors (3 for AO), UVs (2)
- All faces use shared atlas texture (runtime-generated from BLOCK_COLORS)
- MeshStandardMaterial: roughness 0.88, metalness 0.02
- DoubleSide for water transparency

**Sky & Celestial**:
- Sky color only; no sky box
- Sun: orbiting DirectionalLight (position updated per frame)
- NO MOON - sun is the only celestial object
- NO STARS - no starfield

**Clouds**:
- NO - no cloud layer or particles

**Shadows**:
- NO - no shadow mapping or dynamic shadows
- Sun casts no shadows on terrain or objects

**Particles**:
- NO - no block-break particles, no water splashes, no dust, no explosions

**First/Third-Person**:
- BOTH - V key cycles: first-person → third-person rear → third-person front
- First-person: eye at player head + 0.1 units, FOV 75°
- Third-person: boom at 4 units behind/in front, raycasted against terrain to prevent occlusion
- Gameplay remains eye-relative in all modes (mining reach, combat aim, audio panning)

**Held-Item Model**:
- YES - first-person right hand holds selected hotbar item
- Blocks: rendered as cubes (using HELD_BLOCK_COLORS tint)
- Tools/weapons/food: extruded from 16×16 inventory sprite into pixel-thick voxel meshes
- Animations: swing (attack/mining), walk bob (speed-scaled), equip dip (slot switch), idle sway
- Displayed only in first-person mode

**Player Body** (third-person only):
- YES - humanoid rig: head, torso, arms, legs (all box meshes)
- Colors from 6 skin presets (PlayerPalette: 7 colors each)
- Animations: walk gait (speed-scaled), attack/mining chop, head pitch follow
- Right hand holds the selected hotbar item
- Model visible only in third-person

**Mob Rendering**:
- YES - per-mob-kind models (sheep, chicken, horse, zombie, skeleton, spider)
- Box-mesh construction with color/scale/legs per template
- Leg animation: walk gait, attack/pain reaction
- Scale 0.55× for baby mobs

**Mobile/Performance Optimizations**:
- No LOD (level-of-detail) system
- No frustum culling at block level (entire region mesh rendered)
- No occlusion culling
- Pixel ratio clamped at 2× (max DPI scaling)

---

### 5. AUDIO

**Implementation**: `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/audio/`

**SFX Library** (all synthesized via ZZFX):
- Block interactions: break/place/mining-ticks (per 6 material groups: stone, wood, grass, sand, glass, water)
- Player: footsteps (surface-aware), jump, land (impact-scaled), hurt, eat, death/respawn stingers
- Mobs: idle ambient calls, attack, melee-hit reactions
- Furnace: smelt sound
- Farming: till (hoe), plant (seeds)
- Beds: sleep/wake fades

**Music**:
- Generative ambient pad (pentatonic scale)
- Mood changes: day (bright, faster tempo) ↔ night (dark, slower)
- Biome influence: tune shifts per biome with 5-second hysteresis (avoids mood whipsaw at borders)
- Ducks to 25% volume behind pause menu

**Spatial Audio**:
- Footsteps: position-aware (plays on ground material)
- Mob ambience: distance-attenuated (24-block earshot), stereo pan relative to player look direction
- No 3D positional audio for other sounds (one-shots are center-pan)

**Audio Routing**:
- ZZFX.buildSamples() → AudioBuffer (cached per sound)
- Playback: gain node + pan node per sound
- 10-voice cap per sound type (older voices stop)
- Retrigger throttling: per-sound minimum intervals to de-dupe rapid hits

**Volume Control**:
- Master gain: 0–1 (default 0.8)
- Music gain: 0–1 (default 0.6)
- Mute toggle
- Persisted under separate localStorage key (`minecraft_audio_v1`)

**Context Initialization**:
- AudioContext created lazily on first user gesture (autoplay policy compliance)
- ZZFX module only imported on gesture, not at boot

---

### 6. PERFORMANCE & ARCHITECTURE

**Engine**:
- Headless GameEngine (no DOM, runs in `bun test`)
- Stepped per frame with bounded catch-up substeps (max 50 ms per step)
- Single world instance (512×150×512), all in memory

**Rendering**:
- Three.js WebGL renderer
- Single world mesh (unified geometry, no chunking)
- Mesh rebuild triggered on: player crosses RENDER_GRID boundary, or `state.worldMeshDirty` flag set
- Geometry disposal + recreation (no incremental meshing)

**Web Workers**:
- NO - all work (generation, meshing, simulation) on main thread

**Memory**:
- World voxels: Uint8Array (flat index = x + z×sizeX + y×sizeX×sizeZ)
- Block changes tracked as delta array (save format contract)
- Mobs stored as logical entities in GameState (no Three.js objects until render time)

**Scalability Limits**:
- World size hardcoded at 512×150×512 (no chunking → full world in VRAM)
- Render region: 20-block snap grid, 90-block radius (180×180 visible region, ~65k triangles typical)
- Chunk meshing blocks main thread during rebuild (few milliseconds; noticeable at world boundaries)
- No profiling notes; codebase assumes modern hardware

**Persistence**:
- Autosave every 15 seconds to localStorage
- Save format: seed + block-change deltas only (regenerates terrain from seed + applies deltas)
- Save compatibility: v1 (40 slots/10 hotbar) → v2 (36 slots/9 hotbar) → v3 (adds dayClock, hearts, hunger, spawnPoint)
- Hash-tested worldgen determinism (SHA-256 per seed, pinned in `generation.test.ts`)

---

### 7. INPUT & CONTROLS

**Keyboard**:
- W/A/S/D: move forward/left/back/right
- Space: jump
- C: crouch (slow walk)
- CapsLock + W: sprint (drains hunger)
- 1–9: select hotbar slot
- I: toggle inventory/crafting
- F: eat food (if held)
- U: emergency unstuck (teleport up if stuck in solid)
- V: cycle camera view (first → third rear → third front)
- Esc: pause menu
- F3: debug overlay
- E: place block (alternative to right-click)

**Mouse**:
- Left click: mine block / attack mob (hold to continuous)
- Right click: place block / interact (feed animal, open furnace/bed)
- Mouse movement: look around (requires pointer lock)
- First click: acquires pointer lock (no mining on this click)
- Esc (while locked): releases lock, opens pause menu

**Pointer Lock**:
- YES - required for mouse look
- First click acquires lock; Esc releases it
- Pause menu unlocked (Esc/I exit lock first)

**Gamepad**:
- NO - keyboard/mouse only

**Touch/Mobile**:
- NO - no touch controls or mobile UI

---

### 8. NOTABLE GAPS vs. Real Minecraft

**Caves & Terrain**:
- Caves present but simplistic (random walk + chambers, no shafts/ravines/strongholds)
- No biome-specific structures (villages, temples, mansions, ocean monuments)
- No mineshafts, dungeons, or other underground structures
- No end dimension / Nether dimension / nether portals
- Finite world (no infinite generation)

**Fluids & Physics**:
- Water is static; no flow physics, no current
- NO lava blocks
- NO drowning mechanic
- NO swimming speed effect
- Player occupies water blocks with zero consequence

**Lighting**:
- NO block-level light propagation (no torches, beacons, magma, glow-stone)
- NO dynamic shadows
- NO light attenuation via distance (only sky-based day/night)
- NO cave darkness (caves lit same as surface if visible)

**Biome Features**:
- Biomes exist but are cosmetic (terrain shape + tree density)
- NO biome-specific blocks (swamp water, jungle vines, snowy ice)
- NO weather (no rain, snow, storms)
- NO biome-specific mobs (desert husk, jungle ocelot, plains pillager)

**Mobs**:
- 6 passive + hostile kinds; none natively spawn in specific biomes
- NO Endermen, Creepers, Witches, or other special mobs
- NO bosses (no Ender Dragon, Wither, etc.)
- NO mob pathfinding (simple wander/aggro/flee, no obstacle navigation)
- NO mob variants or damage-knockback stun

**Vegetation**:
- Trees exist but minimal (no saplings, no growth)
- Crops (wheat) grow only via random ticks
- NO flowers, NO tall grass, NO seagrass
- NO tree logs from foliage

**Mining & Tools**:
- Mine-tier gating (wrong tool → slow)
- Tool durability tracked and displayed
- NO enchantments, NO anvil
- NO tool efficiency levels beyond mineTier

**Combat**:
- NO projectiles (bow, crossbow, trident)
- NO shields
- NO potion effects (strength, weakness, speed, slowness)
- NO poison damage (cave spiders exist but don't poison)
- NO knockback distance variation

**Crafting & Recipes**:
- 31 recipes (tools, weapons, armor, food, misc)
- Furnace (smelting station) exists; fuel model simplified (1 plank per recipe)
- NO anvil for repair
- NO brewing stand for potions
- NO enchanting table

**Mechanics**:
- NO redstone
- NO pistons, dispensers, hoppers, droppers
- NO item frames, armor stands
- NO repeaters or comparators
- NO daylight sensors or other automation
- NO TNT or explosions
- NO beds respawn only (no world respawn points beyond beds)

**Visual Effects**:
- NO particles (block break, water splash, fire, explosions, magic)
- NO visual crack overlay while mining (crack texture applied, but no animation)
- NO weather particles (rain, snow)
- NO portal/teleport vfx

**Sound**:
- NO ambient cave sounds
- NO wind/weather audio
- Music is single generative pad (no varied tracks)

**Multiplayer & Networking**:
- SINGLE-PLAYER ONLY - no multiplayer, no server, no networking
- NO PvP
- NO co-op

**Performance & Scale**:
- NO chunk streaming (entire world in memory)
- NO web workers (main thread only)
- NO visible LOD system
- NO frustum culling (entire region mesh rendered)

**UI / Quality of Life**:
- NO map interface beyond minimap
- NO item sorting / quick-move from inventory
- NO keybind customization UI (keybindings exist but hardcoded)
- NO server list / multiplayer lobby
- NO achievements or statistics tracking
- NO creative mode

**Dimensions**:
- NO Nether (no nether blocks, portals, mobs)
- NO End (no end dimension, dragon, endermen)
- NO custom dimensions
```
