  "content": "import { describe, expect, test } from \"bun:test\";\nimport { generateStarPositions } from \"@/lib/game/render/starField\";\n\ndescribe(\"generateStarPositions\", () => {\n  test(\"returns count*3 floats\", () => {\n    expect(generateStarPositions(800, 1).length).toBe(2400);\n  });\n\n  test(\"is deterministic for a seed and varies by seed\", () => {\n    const a = generateStarPositions(64, 7);\n    const b = generateStarPositions(64, 7);\n    const c = generateStarPositions(64, 8);\n    expect(Array.from(a)).toEqual(Array.from(b));\n    expect(Array.from(a)).not.toEqual(Array.from(c));\n  });\n\n  test(\"places every star on the unit sphere, above the horizon\", () => {\n    const p = generateStarPositions(200, 3);\n    for (let i = 0; i < 200; i += 1) {\n      const x = p[i * 3];\n      const y = p[i * 3 + 1];\n      const z = p[i * 3 + 2];\n      expect(Math.hypot(x, y, z)).toBeCloseTo(1, 4);\n      expect(y).toBeGreaterThan(0);\n    }\n  });\n});\n"
}

{
  "file_path": "/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/render/skyView.ts",
  "content": "import * as THREE from \"three\";\nimport { sunAngleAt } from \"@/lib/game/engine/systems/dayNight\";\nimport type { GameState } from \"@/lib/game/engine/state\";\nimport { generateStarPositions } from \"./starField\";\n\n/**\n * Atmospheric sky dressing layered over the existing day-night sky-color lerp:\n * a star field that fades in at dusk, a moon and sun disc that ride opposite\n * ends of the sun arc, and a drifting cloud sheet. Everything follows the\n * camera and ignores fog so it reads as distant sky. All visuals are\n * procedural (Points + canvas-noise textures) — no image assets.\n */\n\nconst SKY_RADIUS = 900;\nconst CLOUD_HEIGHT = 140;\nconst STAR_SEED = 1337;\n\nexport type SkyView = {\n  sync(state: GameState, timeMs: number): void;\n  dispose(): void;\n};\n\nfunction clamp01(v: number): number {\n  return Math.max(0, Math.min(1, v));\n}\n\nfunction skyDir(angle: number, out: THREE.Vector3): THREE.Vector3 {\n  return out.set(Math.cos(angle), Math.sin(angle), Math.sin(angle * 0.7) * 0.6).normalize();\n}\n\n/** Soft radial disc (white core → transparent edge) for the sun/moon sprites. */\nfunction discTexture(core: string): THREE.CanvasTexture {\n  const size = 64;\n  const canvas = document.createElement(\"canvas\");\n  canvas.width = size;\n  canvas.height = size;\n  const ctx = canvas.getContext(\"2d\");\n  if (ctx) {\n    const g = ctx.createRadialGradient(size / 2, size / 2, 1, size / 2, size / 2, size / 2);\n    g.addColorStop(0, core);\n    g.addColorStop(0.55, core);\n    g.addColorStop(1, \"rgba(255,255,255,0)\");\n    ctx.fillStyle = g;\n    ctx.fillRect(0, 0, size, size);\n  }\n  return new THREE.CanvasTexture(canvas);\n}\n\n/** Low-frequency, seamlessly tiling cloud alpha mask (white puffs on clear). */\nfunction cloudTexture(): THREE.CanvasTexture {\n  const size = 128;\n  const canvas = document.createElement(\"canvas\");\n  canvas.width = size;\n  canvas.height = size;\n  const ctx = canvas.getContext(\"2d\");\n  if (ctx) {\n    const image = ctx.createImageData(size, size);\n    const wave = (k: number, t: number) => Math.sin(((2 * Math.PI * k) / size) * t);\n    for (let y = 0; y < size; y += 1) {\n      for (let x = 0; x < size; x += 1) {\n        const v = wave(3, x) + wave(2, y) + wave(2, x + y) + wave(1, x - y);\n        const n = v / 4; // -1..1\n        const alpha = clamp01((n - 0.15) * 1.8);\n        const i = (y * size + x) * 4;\n        image.data[i] = 255;\n        image.data[i + 1] = 255;\n        image.data[i + 2] = 255;\n        image.data[i + 3] = Math.round(alpha * 255);\n      }\n    }\n    ctx.putImageData(image, 0, 0);\n  }\n  const texture = new THREE.CanvasTexture(canvas);\n  texture.wrapS = THREE.RepeatWrapping;\n  texture.wrapT = THREE.RepeatWrapping;\n  texture.repeat.set(2, 2);\n  return texture;\n}\n\nexport function createSkyView(scene: THREE.Scene, camera: THREE.PerspectiveCamera): SkyView {\n  // Stars.\n  const starGeo = new THREE.BufferGeometry();\n  const positions = generateStarPositions(800, STAR_SEED).map((v) => v * SKY_RADIUS);\n  starGeo.setAttribute(\"position\", new THREE.BufferAttribute(positions, 3));\n  const starMat = new THREE.PointsMaterial({ color: 0xffffff, size: 2.4, sizeAttenuation: false, transparent: true, depthWrite: false, fog: false });\n  const stars = new THREE.Points(starGeo, starMat);\n  stars.frustumCulled = false;\n  stars.raycast = () => {};\n  scene.add(stars);\n\n  // Sun + moon discs.\n  const sunTex = discTexture(\"rgba(255,247,214,1)\");\n  const moonTex = discTexture(\"rgba(226,232,245,1)\");\n  const sunMat = new THREE.SpriteMaterial({ map: sunTex, transparent: true, depthWrite: false, fog: false });\n  const moonMat = new THREE.SpriteMaterial({ map: moonTex, transparent: true, depthWrite: false, fog: false });\n  const sun = new THREE.Sprite(sunMat);\n  const moon = new THREE.Sprite(moonMat);\n  sun.scale.setScalar(95);\n  moon.scale.setScalar(70);\n  scene.add(sun);\n  scene.add(moon);\n\n  // Clouds.\n  const cloudTex = cloudTexture();\n  const cloudGeo = new THREE.PlaneGeometry(1400, 1400);\n  const cloudMat = new THREE.MeshBasicMaterial({ map: cloudTex, transparent: true, opacity: 0.32, depthWrite: false, fog: false });\n  const clouds = new THREE.Mesh(cloudGeo, cloudMat);\n  clouds.rotation.x = -Math.PI / 2;\n  clouds.frustumCulled = false;\n  clouds.raycast = () => {};\n  scene.add(clouds);\n\n  const dir = new THREE.Vector3();\n  let lastMs = Number.NaN;\n\n  return {\n    sync(state, timeMs) {\n      const dtMs = Number.isNaN(lastMs) ? 16 : Math.max(0, timeMs - lastMs);\n      lastMs = timeMs;\n      const { daylight } = state;\n      const cam = camera.position;\n      const sunAngle = sunAngleAt(state.dayClock);\n\n      stars.position.copy(cam);\n      starMat.opacity = clamp01(1 - daylight * 2.2);\n\n      skyDir(sunAngle, dir);\n      sun.position.copy(cam).addScaledVector(dir, SKY_RADIUS);\n      sunMat.opacity = clamp01(daylight * 1.4 - 0.08);\n\n      skyDir(sunAngle + Math.PI, dir);\n      moon.position.copy(cam).addScaledVector(dir, SKY_RADIUS);\n      moonMat.opacity = clamp01(0.9 - daylight * 1.8);\n\n      clouds.position.set(cam.x, cam.y + CLOUD_HEIGHT, cam.z);\n      cloudMat.opacity = 0.12 + daylight * 0.24;\n      cloudTex.offset.x += dtMs * 0.000004;\n      cloudTex.offset.y += dtMs * 0.0000022;\n    },\n\n    dispose() {\n      scene.remove(stars);\n      scene.remove(sun);\n      scene.remove(moon);\n      scene.remove(clouds);\n      starGeo.dispose();\n      starMat.dispose();\n      sunMat.dispose();\n      moonMat.dispose();\n      sunTex.dispose();\n      moonTex.dispose();\n      cloudGeo.dispose();\n      cloudMat.dispose();\n      cloudTex.dispose();\n    }\n  };\n}\n"
}

{
  "file_path": "/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/engine/systems/weather.ts",
  "content": "import { WEATHER_CYCLE_SECONDS, WEATHER_RAIN_FRACTION } from \"@/lib/game/config\";\nimport { BiomeId } from \"@/lib/world\";\nimport type { GameState, WeatherKind } from \"../state\";\n\n/**\n * Cosmetic, transient weather. Deterministic from `dayClock` + world seed: time\n * is split into fixed windows, a seeded hash of the window index decides whether\n * it precipitates, and a triangular envelope ramps intensity 0→1→0 across the\n * window so it eases in and out.\n *\n * Being a pure function of `dayClock` (no internal accumulator) makes it immune\n * to the engine's substep catch-up loop — slicing the same elapsed time into\n * more or fewer steps yields the identical result. Weather is NEVER persisted\n * and NEVER touches spawn/daylight balance — it only sets `state.weather`.\n */\n\nfunction hash01(n: number, seed: number): number {\n  const v = Math.sin((n + 1) * 12.9898 + seed * 78.233) * 43758.5453;\n  return v - Math.floor(v);\n}\n\nexport function weatherAt(dayClock: number, seed: number): { active: boolean; intensity: number } {\n  const cycle = Math.floor(dayClock / WEATHER_CYCLE_SECONDS);\n  if (hash01(cycle, seed) >= WEATHER_RAIN_FRACTION) return { active: false, intensity: 0 };\n  const phase = (dayClock % WEATHER_CYCLE_SECONDS) / WEATHER_CYCLE_SECONDS; // 0..1\n  return { active: true, intensity: 1 - Math.abs(phase * 2 - 1) };\n}\n\n/** Snowy biomes get snow; dry biomes stay clear; everything else rains. */\nfunction precipKindFor(biome: BiomeId): WeatherKind {\n  if (biome === BiomeId.Mountains) return \"snow\";\n  if (biome === BiomeId.Desert || biome === BiomeId.Ocean) return \"clear\";\n  return \"rain\";\n}\n\nexport function tickWeather(state: GameState): void {\n  const { active, intensity } = weatherAt(state.dayClock, state.world.seed);\n  if (!active) {\n    state.weather.kind = \"clear\";\n    state.weather.intensity = 0;\n    return;\n  }\n  const kind = precipKindFor(state.world.getBiome(Math.floor(state.player.position.x), Math.floor(state.player.position.z)));\n  state.weather.kind = kind;\n  state.weather.intensity = kind === \"clear\" ? 0 : intensity;\n}\n"
}

{
  "file_path": "/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/engine/systems/weather.test.ts",
  "content": "import { describe, expect, test } from \"bun:test\";\nimport { WEATHER_CYCLE_SECONDS } from \"@/lib/game/config\";\nimport { tickWeather, weatherAt } from \"@/lib/game/engine/systems/weather\";\nimport type { GameState } from \"@/lib/game/engine/state\";\nimport { BiomeId } from \"@/lib/world\";\n\n/** Finds a dayClock at the mid-point (peak intensity) of an active window. */\nfunction activeDayClock(seed: number): number {\n  for (let cycle = 0; cycle < 200; cycle += 1) {\n    const t = cycle * WEATHER_CYCLE_SECONDS + WEATHER_CYCLE_SECONDS / 2;\n    if (weatherAt(t, seed).active) return t;\n  }\n  throw new Error(\"no active weather window found\");\n}\n\nfunction stubState(biome: BiomeId, dayClock: number, seed: number): GameState {\n  return {\n    dayClock,\n    weather: { kind: \"clear\", intensity: 0 },\n    player: { position: { x: 5, y: 64, z: 5 } },\n    world: { seed, getBiome: () => biome }\n  } as unknown as GameState;\n}\n\ndescribe(\"weatherAt\", () => {\n  test(\"is deterministic and bounded to [0,1]\", () => {\n    for (let t = 0; t < 2000; t += 37) {\n      const a = weatherAt(t, 42);\n      const b = weatherAt(t, 42);\n      expect(a).toEqual(b);\n      expect(a.intensity).toBeGreaterThanOrEqual(0);\n      expect(a.intensity).toBeLessThanOrEqual(1);\n    }\n  });\n\n  test(\"intensity peaks at the middle of an active window\", () => {\n    const seed = 42;\n    const mid = activeDayClock(seed);\n    expect(weatherAt(mid, seed).intensity).toBeCloseTo(1, 5);\n    // Window edges ramp to ~0.\n    expect(weatherAt(mid - WEATHER_CYCLE_SECONDS / 2 + 0.001, seed).intensity).toBeLessThan(0.02);\n  });\n\n  test(\"only a fraction of windows precipitate\", () => {\n    let active = 0;\n    const windows = 400;\n    for (let c = 0; c < windows; c += 1) {\n      if (weatherAt(c * WEATHER_CYCLE_SECONDS + 1, 9).active) active += 1;\n    }\n    const fraction = active / windows;\n    expect(fraction).toBeGreaterThan(0.15);\n    expect(fraction).toBeLessThan(0.6);\n  });\n});\n\ndescribe(\"tickWeather\", () => {\n  test(\"snows in the mountains, rains elsewhere, during an active window\", () => {\n    const seed = 42;\n    const t = activeDayClock(seed);\n\n    const snowy = stubState(BiomeId.Mountains, t, seed);\n    tickWeather(snowy);\n    expect(snowy.weather.kind).toBe(\"snow\");\n    expect(snowy.weather.intensity).toBeGreaterThan(0);\n\n    const rainy = stubState(BiomeId.Forest, t, seed);\n    tickWeather(rainy);\n    expect(rainy.weather.kind).toBe(\"rain\");\n\n    const dry = stubState(BiomeId.Desert, t, seed);\n    tickWeather(dry);\n    expect(dry.weather.kind).toBe(\"clear\");\n    expect(dry.weather.intensity).toBe(0);\n  });\n\n  test(\"clears outside an active window regardless of biome\", () => {\n    const seed = 42;\n    // Find an inactive window.\n    let t = 0;\n    for (let cycle = 0; cycle < 200; cycle += 1) {\n      const candidate = cycle * WEATHER_CYCLE_SECONDS + WEATHER_CYCLE_SECONDS / 2;\n      if (!weatherAt(candidate, seed).active) {\n        t = candidate;\n        break;\n      }\n    }\n    const state = stubState(BiomeId.Forest, t, seed);\n    tickWeather(state);\n    expect(state.weather.kind).toBe(\"clear\");\n    expect(state.weather.intensity).toBe(0);\n  });\n});\n"
}

{
  "file_path": "/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/render/precipitation.ts",
  "content": "import * as THREE from \"three\";\nimport type { GameState } from \"@/lib/game/engine/state\";\n\n/**\n * Camera-following rain/snow field driven by state.weather. A fixed pool of\n * points falls through a box around the camera; particles that drop below or\n * drift out of the box are recycled at the top, so the field never depletes and\n * always surrounds the player. Rain falls fast and bluish; snow drifts slowly\n * and white. Procedural soft-dot sprite — no asset. Hidden when weather is clear.\n */\n\nconst MAX_PRECIP = 320;\nconst BOX_HALF = 20;\nconst SPAWN_TOP = 16;\nconst FLOOR_BELOW = 8;\n\nconst RAIN_COLOR = new THREE.Color(0.62, 0.68, 0.82);\nconst SNOW_COLOR = new THREE.Color(0.95, 0.96, 1);\n\nfunction dotTexture(): THREE.CanvasTexture {\n  const size = 32;\n  const canvas = document.createElement(\"canvas\");\n  canvas.width = size;\n  canvas.height = size;\n  const ctx = canvas.getContext(\"2d\");\n  if (ctx) {\n    const g = ctx.createRadialGradient(size / 2, size / 2, 0.5, size / 2, size / 2, size / 2);\n    g.addColorStop(0, \"rgba(255,255,255,1)\");\n    g.addColorStop(0.5, \"rgba(255,255,255,0.85)\");\n    g.addColorStop(1, \"rgba(255,255,255,0)\");\n    ctx.fillStyle = g;\n    ctx.fillRect(0, 0, size, size);\n  }\n  return new THREE.CanvasTexture(canvas);\n}\n\nexport type PrecipitationView = {\n  sync(state: GameState, dtMs: number, cameraPos: THREE.Vector3): void;\n  dispose(): void;\n};\n\nexport function createPrecipitation(scene: THREE.Scene): PrecipitationView {\n  const px = new Float32Array(MAX_PRECIP);\n  const py = new Float32Array(MAX_PRECIP);\n  const pz = new Float32Array(MAX_PRECIP);\n  const vx = new Float32Array(MAX_PRECIP);\n  const vy = new Float32Array(MAX_PRECIP);\n  const vz = new Float32Array(MAX_PRECIP);\n  let seeded = false;\n\n  const geometry = new THREE.BufferGeometry();\n  const position = new THREE.BufferAttribute(new Float32Array(MAX_PRECIP * 3), 3);\n  position.setUsage(THREE.DynamicDrawUsage);\n  geometry.setAttribute(\"position\", position);\n  geometry.setDrawRange(0, 0);\n\n  const texture = dotTexture();\n  const material = new THREE.PointsMaterial({ map: texture, color: RAIN_COLOR, size: 0.2, transparent: true, depthWrite: false, sizeAttenuation: true });\n  const points = new THREE.Points(geometry, material);\n  points.frustumCulled = false;\n  points.raycast = () => {};\n  scene.add(points);\n\n  const reseed = (i: number, cam: THREE.Vector3): void => {\n    px[i] = cam.x + (Math.random() * 2 - 1) * BOX_HALF;\n    pz[i] = cam.z + (Math.random() * 2 - 1) * BOX_HALF;\n    py[i] = cam.y + 2 + Math.random() * SPAWN_TOP;\n  };\n\n  return {\n    sync(state, dtMs, cam) {\n      const { kind, intensity } = state.weather;\n      if (kind === \"clear\" || intensity <= 0) {\n        geometry.setDrawRange(0, 0);\n        return;\n      }\n      const dt = Math.min(dtMs / 1000, 0.1);\n      const snow = kind === \"snow\";\n      const target = Math.floor(intensity * MAX_PRECIP);\n      const fall = snow ? 3.2 : 22;\n      const wind = snow ? 1.2 : 2.5;\n\n      material.color.copy(snow ? SNOW_COLOR : RAIN_COLOR);\n      material.size = snow ? 0.32 : 0.18;\n      material.opacity = 0.5 + intensity * 0.45;\n\n      if (!seeded) {\n        for (let i = 0; i < MAX_PRECIP; i += 1) {\n          reseed(i, cam);\n          vx[i] = (Math.random() * 2 - 1) * wind;\n          vy[i] = -fall * (0.85 + Math.random() * 0.3);\n          vz[i] = (Math.random() * 2 - 1) * wind;\n        }\n        seeded = true;\n      }\n\n      const arr = position.array as Float32Array;\n      for (let i = 0; i < target; i += 1) {\n        px[i] += vx[i] * dt;\n        py[i] += (snow ? vy[i] : -fall) * dt;\n        pz[i] += vz[i] * dt;\n        if (py[i] < cam.y - FLOOR_BELOW || Math.abs(px[i] - cam.x) > BOX_HALF + 6 || Math.abs(pz[i] - cam.z) > BOX_HALF + 6) {\n          reseed(i, cam);\n          vx[i] = (Math.random() * 2 - 1) * wind;\n          vy[i] = -fall * (0.85 + Math.random() * 0.3);\n          vz[i] = (Math.random() * 2 - 1) * wind;\n        }\n        arr[i * 3] = px[i];\n        arr[i * 3 + 1] = py[i];\n        arr[i * 3 + 2] = pz[i];\n      }\n      geometry.setDrawRange(0, target);\n      position.needsUpdate = true;\n    },\n\n    dispose() {\n      scene.remove(points);\n      geometry.dispose();\n      material.dispose();\n      texture.dispose();\n    }\n  };\n}\n"
}

{
  "file_path": "/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/audio/rainLoop.ts",
  "content": "/**\n * A continuous rain bed: a looping white-noise buffer through a lowpass into a\n * gain that ramps with intensity. Fully synthesized (no asset), lives off the\n * one-shot SFX voice budget, and is silent at intensity 0.\n */\nexport type RainLoop = {\n  setIntensity(level: number): void;\n  dispose(): void;\n};\n\nconst MAX_GAIN = 0.16;\n\nexport function createRainLoop(ctx: AudioContext, destination: AudioNode): RainLoop {\n  const buffer = ctx.createBuffer(1, Math.floor(ctx.sampleRate * 2), ctx.sampleRate);\n  const data = buffer.getChannelData(0);\n  for (let i = 0; i < data.length; i += 1) data[i] = Math.random() * 2 - 1;\n\n  const source = ctx.createBufferSource();\n  source.buffer = buffer;\n  source.loop = true;\n  const filter = ctx.createBiquadFilter();\n  filter.type = \"lowpass\";\n  filter.frequency.value = 1600;\n  const gain = ctx.createGain();\n  gain.gain.value = 0;\n\n  source.connect(filter);\n  filter.connect(gain);\n  gain.connect(destination);\n  source.start();\n\n  let disposed = false;\n  return {\n    setIntensity(level) {\n      if (disposed) return;\n      const target = Math.max(0, Math.min(1, level)) * MAX_GAIN;\n      gain.gain.setTargetAtTime(target, ctx.currentTime, 0.4);\n    },\n    dispose() {\n      disposed = true;\n      try {\n        source.stop();\n      } catch {\n        // already stopped\n      }\n      source.disconnect();\n      filter.disconnect();\n      gain.disconnect();\n    }\n  };\n}\n"
}

A pure, deterministic weather system (weatherAt +\ntickWeather) splits time into fixed windows and a seeded hash of the window\nindex decides whether it precipitates, with a triangular intensity envelope.\nBeing a pure function of dayClock + seed makes it immune to the engine's\nsubstep catch-up loop. The player's biome picks the form: snow in the\nmountains, clear in desert/ocean, rain elsewhere.\n\nstate.weather is transient — deliberately NOT added to serialize() or the\nsnapshot (so saves and worldgen are untouched) — and weather never changes\nspawn/daylight balance; it only drives visuals and audio:\n- a camera-following precipitation field (fast bluish rain / slow white snow)\n  that recycles points through a box around the player,\n- an overcast tint in syncDayNight that grays the sky, dims sun/hemi light,\n  and pulls fog in (cosmetic — daylight itself is unchanged),\n- a synthesized looping rain bed (filtered noise) on the audio graph, off the\n  SFX voice budget and silent for snow / while paused.\n\nConfig gains WEATHER_CYCLE_SECONDS and WEATHER_RAIN_FRACTION. No save-format\nor worldgen impact.\nEOF\ngit log --oneline -1",
  "description": "Commit Slice 3"
}

id: toolu_01FPNQSHPFueBtVnojGygdUy
```
13:AudioDirector (lib/game/audio/)  WebAudio: maps engine events + state → procedural sound
16:The engine has **no React, no DOM, no rendering** — it runs (and is tested) headlessly in `bun test`. The renderer, the audio director, and the input controller are the only modules touching Three.js scene objects, the `AudioContext`, and DOM listeners respectively.
18:## React shell (`lib/game/useMinecraftGame.ts`, `components/`)
20:- `useMinecraftGame` creates the `GameEngine` in the canvas mount's callback ref, then an effect builds the `GameRenderer`, the `AudioDirector`, and `inputController` and drives the `requestAnimationFrame` loop: `engine.step(dt, input)` (in bounded catch-up substeps of ≤50 ms, so slow frames — e.g. software GL — don't run the simulation in slow motion) → drain engine events (death/respawn handling, `attackSwung` → `renderer.triggerSwing()`, + `audio.handleEvent`) → `minimap.sync(state)` → `renderer.sync(state)` → `audio.sync(state, dt)` → `renderer.render()`. The minimap must sync **before** the renderer because it reads `state.worldMeshDirty`, which `renderer.sync` clears.
30:## Game engine (`lib/game/engine/`)
38:### Per-frame step order (in `GameEngine.step`)
44:4. Player movement & physics (`systems/playerMotion.ts` — derives direction from `yaw`, scratch vectors, no per-frame allocations)
47:7. Day-night clock (`systems/dayNight.ts` — `daylightAt()` is the single daylight formula)
48:8. Random block ticks (`systems/randomTicks.ts` — each interval samples columns near the player and runs per-block handlers; drives crop growth and is the extension point for future saplings / grass spread)
51:11. Animal breeding (`systems/breeding.ts` — feed-to-love timers, baby maturity, pairing in-love adults within range; the passive cap bounds the population)
55:## Renderer (`lib/game/render/`)
60:- `mobVisuals.ts` — mob id → model map; creates/removes models as mobs spawn/die and animates bob + leg gait from mob state (the simulation knows nothing about legs).
61:- `heldItem.ts` / `crackOverlay.ts` — first-person item model and the 8-stage mining crack box (stage = progress / hardness). Held blocks stay cubes; everything else is the item's 16×16 inventory sprite extruded into a pixel-thick voxel mesh (`extrudedSprite.ts`, vertex colors, silhouette-only side faces) — the render layer imports `lib/ui/spritePixels` for this, which is legal because spritePixels is pure pixel-buffer code with no DOM. The holder group is posed every frame by `heldItemPose.ts` (pure math): a one-shot swing on the `attackSwung` event (the shell calls `renderer.triggerSwing()` from the event drain), a looping swing while mining, a walk bob scaled by horizontal speed, an equip dip on slot switch, and a faint idle sway.
62:- `playerModel.ts` / `playerPose.ts` / `playerVisuals.ts` — the player's own humanoid body, visible only in third person: box meshes with pivot groups at the joints, walk gait + attack/mining chop from pure pose math (mirroring `heldItemPose.ts`), the look pitch applied to the head only, and the held hotbar item in the right hand via the shared `itemModel.ts` builder (also used by `heldItem.ts`).
66:## Audio (`lib/game/audio/`)
68:All sound is procedural — no audio files, mirroring the sprite system. SFX are synthesized by [ZZFX](https://github.com/KilledByAPixel/ZzFX) parameter arrays; music is a generative ambient pad over raw WebAudio nodes.
70:- `audioDirector.ts` — the shell-side observer, exactly parallel to the renderer: `handleEvent(GameEvent)` plays one-shots (block break/place by material, hurt/eat/jump/land, mob attacks, death/respawn stingers) and `sync(state, dt)` drives everything continuous (footsteps, mining hit ticks, mob ambience, music mood). Music follows `state.daylight` immediately and the biome with 5 s hysteresis, and ducks while paused. The engine never imports this module.
71:- `synth.ts` — `SynthBackend`, **the only seam that touches WebAudio for SFX**: renders each `SoundDef` to an `AudioBuffer` once via `ZZFX.buildSamples` and plays it through gain/pan nodes with playback-rate jitter, a 10-voice cap, and per-sound retrigger throttling (which also de-dupes catch-up substep event bursts). Tests inject a recording fake instead.
74:- **Lazy unlock invariant**: no `AudioContext` exists until the first user gesture calls `unlock()` — including the `zzfx` import itself, which instantiates one at module scope and is therefore only ever imported dynamically inside `unlock()`. This satisfies the browser autoplay policy, keeps SSR/`bun test` import-safe, and keeps the console clean for the E2E fixture.
77:## Input (`lib/game/input/inputController.ts`)
83:## Inventory and items (`lib/game/`)
91:## World layer (`lib/world/`)
102:## Engine invariants & gotchas
```

44	4. Player movement & physics (`systems/playerMotion.ts` — derives direction from `yaw`, scratch vectors, no per-frame allocations)
45	5. Hunger drain from sprint/walk/jump budgets + health regen (`systems/playerStats.ts`)
46	6. Mining progress and block breaking (`systems/mining.ts`; placement also lives here)
47	7. Day-night clock (`systems/dayNight.ts` — `daylightAt()` is the single daylight formula)
48	8. Random block ticks (`systems/randomTicks.ts` — each interval samples columns near the player and runs per-block handlers; drives crop growth and is the extension point for future saplings / grass spread)
49	9. Night hostile spawning (`systems/spawnDirector.ts`, interval/cap in config)
50	10. Mob AI: wander/aggro/flee, attacks with line-of-sight, daylight burn (`systems/mobAI.ts`)
51	11. Animal breeding (`systems/breeding.ts` — feed-to-love timers, baby maturity, pairing in-love adults within range; the passive cap bounds the population)
52	
53	Combat (`systems/combat.ts`) runs on the `attack` command rather than per frame. The `placeBlock` command runs a fixed right-click precedence (`systems/interact.ts`) — feed an aimed animal (`tryFeedAimedMob`) → interact with the aimed block (`tryInteractBlock`, e.g. sleep in a bed, open a furnace) → use the held item (`tryUseHeldItem`, e.g. a hoe tills soil, seeds plant a crop) → `placeSelectedBlock` — so each interaction consumes the click instead of getting a block placed against it. New mechanics get a new system module and a slot in this sequence — don't grow the engine class with inline logic.
54	
55	## Renderer (`lib/game/render/`)
56	
57	- `GameRenderer.ts` — scene/camera/WebGL/lighting; `sync(state)` maps simulation state to visuals each frame; `dispose()` frees every GPU resource. WebGL creation returns `{ ok: false, error }` instead of throwing.
58	- **Camera modes**: `state.cameraMode` (session-only, V key) selects first-person or a third-person rear/front boom. Pose math is pure (`cameraView.ts`); the boom is clamped each frame by a `voxelRaycast` from the eye so walls never occlude the player. Gameplay stays eye-relative in every mode — mining/combat raycasts and audio panning read player yaw/pitch, never the camera.
59	- **World mesh**: one mesh covers the visible region (not chunked), rebuilt when the player crosses a `RENDER_GRID` (20-block) boundary or when the engine sets `state.worldMeshDirty` (block edits, respawn, unstuck). Old geometry is disposed on rebuild.
60	- `mobVisuals.ts` — mob id → model map; creates/removes models as mobs spawn/die and animates bob + leg gait from mob state (the simulation knows nothing about legs).
61	- `heldItem.ts` / `crackOverlay.ts` — first-person item model and the 8-stage mining crack box (stage = progress / hardness). Held blocks stay cubes; everything else is the item's 16×16 inventory sprite extruded into a pixel-thick voxel mesh (`extrudedSprite.ts`, vertex colors, silhouette-only side faces) — the render layer imports `lib/ui/spritePixels` for this, which is legal because spritePixels is pure pixel-buffer code with no DOM. The holder group is posed every frame by `heldItemPose.ts` (pure math): a one-shot swing on the `attackSwung` event (the shell calls `renderer.triggerSwing()` from the event drain), a looping swing while mining, a walk bob scaled by horizontal speed, an equip dip on slot switch, and a faint idle sway.
62	- `playerModel.ts` / `playerPose.ts` / `playerVisuals.ts` — the player's own humanoid body, visible only in third person: box meshes with pivot groups at the joints, walk gait + attack/mining chop from pure pose math (mirroring `heldItemPose.ts`), the look pitch applied to the head only, and the held hotbar item in the right hand via the shared `itemModel.ts` builder (also used by `heldItem.ts`).
63	- **Skins**: the body is colored by a `PlayerPalette` from `lib/game/playerSkins.ts` (six presets); `GameRenderer.setPlayerSkin(palette)` recolors the named materials in place. The choice persists under its own localStorage key (`lib/game/skinSettings.ts`, `minecraft_skin_v1`) — a player preference like audio volumes, never part of the world save — and the pause-menu picker's bust portraits (`lib/ui/skinPortrait.ts`) derive from the same palettes.
64	- `minimap.ts` / `minimapColors.ts` — the top-right minimap: pure column sampling (top non-air block, height-shaded `BLOCK_COLORS`) feeding a 2D canvas that rebuilds its 128×128 base only when the player crosses a 16-block grid boundary or `worldMeshDirty` is set (read-only — the renderer owns clearing it), then blits with a yaw-rotated player arrow at ~10 Hz.
65

{
  "command": "gh pr create --repo hutusi/monecraft --base main --head feat/juice-atmosphere --title \"feat: juice & atmosphere — particles, night sky, and weather\" --body \"$(cat <<'EOF'\nMakes the world react and breathe. Three additive layers, all **pure procedural rendering/audio** (zero new assets) with **no save-format and no worldgen impact**.\n\n## What's new\n\n- **Particles.** A pure, unit-tested structure-of-arrays pool (`particlePool.ts`) wrapped by a single `THREE.Points` draw call (`particleSystem.ts`); a small shader draws each particle as a soft round sprite that fades over its life (no texture). The renderer gained `handleEvent(event, state)`, wired into the event drain beside `audio.handleEvent`: breaking a block throws shards tinted from `BLOCK_COLORS`, placing puffs, mob death bursts in the mob's body color, eating drops crumbs, and jumping/landing kick dust (scaled by impact). Footstep dust spawns from a stride accumulator. The three positioned events (`blockBroken`/`blockPlaced`/`mobDied`) gained additive `x,y,z` fields.\n- **Night sky.** A deterministic 800-point star field that fades in at dusk, sprite discs for the moon and a now-visible sun on opposite ends of the sun arc, and a drifting cloud sheet from a seamlessly tiling canvas-noise mask (`starField.ts`, `skyView.ts`). Camera-following and fog-exempt; opacities ramp off `daylight`.\n- **Weather.** A pure, deterministic system (`engine/systems/weather.ts`) sets a **transient** `state.weather` (never serialized) — time splits into windows, a seeded hash decides precipitation, biome picks snow (mountains) / clear (desert, ocean) / rain. It drives a camera-following precipitation field (`precipitation.ts`), an overcast sky tint + dimmed light + nearer fog in `syncDayNight`, and a synthesized looping rain bed on the audio graph (`rainLoop.ts`). Strictly cosmetic — spawn/daylight balance untouched.\n\n## Design notes\n\n- Particles freeze with the sim while paused; the pool recycles oldest at its 512 cap and clamps dt so a stalled tab can't teleport everything.\n- `weatherAt` is a pure function of `dayClock` + seed (no accumulator), so it's immune to the engine's substep catch-up loop. `state.weather` is deliberately **not** in `serialize()` or the snapshot.\n- Every new GPU resource (Points geometries/materials, sky sprites, canvas textures) is freed in `GameRenderer.dispose()`.\n\n## Verification\n\n- `bun run lint`, `bun run typecheck`, `bun run format:check`, `bun test` (**326 pass**, +14 new: particle pool, star positions, weather system), and `bun run build` all green.\n- Full **9-test Playwright e2e** suite passes after each slice — the new sky/particle/precip `sync` paths run every frame, and breaking blocks (mining test) exercises a burst with no console errors.\n- **No save-format / worldgen change**: `generation.test.ts` (worldgen hashes) and `save.test.ts` + the save round-trip e2e are untouched and green.\n\n## Commits\n\n1. `feat(render)` procedural particle system\n2. `feat(render)` night sky (stars, moon, sun disc, clouds)\n3. `feat(weather)` transient rain/snow + overcast + rain bed\n4. `docs` CHANGELOG + architecture\n\nA manual `bun run dev` pass is worth it for feel (break blocks, scrub to night, catch a rain window), but isn't doable in CI.\nEOF\n)\" 2>&1 | tail -3",

  "dangerouslyDisableSandbox": true
}

feat(render): night sky with stars, moon, sun disc, and drifting clouds
feat(render): procedural particle system for break/death/dust juice
```

**What shipped** — three additive, purely-procedural layers (zero new assets, no save-format or worldgen impact):
- **Particles** — a pure, unit-tested pool + one `THREE.Points` draw call with a soft-sprite shader; block-break shards, mob-death puffs, eat crumbs, and jump/land/footstep dust, wired through a new `renderer.handleEvent`.
- **Night sky** — fading star field, moon, a now-visible sun disc, and drifting procedural clouds, all ramped by daylight.
- **Weather** — a deterministic, **transient** weather system driving rain/snow precipitation, an overcast sky, and a synthesized rain bed; strictly cosmetic (no balance changes), never serialized.

**Verification** — lint, typecheck, format, **326 unit tests** (+14 new), and `bun run build` all green; I ran the **full 9-test Playwright e2e suite locally after each slice** (no console/WebGL errors). The worldgen-hash and save round-trip tests stay untouched, proving the no-impact claim.

CI is running now. Given last time the e2e check caught a regression, want me to watch CI + CodeRabbit on this PR and report back (and fix anything that comes up), or leave it with you? I held off auto-watching since you interrupted that before.

1	# Testing
2	
3	Two runners, three layers:
4	
5	- **`bun test`** — unit, integration, and component tests, colocated as `*.test.ts[x]` next to the code they cover. The shared setup (`tests/setup.ts`, preloaded via `bunfig.toml`) registers happy-dom so React component tests run under the same runner; the game engine itself needs no DOM (see [architecture.md](architecture.md)).
6	- **`bun run test:e2e`** — Playwright browser smoke tests in `e2e/*.e2e.ts` against the production build. The file suffix matters: `bun test` auto-collects `*.test.ts` AND `*.spec.ts`, so E2E files must use `.e2e.ts`.
7	
8	## What is covered
9	
10	| Area                 | File                                                                                                                                  | What it pins down                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
11	| -------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
12	| Worldgen determinism | `lib/world/generation.test.ts`                                                                                                        | SHA-256 digests of generated worlds per seed (the save-compat contract), plus structural probes and meshing snapshots                                                                                                                                                                                       |
13	| Voxel store          | `lib/world/voxelWorld.test.ts`                                                                                                        | get/set/bounds/solidity and the voxel index formula saves depend on                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
14	| Raycast & collision  | `lib/world/queries.test.ts`                                                                                                           | DDA hit/previous cells and hit distance, AABB collision edges, water non-solidity                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
15	| Items & recipes      | `lib/game/config.test.ts`                                                                                                             | Referential integrity: every recipe/drop/armor mapping points at a real item                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
16	| Inventory algebra    | `lib/game/inventory.test.ts`                                                                                                          | Stack math, durability, crafting (including the refuse-on-full behavior), armor equip rules                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
17	| Save format          | `lib/game/save.test.ts`                                                                                                               | Round-trips, legacy-shape parsing, corrupt-data rejection                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
18	| Simulation           | `lib/game/engine/GameEngine.test.ts`                                                                                                  | Headless gameplay: boot, movement, hunger/regen gates, mining, commands (incl. pause/debug/camera-view/respawn), death/respawn, night spawning, save round-trips, gameplay event emission (block break/place, hurt, eat, jump/land, mob attack/hit)                                                         |
19	| Audio data           | `lib/game/audio/{materials,soundParams}.test.ts`                                                                                      | Every `BlockId` has a material group; every group/`MobKind` has well-formed ZZFX sound entries                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
20	| Audio schedulers     | `lib/game/audio/{footsteps,mobAmbience,musicBrain}.test.ts`                                                                           | Stride cadence and airborne reset; earshot gating, distance gain, look-relative pan, seeded call intervals; notes stay on scale, day/night/biome moods, deterministic under a seeded rng                                                                                                                    |
21	| Audio routing        | `lib/game/audio/audioDirector.test.ts`                                                                                                | With an injected fake `SynthBackend`: events → correct sound tables, staged mining ticks, footsteps while walking, pause ducking, mute/volume application                                                                                                                                                   |
22	| Audio settings       | `lib/game/audio/settings.test.ts`                                                                                                     | Round-trips, corrupt-data fallback, range clamping (own localStorage key, not the world save)                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
23	| Skin presets         | `lib/game/{playerSkins,skinSettings}.test.ts`                                                                                         | Preset integrity (six unique, valid colors, default-id fallback) and preference persistence (round-trip, corrupt/unknown-id fallback, swallowed write failures)                                                                                                                                             |
24	| UI sprites           | `lib/ui/*.test.ts`                                                                                                                    | Every item id renders a non-empty deterministic 16×16 sprite; HUD icon variants (full/half/container) differ; every skin preset renders a distinct bust portrait in its own palette                                                                                                                         |
25	| Minimap sampling     | `lib/game/render/minimapColors.test.ts`                                                                                               | Top-block detection (water visible), height shading, per-block colors                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
26	| Renderer logic       | `lib/game/render/{mobVisuals,heldItem,heldItemPose,extrudedSprite,itemModel,playerModel,playerPose,playerVisuals,cameraView}.test.ts` | Model lifecycle (create/reuse/remove/dispose), positioning, bob, item swaps, held-item swing/equip/bob poses, sprite-extrusion face culling and vertex colors, player-body gait/chop poses and visibility per camera mode, third-person camera placement (rear/front, clamped boom) — pure Three.js, no DOM |
27	| Components           | `components/game/*.test.tsx`                                                                                                          | InventoryPanel click-to-swap/equip/craft gating, Hotbar slots, StatusBars heart/hunger/armor meters, PauseMenu, DeathScreen (happy-dom + Testing Library)                                                                                                                                                   |
28	| Browser E2E          | `e2e/smoke.e2e.ts`                                                                                                                    | Real browser: boot without console errors, scene draws, input → movement, craft via UI, mining, camera view cycling, skin pick persisting across reload, pause freeze/resume, save via pause menu across reload                                                                                             |
29	
30	## Running the E2E suite
31	
32	```bash
33	bunx playwright install chromium   # once
34	bun run test:e2e                   # builds and starts the prod server itself
35	```
36	
37	E2E tests assert through `window.__monecraft` (the live engine/renderer/input handle — also handy in the browser console) instead of pixels; the one render check uses `renderer.renderedTriangles()`. The shared fixture fails any test that logs a console error.
38	
39	**happy-dom has no 2D canvas context**: generated sprites degrade to a transparent data URL in component tests — assert sprite pixels in the pure `lib/ui` tests and component structure via roles/labels/`data-icon` attributes, not image sources.
40	
41	**happy-dom has no `AudioContext`** (and the `zzfx` package instantiates one at import): everything above the `SynthBackend` seam is pure and tested with a recording fake (see `audioDirector.test.ts`); the WebAudio glue itself (`synth.ts`, `musicPlayer.ts`) plus real synthesis quality belong to the manual gameplay pass.
42	
43	**Known limitation:** headless Chromium cannot engage pointer lock (`requestPointerLock` never resolves). `acquirePointerLock` in `e2e/helpers.ts` tries the real thing, then falls back to forcing the input controller's lock flag — so key/mouse → engine wiring is still tested end to end, but real lock acquisition and look-around feel remain in the manual gameplay pass, along with visual quality. That manual pass is now only required for changes to pointer-lock handling or visual appearance.
44	
45	## The worldgen hash policy
46	
47	Saves store only the world seed plus block-change deltas, so `generateWorld` must produce byte-identical output for a given seed forever. The digests in `generation.test.ts` enforce that.
48	
49	**If a hash test fails after your change, your change breaks every existing save. Fix the code, never the hash.**
50	
51	Re-baselining the digests is legitimate in exactly two cases:
52	
53	1. A **deliberate worldgen change** — flag it in the PR and CHANGELOG, and bump `SAVE_KEY` so old saves are discarded rather than corrupted.
54	2. A **Bun version bump** — the noise functions use `Math.sin`, whose exact results are engine-defined (ECMA-262 leaves transcendentals implementation-specific). CI pins `bun-version` in `.github/workflows/ci.yml` for this reason; bump both together and verify the digests merely shifted rather than the generation logic changing.
55	
56	To regenerate digests: temporarily log the computed hash in the failing test, run `bun test`, and paste the new values in the same commit as the cause.
57	
58	The same caveat means the hash tests prove _refactor purity on the pinned engine_, not cross-browser save portability — a player switching browsers may theoretically regenerate slightly different terrain for the same seed. That limitation predates the test suite; it is now at least documented.
59	
60	## Writing new tests
61	
62	- Pure logic (inventory, spawn rules, a new system): unit-test the module directly.
63	- Gameplay behavior: boot a small engine — `new GameEngine({ seed, rng, worldSize: { x: 64, y: 150, z: 64 } })` — and step it with synthetic `FrameInput`. Inject a seeded `rng` (see `mulberry32` in the engine tests) for determinism.
64	- The world boots at dawn with hostiles nearby; use the `calmDaytime` pattern from the engine tests when testing unrelated mechanics.
65	- A ray origin exactly on a voxel boundary may target a diagonal neighbor (DDA ambiguity) — center positions in cells when aiming at specific blocks.
66	
```

1	# Tuning & Balance
2	
3	Every gameplay tunable lives in one place: [`lib/game/config.ts`](../lib/game/config.ts).
4	Each constant there carries a one-line comment; this guide is the companion that
5	groups them by **what they affect**, names the **system that reads each**, and
6	flags the **balance trade-offs** and the few that are **save-sensitive**.
7	
8	To rebalance, edit `config.ts` — not the systems. Item-level numbers (a pickaxe's
9	power, a sword's damage, a food's hunger) are **not** here; they live with the
10	content in [`items.ts`](../lib/game/items.ts) (see the [reference](reference.md)).
11	Worldgen constants live in the frozen `GEN` object in
12	[`generation.ts`](../lib/world/generation.ts) and are pinned by hash tests — see
13	[testing.md](testing.md).
14	
15	## Player feel — physics & movement
16	
17	`GRAVITY`, `JUMP_VELOCITY`, `WALK_SPEED`, `SPRINT_SPEED`, `CROUCH_SPEED`,
18	`PLAYER_HEIGHT`, `PLAYER_HALF_WIDTH`, `EYE_HEIGHT`, `WORLD_BORDER_PADDING`.
19	
20	Read by `systems/playerMotion.ts`. These set the moment-to-moment feel.
21	`JUMP_VELOCITY` vs `GRAVITY` together fix jump height (currently a ~1-block hop);
22	raise gravity for a snappier, heavier fall. `SPRINT_SPEED` is deliberately far
23	above `WALK_SPEED` so sprinting feels like a meaningful choice (and it's what burns
24	hunger fastest). `PLAYER_HALF_WIDTH`/`PLAYER_HEIGHT` are also the collision box, so
25	changing them affects which gaps the player fits through.
26	
27	## Survival pressure — the main difficulty dial
28	
29	`MAX_HEARTS`, `MAX_HUNGER`, `RESPAWN_SECONDS`, `HEALTH_REGEN_INTERVAL_SECONDS`,
30	`REGEN_MIN_HUNGER`, `SPRINT_MIN_HUNGER`, `SPRINT_BLOCKS_PER_HUNGER`,
31	`WALK_BLOCKS_PER_HUNGER`, `JUMPS_PER_HUNGER`.
32	
33	Read by `systems/playerStats.ts` (drain + regen) and `systems/playerLife.ts`
34	(respawn). This group is where "how hard is it to stay alive" is set. The
35	`*_PER_HUNGER` budgets control how fast activity drains food — lower numbers = more
36	eating. The two gates are the key feedback loop: you must stay above
37	`REGEN_MIN_HUNGER` (12) to heal and above `SPRINT_MIN_HUNGER` (6) to sprint, so
38	hunger pressure indirectly throttles both combat recovery and escape speed. Keep
39	`REGEN_MIN_HUNGER > SPRINT_MIN_HUNGER` so there's a "can run but can't heal" band.
40	
41	## Danger — day-night & the mob director
42	
43	`DAY_CYCLE_SECONDS`, `HOSTILE_SPAWN_BELOW_DAYLIGHT`, `SPIDER_AGGRO_BELOW_DAYLIGHT`,
44	`HOSTILE_BURN_ABOVE_DAYLIGHT`, `HOSTILE_SPAWN_INTERVAL_SECONDS`, `HOSTILE_CAP`.
45	
46	Read by `systems/dayNight.ts`, `systems/spawnDirector.ts`, and `systems/mobAI.ts`.
47	`DAY_CYCLE_SECONDS` (240) sets the whole rhythm — a shorter day means more frequent
48	nights and more pressure. The three daylight thresholds are a **documented engine
49	invariant** (daylight ranges 0.04–1.0; see architecture.md): hostiles spawn below
50	0.28, spiders turn hostile below 0.42, and exposed hostiles burn above 0.72. Keep
51	them ordered `spawn ≤ spider_aggro` and `burn` well above both, or mobs will spawn
52	into instant sunlight. `HOSTILE_CAP` × `HOSTILE_SPAWN_INTERVAL_SECONDS` bounds how
53	crowded a night gets. Tests aim daylight explicitly (a `calmDaytime` helper) to
54	avoid first-night aggro.
55	
56	## Progression — mining & combat reach
57	
58	`MINE_REACH`, `MINING_RATE`, `BARE_HAND_MINE_POWER`, `FIST_DAMAGE`, `ATTACK_REACH`,
59	`ATTACK_AIM_DOT`.
60	
61	Read by `systems/mining.ts` and `systems/combat.ts`. `MINING_RATE` × a tool's
62	`minePower` (from `items.ts`) ÷ block hardness = break time, so this constant scales
63	_all_ mining globally while item tiers scale it per-tool. Note the deliberate
64	asymmetry: `MINE_REACH` (7) is longer than `ATTACK_REACH` (4.5) — you can dig
65	farther than you can punch. `ATTACK_AIM_DOT` (0.89) is how precisely the crosshair
66	must point at a mob to hit it — lower is more forgiving. The per-ore **tool-tier
67	gate** itself lives in `systems/mining.ts` (`canMineBlock`), not config.
68	
69	## Farming & breeding pace
70	
71	Farming: `RANDOM_TICK_INTERVAL_SECONDS`, `RANDOM_TICK_SAMPLES`, `RANDOM_TICK_RADIUS`,
72	`CROP_GROWTH_CHANCE`, `GRASS_SEED_DROP_CHANCE`.
73	Breeding: `BREED_FED_WINDOW_SECONDS`, `BREED_PARTNER_RADIUS`,
74	`BREED_CHECK_INTERVAL_SECONDS`, `BABY_GROW_SECONDS`, `BABY_SCALE`, `PASSIVE_CAP`.
75	
76	Read by `systems/randomTicks.ts` and `systems/breeding.ts`. The random-tick group
77	controls crop growth statistically: each interval samples `RANDOM_TICK_SAMPLES`
78	columns within `RANDOM_TICK_RADIUS` of the player and advances eligible crops with
79	`CROP_GROWTH_CHANCE` probability — together ≈ 50 s/stage (~2.5 min to mature). More
80	samples or a larger radius means crops far from the player keep growing, at more
81	per-tick work. Breeding is bounded by `PASSIVE_CAP` (24) plus the crop cost of
82	feeding, so animals can't explode in number.
83	
84	## Beds & sleep
85	
86	`SLEEP_ALLOWED_BELOW_DAYLIGHT`, `SLEEP_HOSTILE_RADIUS`, `SLEEP_FADE_SECONDS`,
87	`WAKE_DAY_PHASE`.
88	
89	Read by the sleep path in `systems/interact.ts` / `GameEngine`. Sleeping is gated
90	to night (matching `HOSTILE_SPAWN_BELOW_DAYLIGHT`) and refused if a hostile is
91	within `SLEEP_HOSTILE_RADIUS`. `WAKE_DAY_PHASE` is where the day clock lands on
92	waking (a fresh dawn).
93	
94	## Inventory
95	
96	`HOTBAR_SLOTS`, `INVENTORY_SLOTS`, `MAX_STACK_SIZE`. **Save-sensitive** — see below.
97	
98	## Persistence & rendering
99	
100	`AUTOSAVE_INTERVAL_MS`, `SAVE_KEY`, `STUCK_RESET_SECONDS`, `RENDER_RADIUS`,
101	`RENDER_GRID`, `THIRD_PERSON_DISTANCE`, `THIRD_PERSON_MARGIN`.
102	
103	`RENDER_RADIUS` is the biggest **performance** lever: the renderer meshes one region
104	of this radius around the player, so larger values draw more terrain at higher cost;
105	`RENDER_GRID` is how far the player moves before that mesh rebuilds (smaller = more
106	frequent rebuilds, fresher view). `STUCK_RESET_SECONDS` is how long an overlap is
107	tolerated before the auto-unstuck teleport fires.
108	
109	## Save- and worldgen-sensitive tunables
110	
111	Change these only with care:
112	
113	- **`SAVE_KEY`** (`"minecraft_save_v5"`) is the localStorage key name. Note it's
114	  versioned independently of the save **schema** (currently v3, per
115	  [save-format.md](save-format.md)) — renaming `SAVE_KEY` orphans every existing
116	  player's save. Don't bump it to express a schema change; add a migration instead.
117	- **`INVENTORY_SLOTS` / `HOTBAR_SLOTS` / `MAX_STACK_SIZE`** affect the saved
118	  inventory layout. `save.ts` already migrates the v1 (40-slot) → v2 (36-slot)
119	  change; altering these again needs a matching migration or old saves break.
120	- **Daylight thresholds and `WAKE_DAY_PHASE`** shape the persisted `dayClock`'s
121	  meaning but are not save-format-breaking on their own.
122	- **Worldgen is not tuned here.** Terrain/ore/structure constants live in the
123	  frozen `GEN` object in `generation.ts` and are a byte-identical save contract
124	  pinned by hash tests — changing them requires the re-baseline policy in
125	  [testing.md](testing.md).
126	
```

Here's my honest assessment — yes, there are gaps. The code tests I added (particle pool, star positions, weather system) cover the pure logic well, but I missed two docs that the repo explicitly keeps in sync, plus a couple of small additions:

**Real gaps (should fix):**
1. **`docs/tuning.md`** — I added `WEATHER_CYCLE_SECONDS`/`WEATHER_RAIN_FRACTION` to `config.ts`, but this guide documents *every* tunable and doesn't mention them.
2. **`docs/testing.md`** — its coverage map lists tests per file; my 3 new test files aren't in it, and the WebAudio-glue note should include `rainLoop.ts`.
3. **`docs/manual.md`** — the player guide has a "Day, night, and danger" section; weather/stars deserve a short player-facing note.

**Small test win:** `hexToRgb` (used for mob-death particle color) is pure and untestable-free — worth a tiny unit test.

**Conscious skip:** an e2e that *forces* rain/night rendering — `tickWeather` overwrites forced state each frame, so it'd need brittle runtime window-scanning, and the precip path reuses the already-proven `THREE.Points` pattern while weather logic is fully unit-tested. Low ROI.

> DEVELOPER

do we need to add this to AGENTS.md as a rule ?

> AGENT

Honest answer: there's already a rule for it — it's just too vague to have caught my miss. AGENTS.md's Development Workflow has:

> **Update docs in the same change.** When behavior or a documented concept changes, update AGENTS.md / `docs/` and add a `CHANGELOG.md` entry. Flag any save-format or worldgen impact.

That generic "update `docs/`" is *why* I should have updated tuning.md and testing.md — but because it doesn't name the companion docs that track specific artifacts, it's easy to forget the ones that aren't obviously "behavior." So I wouldn't add a brand-new rule; I'd **tighten the existing one** to map each kind of change to the doc that tracks it. That directly prevents the exact miss that happened.

Proposed replacement for that bullet:

> **Update docs in the same change**, and keep the companion docs in sync — they track specific artifacts and are the easiest to forget: a new/changed `config.ts` tunable → `docs/tuning.md`; a new test file or system → the `docs/testing.md` coverage map; new player-facing behavior → `docs/manual.md` (and `docs/reference.md` for gameplay tables); new blocks/items/recipes/mobs → `docs/reference.md`. Add a `CHANGELOG.md` entry and flag any save-format or worldgen impact.

Two things worth your call:

1. **Worth doing?** I think yes — it's small, non-redundant, and turns a rule I demonstrably under-followed into a checklist. If you'd rather keep AGENTS.md lean, the alternative is to leave it (the existing rule technically covers it).
2. **Where should it land?** It's really a process change, separate from the juice/atmosphere feature. I'd lean toward **not** bundling it into PR #15 — either a tiny standalone commit/PR, or just commit it locally on `main` later. Putting it on the feature branch muddies that PR's scope.

Want me to apply the tightened wording? And if so, on a separate branch or on the current one?

> DEVELOPER

I think docs in docs/ folder is better than list the file names, because the file names may be renamed or added new? what do you think? and what about tests?

> AGENT

I agree — listing filenames in the rule is brittle and duplicates the **Docs** section that already maps each file to what it covers. Two places to keep in sync, and it rots the moment a doc is renamed or added. Better to state the *principle* and point at the Docs section as the single source of truth.

On tests: notice the test **coverage map is itself a doc** (`docs/testing.md`), so "keep `docs/` in sync" already covers updating it — no separate doc rule needed. The genuinely new thing worth stating is the discipline of *writing* tests for new logic, and `docs/testing.md` already documents the conventions (its "Writing new tests" section). So I'd fold both into one filename-free bullet.

Proposed replacement for the existing bullet:

> **Update docs and tests in the same change.** Keep `docs/` in sync with whatever you change — the **Docs** section below says what each file tracks, so update the pages covering what you touched (a tunable, a system, a mechanic, content, the save format), not only the obviously behavioral ones. Likewise, cover new logic with tests and keep the test coverage map current. Add a `CHANGELOG.md` entry, and flag any save-format or worldgen impact.

Why I think this is the right shape:
- **No hardcoded filenames** → nothing to rot; the Docs section stays the only place that enumerates files.
- **Principle, not checklist** → "update the pages covering what you touched" generalizes to docs that don't exist yet.
- **Tests folded in** the same way → "cover new logic + keep the coverage map current," deferring the *how* to `docs/testing.md`.

Two calls for you:
1. Good with this wording (or want it terser / split docs vs tests into two bullets)?
2. Placement — I'd keep it **off PR #15** (it's a process change, not part of the feature). A tiny standalone commit on `main` is cleanest. 

Say the word and I'll apply it — local commit only, no push.

29	- **Commit in focused slices.** One commit per logical slice (e.g. split a dead-code removal from the feature that replaces it). Keep `bun run lint` green at each commit so the branch stays bisectable. Use Conventional Commit messages; no `Co-Authored-By` trailers.
30	- **Explain the why in the commit body.** The subject says what changed; the body (separated by a blank line) explains why — the problem or motivation, and any non-obvious decision or trade-off. A body is required for anything beyond trivial edits (typos, formatting); `git log` should make sense without opening the PR.
31	- **Update docs in the same change.** When behavior or a documented concept changes, update AGENTS.md / `docs/` and add a `CHANGELOG.md` entry. Flag any save-format or worldgen impact.
32	- **Verify before a PR:** `bun run lint`, `bun run typecheck`, `bun run format:check`, `bun test`, and `bun run build` green (the same list CI's `verify` job runs); `bun run test:e2e` for changes touching the renderer, input, or React shell. > AGENT

Here's where PR #15 stands.

**CI:** `verify` ✅ · CodeRabbit ✅ (review done) · `e2e` ⏳ pending (just re-triggered by the push; it passed locally for every slice and the latest commit only added a test + docs, so it should stay green).

**CodeRabbit left 3 comments — all "Minor / quick win," no Major.** My honest assessment: none are bugs at the current call sites (those paths are already guarded), but all three are cheap robustness improvements and one is a genuine edge-case fix:

1. **`GameEngine.ts:115` — seed weather from the restored `dayClock` on load.** Valid edge case. The constructor sets `weather: clear` and it's only recomputed in `step()`. In practice the rAF loop always steps before rendering, so the first *visible* frame is fine — but the loaded-but-not-yet-stepped state (and a paused-on-load snapshot) is momentarily inconsistent. Fix is a one-liner: call `tickWeather(this.state)` after restoring the save. **Worth doing** — correct by construction.

2. **`particlePool.ts:64` — fail-fast on invalid `cap`.** Pure defensiveness; `cap` is only ever `512` or a positive test value. Harmless one-liner, low real value.

3. **`particlePool.ts:116` — guard `dt` against `NaN`.** `Math.max(0, Math.min(NaN, 0.1))` is `NaN`. Can't happen today (the renderer derives `dtMs` with a `NaN` guard already), but it hardens the pure utility. Cheap.

My recommendation: apply all three — #1 because it's a real (if minor) correctness nit, #2/#3 because they're trivial one-liners that harden reusable pure utilities and clear the review cleanly. I'd add a small `particlePool` test for the `NaN`/`cap` guards too (keeps the coverage-map honest, per the rule we just tightened).

Want me to apply these and commit to the branch (local, no push, as before)? Or skip the defensive ones and do just #1?

> AGENT

Committed `e6f4538` on `feat/juice-atmosphere` — **local only, not pushed** (branch is 1 ahead of origin).

## Review summary

CodeRabbit's 3 comments on PR #15 (all Minor / quick-win) are addressed:

1. **Weather on load** — `GameEngine` now calls `tickWeather` after restoring the save, so a loaded game's first frame/snapshot reflects the deterministic weather instead of momentarily showing clear.
2. **`createParticlePool` cap guard** — fail-fast `RangeError` on a non-positive/non-integer cap.
3. **`updateParticlePool` NaN guard** — a `NaN` dt is treated as 0, so it can't corrupt the buffers.

Added two `particlePool` tests covering the cap and NaN guards (keeping the coverage honest, per the rule we just tightened).

**Verification:** lint, typecheck, format, **330 tests** all green. No renderer/shell change, so the e2e gate doesn't apply.

## CI state on PR #15 (before this commit)
`verify` ✅ · CodeRabbit ✅ · `e2e` was pending when I checked — it should be green (the prior commit only added a test + docs).

When you're ready, pushing `e6f4538` will update the PR and re-run CI. Want me to push, or check whether that pending e2e run finished first?
